# Self-hostable review systems with a first-class revision noun

Generated 2026-08-06 by five web-enabled survey agents plus twelve adversarial
verification passes (17 agents, ~2.1M tokens). Question asked: is there an
open-source, self-hostable forge or review system that already models a change
with immutable base-naming revisions, so JetBridge can adopt it instead of
inventing it?

Where a verification refuted or downgraded a survey claim, the verification wins;
the synthesis marks those inline with a warning glyph.

---

# Is there an open-source forge that models agent code review correctly?

## 1. THE DIRECT ANSWER

Yes — it is solved, it has been solved for more than fifteen years, and GitHub is the outlier rather than the norm. The system that models exactly what you described is **Gerrit Code Review**, which has had a first-class change object since its earliest releases (built for Android, ~2008): a `Change-Id` trailer gives a change an identity independent of any commit, and every upload creates a new **patch set** that is a distinct, individually addressable, never-rewritten git ref at `refs/changes/<last two digits>/<change number>/<patch set number>` — documented verbatim as "a static reference" (https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/access-control.html#refs_changes). Two other self-hostable systems solved it independently: **Review Board** (immutable, ordered, numbered diff revisions per review request, since ~2007) and **Radicle/heartwood** (a `Revision` struct that literally carries `base: git::Oid` alongside `oid`, which is your "a snapshot has a HEAD but a change has a BASE" complaint expressed as a field). What does *not* exist anywhere is a review system built for machine **authors** — every one of these was built for humans and every open-source "AI code review" project on the market is an agent-as-*reviewer* bot bolted onto someone else's PR. You are not late to a solved problem; you are early to a different one, and the right move is to adopt an existing revision noun rather than invent one.

---

## 2. COMPARISON OF THE REALISTIC CANDIDATES

| | **Gerrit 3.14** | **Review Board 8.0 CE** | **Radicle 1.10 (heartwood)** | **GitLab CE 19.2** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Revision noun** | **A.** Change-Id + numeric change number, identity independent of commit. Patch sets are static git refs, individually fetchable. Base via `commit.parents[]` (unambiguous) and `parents_data`/`ParentInfo` incl. `is_merged_in_target_branch` (confirmed). *One change = one commit* — the real limit. | **A−.** Review request id independent of commit; `/diffs/{revision}/` immutable (GET/PUT only, PUT explicitly cannot modify contents, no DELETE). **But `base_commit_id` is optional, client-supplied, unvalidated** — canonical example shows `null`; `branch` is free-form text. | **A+.** `Revision { id (content-addressed COB op OID), base: Oid, oid: Oid, resolves }`. Code pointer immutable in the reducer. Best model surveyed. *Caveat:* revisions are **editable and redactable** by their author — "sealed" needs qualification. | **C+.** `merge_request_diffs` per **push**, with `base_commit_sha`/`start_commit_sha`/`head_commit_sha` + `patch_id_sha`. But change identity is still a mutable branch, and **diff files are deleted at merge** (`DeleteNonLatestDiffsService` in `PostMergeService`), plus a hard **1000-version cap**. |
| **Machine-reviewer API** | **B−.** `POST .../review` with threaded `in_reply_to`, `unresolved`, label votes — solid and core. **Robot comments REMOVED in 3.13** (verified: zero occurrences of "robot" in stable-3.14 REST docs). `fix_suggestions` is **experiment-gated, off by default**. **No server-side REST to post a check result**; checks plugin deprecated with **no branch past stable-3.11** (EOL 2026-05-15). | **A.** Best in class. Private draft review → `diff-comments` (`filediff_id`+`first_line`+`num_lines`) → `PUT public=1` atomic publish. `ship_it` verdict. Replies are a separate resource with required `reply_to_id`. `issue_status` ∈ open/resolved/dropped/verifying-*. **Status Updates** link a check to a specific change *and* a review. All Free tier. | **C.** Action vocabulary is complete (`review`, `review.comment` with `location`, `review.comment.resolve`, `revision` with `resolves`) — but **the HTTP API is read-only by policy**; writes are local `rad cob update` with a JSONL file. Verdict serializes **lowercase** `"accept"`. **One review per actor per revision, silently ignored** on repeat. | **B.** Discussions API with full position hash, resolve, and Draft Notes `bulk_publish` with `reviewer_state` — genuinely good and Free. But **external status checks are Ultimate-only**; CE gets commit statuses keyed to a SHA that cannot block merge, and approvals are advisory. |
| **Push events** | **B+.** `webhooks` is a **core bundled submodule** (verified in `.gitmodules`), per-event filtering. `stream-events` over SSH. `events-log` (not bundled) for replay. **Retries are in-memory and bounded; `sslVerify` defaults false.** | **B.** Five events; `reply_published` is **distinct** from `review_published`, and payloads inline the comments. No status-update event. | **C−.** `rad node events` over the control socket is real (radicle-ci-broker consumes it in production) — but ref-granularity, `#[non_exhaustive]`, no replay guarantee, and **`rad cob` never announces**, so local writes emit nothing without `rad sync --announce`. | **B.** Mature webhooks incl. `approval`/`approved`/`unapproval`/`unapproved` actions. **No review-submitted event** (no review object); delivery best-effort with auto-disable. |
| **Anchoring across revisions** | **A.** The only published, open-source forward-porting algorithm in existence. `GET .../ported_comments`; the ported comment **is** the original ("not copies… simply shown in another place"; resolving the ported view resolves the original). Documented fallback: computed position → file-level comment → not ported at all. Resolved comments are deliberately not ported. **Docs warn callers must not rely on the exact logic and repeated calls may differ.** | **A−, by refusing to guess.** Anchor is `filediff_id` inside an immutable revision — it can never go stale. New revision shows a banner linking back. Continuity carried by `issue_status`, not by re-anchoring. For a machine this is arguably the better contract. | **B.** `CodeLocation { commit, path, old, new }` pinned to a commit OID (never stale), plus declarative `resolves` on the next revision. **No forwarding exists at all** — grep for port/outdated/reanchor returns nothing. | **B−.** `Gitlab::Diff::PositionTracer` **does exist** in MIT core (the common claim that it doesn't is wrong) — but it is entirely **undocumented**, with no specified fallback ladder. |
| **Ops cost (one k8s cluster, small team)** | **C.** Java 21 mandatory since 3.12, stateful single-primary JVM + large PVC, **full reindex** on the 3.12 upgrade, Jetty 9→12 in 3.14. No relational DB (NoteDb = git). `k8s-gerrit` operator exists but is **38 stars, one tag v0.1**. | **B−.** Django + DB + memcached + front nginx, official `beanbag/reviewboard` images, no Helm chart, and a **ReadWriteMany PVC** shared for static media. **Git needs local clone access, GitWeb/cgit, or their RB Gateway** (Go, MIT). | **A−.** Two Rust binaries, git storage + SQLite index. **But: no server-side web review UI** — `radicle-explorer` is read-only, so humans review via `rad` CLI or Radicle Desktop, each on their own node. | **D.** Documented baseline **8 vCPU / 16 GB** single-node, plus Postgres, Redis/Valkey, Gitaly, Sidekiq, Workhorse. |
| **Project health** | **A.** 3.14.2 (2026-07-13), ~6-month cadence, last-3-releases support policy, ~150 commits in five weeks. Note: **community-led more than "Google + community"** — recent commits skew NVIDIA/Qualcomm/independent over Google. | **A−.** Beanbag Inc., ~1 major/yr, 8.0 (2026-05-27), master pushed 2026-08-05 with live 8.x and 9.x branches. Caveat: **Review Bot 4.1 still documents RB 5.0–7.0 only**. | **B+.** 1.10.0 on 2026-08-05, 143 commits/16 contributors — but cadence is **bursty, not monthly** (3.2-month gap Sep 2025→Jan 2026), ~81% of commits from three people, and **Radicle 2 is announced** as the next master. VERSIONING.md: CLI commands are **not stable APIs**. | **A.** Monthly, MIT Expat core, vendor-backed. Not in question. |

**Dismissed, one line each:**
- **Forgejo / Gitea** — inherits GitHub's model wholesale (`pr.HeadCommitID = ...; UpdateRef(...)`); comment anchoring is a one-shot blame check setting a terminal `Invalidated` flag **that isn't even in the API schema**, so your bot can't detect its own stale comments; and Forgejo's March 2026 monthly report records a decision to *prohibit AI-generated work entirely*.
- **Phorge / Differential** — the model is genuinely good (Revision + per-diff staging tag) but **there is no Conduit path to publish an inline comment, no `replyTo` parameter anywhere in the API, and no `isDone` setter**; upstream closed the request as Wontfix in 2015. Bus factor ~1.
- **SourceHut** — I enumerated every mutation in the lists.sr.ht GraphQL schema: **there is no comment, reply, or review mutation**. Posting review text requires SMTP.
- **Tangled** — round-based PRs are conceptually exactly right and `sh.tangled.feed.comment` even carries writer-declared `pullRoundIdx` + `replyTo`, but there is **no structured file/line field, no verdict object, no resolved flag, and no base commit per round**. Alpha, breaking migrations most releases.
- **b4 / public-inbox** — `change-id` trailer is excellent prior art, but there is no server, no event push, no verdict type, and `b4 review` is self-labelled alpha with unstable on-disk formats.
- **Jujutsu** — no review object of any kind; steal the `change-id` commit header, not the tool.
- **Sapling / ReviewStack** — reconstructs versions **in the browser** from GitHub's timeline with an explicit rebase-correctness TODO; adds no revision noun and changes nothing about your bot problem. Last substantive commit 2026-04-19.
- **git-appraise** — the best schema in this whole survey (`parent`/`original`/`resolved`/`location{commit,path,range}`) and it's Go — but last pushed 2023-08-12. Read it; don't depend on it.
- **Patchwork** — comment endpoints are GET-only and the event stream is a polled `/events/` collection.
- **Atlassian Crucible** — closed, new sales ended 2025-05-13, support ends 2028-05-15.

---

## 3. TOP CANDIDATE IN DEPTH: GERRIT

Gerrit wins on your stated priority order because priority #1 is priority #1 and nothing else meets it as completely. Review Board beats it decisively on priority #2 and is the runner-up; I say why at the end of this section.

### What JetBridge would use it for
Gerrit becomes your **change store and human review UI**. The agent pushes to `refs/for/<branch>` with a `Change-Id` trailer; each iteration mints a new patch set; humans review in Gerrit; JetBridge consumes webhooks, posts findings and verdicts as a service user, and lands via the bundled `replication` plugin (mirroring `refs/heads/*` to GitHub, deliberately *not* `refs/changes/*`).

### Endpoint-by-endpoint replacement of what you were building

| Your piece | Gerrit replacement |
|---|---|
| Change record with stable id | The `Change-Id` trailer plus — **use this as the durable key** — the numeric change number. `Change-Id` is *not* globally unique; the canonical REST id is `project~branch~Change-Id`. |
| "Revision 3, based on commit X" | `refs/changes/NN/CCCC/3`. `RevisionInfo` gives you `ref`, a `fetch` map of ready-made checkout commands per protocol, `commit.parents[]`, and `parents_data` (`ParentInfo`, under the `PARENTS` option) telling you whether the parent is a branch tip or patch set N of another change — including `is_merged_in_target_branch`. |
| **Two sealed worktrees, 789 MiB, 99.98% overlap** | One `git fetch <url> refs/changes/NN/CCCC/P`. The base is the commit's parent; the ref is static. This is the single strongest argument for Gerrit and it deletes the whole sealing mechanism. |
| **45-column mirror table** | `GET /changes/{id}?o=CURRENT_REVISION&o=ALL_REVISIONS&o=PARENTS`, `GET /changes/{id}/comments`, `GET .../revisions/{rev}/ported_comments`. Review metadata lives in NoteDb (git) — there is no relational review DB to mirror. You keep a **cursor**, not a mirror. |
| Agent posts findings | `POST /changes/{id}/revisions/{rev}/review` with `ReviewInput{ comments: {path → CommentInput{ range, in_reply_to, unresolved }}, labels, message, tag, notify }`. Threading and per-thread resolution are first-class fields, not conventions. |
| Agent posts a verdict | A label vote (`Verified`, or a custom `Agent-Review` label) in the same call. |
| **"Agent's reply becomes a NEW review object → self-trigger"** | **Structurally impossible.** Gerrit has no mutable "review" entity to mint. A reply is a comment with `in_reply_to` pointing at a comment UUID. |
| Suppressing your own events | **Filter on the event `author` account, not on the tag.** ⚠️ Verification refuted the tempting answer here: `CommentAddedEvent` declares only `type`/`author`/`approvals`/`comment` — **`tag` is not in any webhook or stream-events payload.** Reading the tag requires a REST callback, i.e. the polling you're escaping. Give the agent a Service Users account and filter on it. |
| Bot identity | The **Service Users** group (core access control): never added to the attention set, replies only pull the owner in if they carry a negative vote, not suggested as reviewers, `SERVICE_USER` in `AccountInfo.tags`, granted `Stream Events` capability, BATCH thread pool, and its vote doesn't satisfy "require Code-Review from all human reviewers." (The separate `serviceuser` *plugin* is **not** bundled — verified against `.gitmodules`.) |
| **Comment anchors going stale** | `GET /changes/{id}/revisions/{rev}/ported_comments` and `/ported_drafts`. The ported comment *is* the original — resolving the ported view resolves the original, so there is no forwarding bookkeeping on your side. Verify the exact path on your instance; one doc extraction suggested a `?ported=true` form. |
| CI / check results | ❌ **Nothing.** See below. |
| Landing to GitHub | Bundled `replication` plugin, `push = refs/heads/*:refs/heads/*`, **omit the `+` force prefix** (the config docs call GitHub out by name). |
| Correlating runs/tickets | Push options: `%topic=`, `-o custom-keyed-value=k:v`. |

### What you would still have to build

1. **The Go client.** `andygrunwald/go-gerrit` (MIT, v1.1.1, 2025-12-05, effectively one maintainer, ~17 commits/yr mostly dependabot) is missing exactly the two things you need most: **no `ported_comments`/`ported_drafts` at all**, and **no `fix_suggestions` on `CommentInput`** — while still shipping dead `RobotComments` types removed server-side in 3.13. Budget ~1k lines of hand-rolled client. The REST surface makes this easy: JSON over HTTP basic auth, `/a/` prefix, strip the `)]}'` XSSI guard.
2. **Check-result storage, and any Checks tab.** The Checks API is a **frontend TypeScript plugin API** — your browser plugin implements `fetch()` and pulls results from *your* database; Gerrit persists nothing. The server-side `checks` plugin that had a REST API carries a deprecation notice ("The Gerrit team at Google has decided to discontinue work on the checks plugin") **and has no release branch past `stable-3.11`, which went EOL 2026-05-15.** In practice: express verdicts as label votes and findings as tagged comments, and only write a JS plugin if a Checks tab is worth shipping for.
3. **A durable event cursor.** Webhooks retry in-memory, bounded (`maxTries` default 5), with no delivery receipt, and **`sslVerify` defaults to `false` — set it true.** `stream-events` emits a `dropped-output` event and warns "any field may be missing." `events-log` (not bundled) gives replay but only over a `t1`/`t2` timestamp range, not a monotonic cursor.
4. **Machine-vs-human provenance as convention, not type.** Robot comments are gone; you get the `autogenerated:` tag (real, and a core constant — Gerrit tags its own messages `autogenerated:gerrit:merged` etc.) plus the `SERVICE_USER` account tag. Note the tag's UI effect is weaker than folklore: it hides an *older* message with the *same* tag on an *older* patch set; a single current CI comment is shown.
5. **The one-change-one-commit reconciliation.** A six-commit agent branch becomes six dependent changes (a relation chain), not one reviewable unit. This is the biggest modeling decision and I'd resolve it before anything else.
6. **Empirical verification of patch-set immutability.** "Static reference" is a naming convention plus an access-control boundary — it is *not* a documented immutability invariant. Your entire observation design would rest on it. Test it.

### Why not Review Board, given its better bot API
Review Board's machine-reviewer surface is the best of anything surveyed — atomic draft-then-publish, required `reply_to_id`, a real `issue_status` state machine, and Status Updates that link a check to both a specific change description and a review. If priority #2 were priority #1, it wins outright. It loses on three things: **`base_commit_id` is optional, client-supplied and unvalidated** (the canonical example shows `null`), so "each revision names its own base" is a promise you keep, not one the system enforces; **it needs the base reachable from the repo** (local clone with a per-minute cron, GitWeb/cgit, or RB Gateway), so an unpushed agent base doesn't work; and **it cannot merge or land anything** — `approved`/`approval_failure` are advisory booleans with no gating. Plus: no Go client, official or third-party.

---

## 4. THE THREE ADOPTION SHAPES

### (a) Gerrit as the review UI; land to GitHub via replication
The canonical pattern — GerritHub has run it commercially for years ("push to Gerrit and use your GitHub repository for pull and pull-requests only"), and the self-hosted equivalent needs only bundled plugins.

- **Wins:** humans get a review UI you don't build (and Gerrit's UI is genuinely good at the two things that matter here — patch-set-to-patch-set diffing and comment threads). GitHub stays the archive of record for everyone outside the loop. Zero UI work.
- **Costs:** two systems of record and one-way replication. Your humans must learn Gerrit's push model (`refs/for/`, commit-msg hook, `Change-Id`) — that is a real behavior change with real resistance. GitHub PRs become either absent or a lagging mirror; PR-based tooling downstream breaks. And replication carries a foot-gun the docs call out explicitly: omit the `+` force prefix or you can clobber remote branches.

### (b) Gerrit headless as the review backend; JetBridge UI on top
Better supported than it sounds. NoteDb means every review artifact is git; the REST surface is complete; there is no relational store to fight.

- **Wins:** one UI, yours, matching the workflow-run-first model you already shipped. You get the revision noun, the porting algorithm, and the comment schema without owning any of it. You can present a *change* as your own noun with N Gerrit changes underneath, papering over the one-commit mismatch.
- **Costs:** you build the diff viewer, which is the expensive part and the part nobody thanks you for. You inherit Gerrit's operational tax (Java 21, full reindex on upgrade, Jetty migrations, zero-downtime only under HA/multi-site) while forfeiting the UI value that justifies it. And `ported_comments` is explicitly non-deterministic per the REST docs — anything you cache from it must be a projection, never a source of truth.

### (c) Don't adopt; steal the data model
Concretely worth stealing, in descending order of value:

1. **Radicle's `Revision { id, base, oid, resolves }`** — and especially `resolves: BTreeSet<(EntryId, CommentId)>`. This is the principled answer to comment anchoring: don't forward anchors heuristically; pin every comment to an immutable commit OID and have the *next* revision **declare** which comments it addresses. That converts an unsolvable heuristic into a data field, and it is a better fit for an agent author than porting is, because your agent *knows* what it fixed.
2. **jj's `change-id` git commit header** (32-char reverse-hex, standardized with GitButler, `git.write-change-id-header`). Stable change identity in plain git, survives `--amend`, **stripped by rebase**, invisible to `git log`/`git show` (read with `git cat-file -p`). ~30 lines of Go, no VCS migration, no dependency.
3. **Gerrit's porting fallback ladder** *and* its observability: `ported_comments/as_range_comments`, `/as_file_level`, `/as_patchset_level`. Ship the metrics with the algorithm.
4. **git-appraise's comment JSON schema** — `{ author, parent, original, resolved, description, location{commit, path, range{startLine, startColumn, endLine, endColumn}}, v }` — about 150 lines of schema that solves threading, resolution, inline anchoring and CI results, with an explicit `v` field. It also already splits machine output (`refs/notes/devtools/analyses`) from human review (`refs/notes/devtools/discuss`), which is the distinction GitHub collapses.
5. **ghstack's `base`/`head`/`orig` ref triple** — if you stay on GitHub, this is the cheapest way to make each revision's base an addressable, GC-pinned ref.

- **Wins:** you get exactly the semantics you want, including multi-commit changes as one reviewable unit — which no adoptable system gives you. No second forge, no second on-call, no migration of human habits.
- **Costs:** diff viewer, auth model, notifications, and a UI. Plus you own the anchoring correctness forever, in a domain where the industry has produced exactly one published algorithm in twenty years.

---

## 5. THE HONEST CASE AGAINST ADOPTING ANYTHING

**A second forge is a second everything.** Second identity system, second backup and restore path you have actually tested, second upgrade treadmill, second set of 3am pages. Gerrit's specific tax is well documented and non-trivial: Java 21 became mandatory in 3.12, that upgrade required a full reindex (Lucene 10.1.0), 3.14 moved the embedded server from Jetty 9 to Jetty 12, and zero-downtime upgrades are only supported under the high-availability or multi-site plugins. Your Kubernetes on-ramp, `k8s-gerrit`, is a 38-star repo with a single `v0.1` tag. For a team your size that is a permanent part-time job in exchange for a data model.

**Three of your five measured problems don't actually require leaving GitHub.** The self-trigger bug is a `sender.login` filter plus a dedicated bot identity — that's the same fix you'd apply on Gerrit anyway, since the tag isn't in the event payload. The 789 MiB double-worktree seal is fixable by recording the `(base, head)` SHA pair per observation and pinning both with your own refs, ghstack-style, rather than sealing trees. The 45-column mirror shrinks a lot once you key on `(pr number, base sha, head sha)` and dereference on demand instead of mirroring. Only two problems genuinely require a different system: a true commit-independent change identity, and principled anchor forwarding.

**Humans have to move, and agents don't care.** The review UI is a human tool. Your agent is indifferent to whether it POSTs to Gerrit or GitHub. If your reviewers won't adopt `refs/for/`, `Change-Id`, and one-change-per-commit, the world's best data model buys you nothing.

**What must be true for "steal the model" to beat "run the software":**

1. **You already own a UI humans use daily.** You do — the workflow-run-first UI is already the surface your team looks at. This condition is met, and it is the strongest argument for (c).
2. **Agent-authored diffs are small and scoped enough that a competent-not-great diff viewer suffices.** If reviews are node-sized rather than release-sized, the viewer is weeks, not quarters.
3. **You need a multi-commit change as one reviewable unit.** ⚠️ **This is the decisive one.** Gerrit's change *is* one commit. A typical agent run produces a multi-commit branch, and under Gerrit that becomes N dependent changes with N review conversations. If your mental model is "a change is a branch," adoption loses on your priority-#1 requirement itself, and no amount of API quality compensates. Radicle is the only surveyed system where a revision head is a branch head — and Radicle has no HTTP write API and no server-side review UI.
4. **You can live without ported comments** by adopting Radicle's declarative `resolves` instead. Given that your agent *knows* which findings it addressed in each revision, declaration is strictly more accurate than porting — and it removes the one capability Gerrit uniquely provides.

If (3) is true — and for an agentic CI platform it almost certainly is — the honest recommendation flips to (c), with Gerrit's schema and porting ladder as the spec you implement against rather than the software you run.

---

## 6. WHAT GENUINELY DOES NOT EXIST

**No system has all four of: base-naming immutable revisions + a typed HTTP API for machine verdicts and inline threads + push events + a Go client.** Gerrit has three (Go client is thin and missing the two endpoints you need most). Review Board has three (no Go client at all). Radicle has one and a half (read-only HTTP, no Go client). Tangled has three but no revision base and no verdict object. Nothing has four.

**No review system built for machine authors exists.** Searching the obvious terms returns only agent-as-reviewer products — PR-Agent/Qodo, CodeRabbit, Gito, kodus, auditlm, GerritForge's ai-review-agent-provider. Every one posts LLM commentary onto someone else's change object. None introduces a change model. The category is empty, and the asymmetry is itself the finding. Worse: **Gerrit deleted its one machine-authorship primitive.** Robot comments (`robot_id`, `robot_run_id`, dedicated UI treatment) were disabled by default in 3.12 and removed in 3.13 — verified two ways, including zero occurrences of "robot" in the stable-3.14 REST documentation and a 404 on `RobotComment.java` in `stable-3.13`.

**No major forge shipped anything for agent authors in 2025–2026.** Gerrit 3.14's "Review Agent" is an AI chat sidebar for humans, and its reference provider is BSL 1.1 (source-available, not open source). GitLab's Duo Agent Platform reached GA — it does author MRs, so the flat claim that GitLab shipped nothing is wrong — but it is Premium/Ultimate, proprietary, and adds **no change object**. Forgejo moved the other way and prohibited AI-generated contributions outright (March 2026 monthly report). GitHub's own published guidance on reviewing agent PRs is pure workflow advice on existing PR infrastructure — no new data model, no new API.

**Exactly one published open-source comment-anchor-forwarding algorithm exists: Gerrit's.** `CommentPorter.java` (458 lines) driving `GitPositionTransformer` with `BestPositionOnConflict.INSTANCE` over `DiffMappings`/`FileMapping`, with a documented fallback ladder and shipped counters. GitLab has an implementation (`Gitlab::Diff::PositionTracer`) but publishes no description or fallback contract. Phorge's `PhabricatorInlineCommentAdjustmentEngine` ports at render time only, marks results ephemeral, and never exposes the ported position through Conduit. Everyone else just marks comments outdated. **I found no research paper on the problem.**

**No server-side API for posting check results in Gerrit.** The Checks API is a browser plugin API; results live in your database and the browser pulls them per page load; the plugin that had a REST API is deprecated with no branch for any supported Gerrit version. This is the sharpest single regression in the whole survey.

**No durable, replayable event stream anywhere.** Gerrit webhooks retry in-memory and bounded. Radicle's node events are ref-granularity with no documented replay. Review Board and GitLab make no ordering guarantees. **You will keep a cursor regardless of what you adopt** — which means requirement #3 as stated ("without maintaining a mirrored cursor") is not fully satisfiable by any surveyed system. What you can eliminate is the *mirror*; the cursor stays.

**Nobody has built provenance at the change layer for machine authorship.** Entire (Thomas Dohmke, ex-GitHub CEO, $60M seed at $300M, out of stealth 2026-02-10) is premised on precisely your diagnosis — "Pull requests still assume a human wrote every line and can explain every decision." Six months in, the only public artifact is an MIT Go CLI that stores agent sessions on a hidden `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch. Provenance capture, not a change model. Read that as evidence the revision layer is hard and unbuilt, not that you missed an existing answer.

---

Support policy: \"the last 3 releases on a best effort basis\", EOL happens implicitly when a new major ships (https://www.gerritcodereview.com/support.html). Upstream commit activity is daily \u2014 GerritCodeReview/gerrit last pushed 2026-08-07, 1213 stars, only 5 open issues (issue tracking lives elsewhere). Governance: an Engineering Steering Committee; Google holds appointed seats and hosts the annual user summit (Nov 9-10 2026, Sunnyvale); GerritForge holds 5 maintainer seats and Luca Milanesio was re-elected to the ESC in 2026. Official Docker images are published: gerritcodereview/gerrit:3.14.2 (~432 MB), updated 2026-07-15.",
          "revision_noun": "YES \u2014 this is Gerrit's core competence and it is exactly the noun they are missing. A **Change** has a stable identity independent of any commit: the Change-Id trailer (`I` + 40 hex, injected by the commit-msg hook), and Gerrit resolves a change by the triple (Change-Id, project, branch), so \"Gerrit can automatically associate a new version of a change back to its original review, even across cherry-picks and rebases\" (https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/user-changeid.html). Each upload creates a new **patch set** \u2014 an ordered, numbered, individually addressable git ref: `refs/changes/<last two digits of change number>/<change number>/<patch set number>`, documented as \"a static reference\" (https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/access-control.html#refs_changes). A patch set names its own base directly: RevisionInfo carries `commit.parents[]` always, and `parents_data` (a list of ParentInfo) when the `PARENTS` query option is set. ParentInfo fields: `branch_name`, `commit_id`, `is_merged_in_target_branch`, `change_id`, `change_number`, `patch_set_number`, `change_status` \u2014 i.e. it tells you whether the base is a branch tip or patch set N of another change. RevisionInfo also carries a per-revision `branch` (can differ across patch sets after a Move Change) and, crucially, `kind`: `REWORK`, `TRIVIAL_REBASE`, `TRIVIAL_REBASE_WITH_MESSAGE_UPDATE`, `MERGE_FIRST_PARENT_UPDATE`, `NO_CODE_CHANGE`, `NO_CHANGE` \u2014 Gerrit classifies *what kind of delta* each new patch set is relative to the last. `POST /changes/{id}/rebase` with RebaseInput (optional `base`) creates a NEW patch set; the old one is untouched and remains fetchable.",
          "machine_reviewer_api": "MIXED \u2014 and this is the one place where the answer has REGRESSED, hard. WHAT WAS DELETED: robot comments are gone. Gerrit 3.12 breaking changes: \"Robot comments are disabled by default. Support for robot comments will be removed with the next release. Checks should be used instead.\" Gerrit 3.13: \"Robot comments are not supported anymore and users have to move to the Checks API\" (changes 474301, 474303). I verified against master `Documentation/rest-api-changes.txt`: **zero occurrences of the string \"robot\"**. `robot_comments`, `RobotCommentInput`, `/revisions/{id}/robot-comments` no longer exist. Do not design against them. WHAT ACTUALLY WORKS (and is sufficient): a service-user bot posts *ordinary* comments and votes via `POST /changes/{change-id}/revisions/{revision-id}/review` with a ReviewInput carrying: `comments` (map of file path -> CommentInput), `labels` (map label name -> vote value, e.g. {\"Code-Review\": -1}), `message`, `tag`, `notify`, `on_behalf_of`, `reviewers`, `ready`/`work_in_progress`, `add_to_attention_set`/`remove_from_attention_set`, `ignore_automatic_attention_set_rules`, `omit_duplicate_comments`. CommentInput/CommentInfo carry first-class `in_reply_to` (comment UUID \u2014 real threading), `unresolved` (bool), `side`/`parent`, `range`, and \u2014 importantly \u2014 `fix_suggestions`, a list of FixSuggestionInfo {`description`, `replacements`: [FixReplacementInfo {`path`, `range`, `replacement`}]}. Fix suggestions SURVIVED the robot-comment removal and now live on ordinary comments. Apply/preview: `POST /changes/{id}/revisions/{rev}/fixes/{fix-id}/apply` and `GET .../fixes/{fix-id}/preview`. Resolving threads: set `unresolved` on a reply; \"The state of resolution of a comment thread is stored in the last comment in that thread chronologically.\" CI/CHECK RESULTS: there is NO supported server-side REST API to post a check result. The Checks API (https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/pg-plugin-checks-api.html) is a **frontend TypeScript/JavaScript plugin API** \u2014 `plugin.checks()`, `checksApi.register(provider, config)`, `provider.fetch() -> Promise<FetchResponse>` of CheckRun/CheckResult, plus `announceUpdate()` and `updateResult()`. Results live in YOUR database; the browser pulls them on page load. Gerrit stores nothing. The server-side `checks` plugin that did store them carries a DEPRECATION NOTICE: \"The Gerrit team at Google has decided to discontinue work on the checks plugin. The recommended solution is [Checks UI] which surfaces results from an external CI/analysis system\" (https://gerrit.googlesource.com/plugins/checks/+doc/master/README.md). It still receives build-maintenance commits (last 2026-07-31) but is not a product direction. PRACTICAL VERDICT: express machine verdicts as label votes (Verified, or a custom label), findings as ordinary threaded comments with fix_suggestions, and only build a Checks UI plugin if you want a Checks tab. Auth is HTTP basic against an auth token, with the endpoint prefixed `/a/` (https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/rest-api.html#authentication).",
          "push_events": "YES, three tiers, none of them durable by default. (1) CORE, no plugin: `ssh <host> gerrit stream-events` \u2014 a live JSON stream, one event per line, 18 event types: patchset-created, comment-added, change-merged, change-abandoned, change-restored, change-deleted, reviewer-added, reviewer-deleted, vote-deleted, topic-changed, hashtags-changed, wip-state-changed, private-state-changed, ref-updated, batch-ref-updated, project-created, project-head-updated, dropped-output (https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/cmd-stream-events.html). Requires the `Stream Events` global capability, which the Service Users group has by default. Delivery: fire-and-forget, no ack, no replay, no persistence. Gerrit emits a `dropped-output` event \"to notify a client that events have been dropped\", and the docs warn \"any field may be missing in the JSON messages\". (2) `webhooks` \u2014 a BUNDLED core plugin (it is a git submodule at `plugins/webhooks` in the gerrit tree, so it ships in the release war; verified via .gitmodules). Global config in gerrit.config, per-project config in `webhooks.config` on `refs/meta/config`, so per-repo remotes. Per-remote: `url`, repeated `event` filters, `maxTries` (default 5, 0 disables), `retryInterval` (default 1000ms), `connectionTimeout`/`socketTimeout` (default 5000ms), `sslVerify` (**default false** \u2014 turn it on), `threadPoolSize` (default 2); global caps `allowedEvent`, `allowedUrlPattern`, `maxAllowedTries` etc. Retries are in-memory and bounded: \"After this number of failed tries, an error is logged.\" No durable queue, no delivery receipt \u2014 treat as best-effort at-most-once. (3) `events-log` \u2014 NOT bundled. Stores stream events in a DB and exposes `GET /plugins/events-log/events/?t1=<from>&t2=<to>` with `yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss` date-range filtering, for \"users who use scripts which listen to stream events [to] query for any events that they might have missed.\" This is your catch-up/replay mechanism, but the query surface is a timestamp range, not a monotonic cursor. `events-kafka` is the durable-broker option but its Apache-2.0 build is DEPRECATED and relicensed BSL by GerritForge.",
          "anchoring": "BEST-IN-CLASS, with an explicitly documented and explicitly non-deterministic fallback ladder. The feature is \"Ported Comments\" (https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/user-porting-comments.html). REST: `GET /changes/{change-id}/revisions/{revision-id}/ported_comments` and `GET .../ported_drafts`. Semantics: \"Ported comments are not copies of the comment but the comment simply shown in another place\" and \"Marking a ported comment resolved/unresolved will also update the original comment\" \u2014 so there is exactly one comment identity, no forwarding bookkeeping on your side. Ported: unresolved comments, unresolved drafts, resolved drafts. NOT ported: resolved comments. Documented fallback ladder when the position cannot be computed: exact position -> \"next best position\" -> drop `line`/`range` and become a file comment -> possibly move to a different file -> patch-set-level comment -> if the file is gone entirely (e.g. whole file reverted) the comment is simply NOT ported and \"is still present at its original location and visible in the Comments Tab & Change Log\". Three caveats the docs make you sign for: (a) \"Callers of this endpoint shouldn't rely on the exact logic of which comments are ported as that logic might change in the future\"; (b) the same comment UUID can be returned TWICE with different file/line/range when Gerrit detects a copied file \u2014 \"This is not a bug but a feature\"; (c) \"Repeated calls of this endpoint might produce different results\" because internal position-computation errors map to fallback locations and may resolve on a later call. Resolution state IS a first-class per-comment bit: `unresolved` on both CommentInfo and CommentInput, defaulting to the value of the `in_reply_to` comment when replying, and the thread's state is the last comment chronologically.",
          "ops_cost": "Moderate but real for a one-cluster small team. Runtime: JRE **21 required** as of 3.12 (https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/install.html); single fat war, `java -jar gerrit.war init -d <site>`; heap via `container.heapLimit` (-> -Xmx) and JGit `core.packedGitLimit` for the pack cache. DATABASE: none required. Since Gerrit 3.0, NoteDb is the only backend \u2014 all review metadata (patch sets, comments, votes, resolution state) lives in git itself: patch sets at `refs/changes/45/12345/<N>`, review metadata as an append-only notes branch at `refs/changes/45/12345/meta`, with inline comments keyed by patch-set commit SHA-1 and stored as JSON including author, line numbers and resolution status (https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/note-db.html). H2 is used for caches only (3.12 moved to H2 v2 and the release notes flag a known cache-file-corruption issue on ungraceful shutdown). Search index is Lucene on local disk by default; Elasticsearch/OpenSearch optional. Footprint: it is a STATEFUL single-primary JVM with a large PVC (bare repos + Lucene index + caches). Docker: official `gerritcodereview/gerrit` images, 3.14.2 ~432 MB. Kubernetes: GerritCodeReview/k8s-gerrit provides Helm charts and a Kubernetes Operator with CRDs GerritCluster, Gerrit, GitGarbageCollection, Receiver \u2014 actively developed (last commit 2026-07-10, repo pushed 2026-08-05) but SMALL: 38 stars, 23 forks, only tag v0.1. Treat it as a starting point, not a hardened product. UPGRADE PAIN is genuine: 3.12 mandates Java 21 and requires a **full reindex** (Lucene 10.1.0 bump); 3.14.1 moved the embedded server from Jetty 9 to Jetty 12 because Jetty 9 hit EOL and left Maven Central in Jan 2026; zero-downtime upgrades are only supported when running high-availability or multi-site. Version skipping is not free \u2014 the 2.x->3.x NoteDb migration had to route through 2.16.",
          "go_client": "EXISTS, workable, but thin and lagging \u2014 plan to fork or write your own. **andygrunwald/go-gerrit**, MIT, the de-facto Go client. Health: 106 stars, 53 forks, 31 open issues, 3 watchers, NOT archived. Releases: v1.0.0 (2024-10-20), v1.1.0 (2025-07-12), v1.1.1 (2025-12-05). Last commit 2026-03-16. Only ~17 commits in the trailing 12 months and most are renovate/dependabot dependency bumps; the last two substantive commits were community PRs (X-Gerrit-RunAs support, ChangeInfo.CurrentRevisionNumber). Effectively a one-maintainer project. Coverage: services for /access/, /accounts/, /changes/ (incl. changes_revision, changes_reviewer, changes_edit, changes_hashtags, changes_attention), /config/, /groups/, /plugins/, /projects/, plus the events-log plugin API. `ChangesService.SetReview(ctx, changeID, revisionID, *ReviewInput)` exists; ReviewInput covers Message/Tag/Labels/Comments/Reviewers/Ready/WorkInProgress/AttentionSet; CommentInput has InReplyTo and Unresolved. VERIFIED GAPS against master source: (1) it still ships `ReviewInput.RobotComments map[string][]RobotCommentInput` \u2014 dead weight against Gerrit 3.13+; (2) `CommentInput` has **no `fix_suggestions` field**, so you cannot post a suggested fix through the library unpatched; (3) no `ported_comments` / `ported_drafts` endpoint at all \u2014 the single most important endpoint for your anchoring problem is missing; (4) `FixSuggestions` is typed `*FixSuggestionInfo` rather than a slice, which contradicts the spec (\"a list of FixSuggestionInfo entities\"); (5) README still says it was \"implemented based on the REST API of Gerrit version 2.11.3\". The underlying REST surface is excellent and easy to hand-roll: plain JSON over HTTP basic auth with an auth token, `/a/` prefix for authenticated endpoints, responses prefixed with the `)]}'` XSSI guard which you strip. Writing a ~1k-line house client against the endpoints you actually need is a reasonable alternative to depending on go-gerrit.",
          "verdict": "strong-fit",
          "key_facts": [
            "DOCUMENTED: Change identity is the Change-Id trailer, resolved as (Change-Id, project, branch); Gerrit \"can automatically associate a new version of a change back to its original review, even across cherry-picks and rebases\" \u2014 https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/user-changeid.html",
            "DOCUMENTED: Patch sets are addressable at `refs/changes/<last two digits of change number>/<change number>/<patch set number>`, described as \"a static reference\" \u2014 https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/access-control.html#refs_changes . Fetch one with `git fetch <url> refs/changes/99/4799/2 && git checkout FETCH_HEAD`; RevisionInfo exposes `ref` plus a `fetch` map of ready-made Checkout/Cherry-Pick/Format-Patch/Pull commands per protocol.",
            "DOCUMENTED: Each revision names its own base. RevisionInfo carries `commit.parents[]`, and `parents_data` (list of ParentInfo with `commit_id`, `branch_name`, `is_merged_in_target_branch`, `change_id`, `change_number`, `patch_set_number`, `change_status`) under the `PARENTS` option. RevisionInfo.`kind` classifies the delta: REWORK / TRIVIAL_REBASE / TRIVIAL_REBASE_WITH_MESSAGE_UPDATE / MERGE_FIRST_PARENT_UPDATE / NO_CODE_CHANGE / NO_CHANGE \u2014 https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/rest-api-changes.html#revision-info",
            "DOCUMENTED: `POST /changes/{change-id}/rebase` (RebaseInput, optional `base` pointing at another patch set) creates a NEW patch set; earlier patch sets stay addressable \u2014 https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/rest-api-changes.html#rebase-change",
            "DOCUMENTED \u2014 CRITICAL REGRESSION: robot comments are REMOVED. Gerrit 3.12 (2025-05-19): \"Robot comments are disabled by default. Support for robot comments will be removed with the next release. Checks should be used instead.\" (https://www.gerritcodereview.com/3.12.html). Gerrit 3.13 (2025-11-10): \"Robot comments are not supported anymore and users have to move to the Checks API.\" (https://www.gerritcodereview.com/3.13.html, changes 474301/474303). VERIFIED: master Documentation/rest-api-changes.txt contains zero occurrences of \"robot\".",
            "DOCUMENTED: the rationale, from Ben Rohlfs (Google) in the deprecation thread \u2014 CI/analyzer data should not be stored in Gerrit because those systems have their own databases, and robot comments are \"transient and rapidly evolving\", unsuitable for permanent NoteDb storage. Pushback (Xiao Jia) was exactly your concern: robot comments needed only a service account, whereas Checks needs a deployed plugin. Rohlfs's answer: use ordinary comments \u2014 https://groups.google.com/g/repo-discuss/c/fIgEGcB2IAs",
            "DOCUMENTED: fix suggestions survived and moved onto ORDINARY comments. `fix_suggestions` is a field on both CommentInput and CommentInfo: FixSuggestionInfo {description, replacements:[FixReplacementInfo{path, range, replacement}]}. Apply with `POST /changes/{id}/revisions/{rev}/fixes/{fix-id}/apply`, preview with `GET .../fixes/{fix-id}/preview`. The magic path `/COMMIT_MSG` lets a fix edit the commit message.",
            "DOCUMENTED: the Checks API is FRONTEND-ONLY. `plugin.checks()`, `checksApi.register(provider, config)`, `provider.fetch()` returning CheckRun/CheckResult, `announceUpdate()`, `updateResult()`. Results are fetched by the browser from your external system; Gerrit persists nothing \u2014 https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/pg-plugin-checks-api.html",
            "DOCUMENTED: the server-side `checks` plugin is deprecated \u2014 \"The Gerrit team at Google has decided to discontinue work on the checks plugin. The recommended solution is [Checks UI] which surfaces results from an external CI/analysis system\" \u2014 https://gerrit.googlesource.com/plugins/checks/+doc/master/README.md (still gets build-maintenance commits, last 2026-07-31, but is not a product direction).",
            "DOCUMENTED \u2014 this is the direct answer to their GitHub self-trigger bug: the `tag` field on ReviewInput. \"Tags with an 'autogenerated:' prefix may be used by CI or other automated systems to distinguish them from human reviews. If another message was posted on a newer patchset, but with the same tag, then the older message will be hidden in the UI. Suffixes starting with `~` are not considered, so `autogenerated:my-ci-system~trigger` and `autogenerated:my-ci-system~result` will be considered being the same tag with regards to the hiding rule.\" The tag rides along on the emitted events, so consumers can filter their own writes.",
            "DOCUMENTED: the Service Users group (renamed from \"Non-Interactive Users\" in 3.3). Special behaviours: never added to the attention set; \"replies by such users will only add the change owner (and uploader) to the attention set, if it comes along with a negative vote\"; not suggested as reviewers; tagged `SERVICE_USER` in AccountInfo.tags; granted BATCH priority (separate thread pool) and the `Stream Events` capability by default; their vote is not required by the \"require Code-Review from all human reviewers\" submit requirement \u2014 https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/access-control.html#service_users and https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/user-attention-set.html#bots",
            "DOCUMENTED: Ported Comments. `GET /changes/{id}/revisions/{rev}/ported_comments` and `/ported_drafts`. Ports unresolved comments, unresolved drafts, resolved drafts; does NOT port resolved comments. \"Ported comments are not copies of the comment but the comment simply shown in another place\"; \"Marking a ported comment resolved/unresolved will also update the original comment.\" Documented failure ladder ends in \"we do not port this comment over. The comment is still present at its original location and visible in the Comments Tab & Change Log.\" \u2014 https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/user-porting-comments.html",
            "DOCUMENTED WARNING: the porting endpoint is explicitly non-deterministic. \"Callers of this endpoint shouldn't rely on the exact logic of which comments are ported as that logic might change in the future\"; the same comment UUID may be returned twice on copied files (\"This is not a bug but a feature\"); \"Repeated calls of this endpoint might produce different results.\" Any cache you build on top must be treated as a projection, not a source of truth.",
            "DOCUMENTED: NoteDb removed the relational database entirely. Gerrit 3.0+ supports only NoteDb; \"all data is stored in one location in the site directory.\" Patch sets at refs/changes/45/12345/N, review metadata as an append-only notes branch at refs/changes/45/12345/meta with comments as JSON keyed by patch-set SHA-1 including resolution status \u2014 https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/note-db.html",
            "DOCUMENTED/VERIFIED: `webhooks` and `replication` are BUNDLED core plugins \u2014 both are git submodules in the gerrit tree (`plugins/webhooks`, `plugins/replication`, alongside codemirror-editor, commit-message-length-validator, delete-project, download-commands, gitiles, hooks, plugin-manager, reviewnotes, singleusergroup). No third-party install needed for webhooks or GitHub mirroring.",
            "DOCUMENTED: webhooks delivery is best-effort. Per-remote `maxTries` (default 5, 0 disables), `retryInterval` (default 1000ms), `threadPoolSize` (default 2), and `sslVerify` **defaults to false**. \"After this number of failed tries, an error is logged\" \u2014 there is no durable queue and no delivery receipt \u2014 https://gerrit.googlesource.com/plugins/webhooks/+/refs/heads/master/src/main/resources/Documentation/config.md",
            "DOCUMENTED: stream-events has a `dropped-output` event \"Sent to notify a client that events have been dropped\", and \"any field may be missing in the JSON messages, so consumers of this JSON stream should deal with that appropriately.\" No ordering or completeness guarantee is stated \u2014 https://gerrit-review.googlesource.com/Documentation/cmd-stream-events.html",
            "DOCUMENTED: `events-log` plugin gives replay: `GET /plugins/events-log/events/?t1=<from>&t2=<to>` with `yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss` date ranges, explicitly for scripts to \"query for any events that they might have missed.\" Not bundled; must be installed.",
            "DOCUMENTED \u2014 LICENSE RISK: the Apache-2.0 `events-kafka` plugin README says \"GerritForge has decided to change the license to BSL therefore the Apache 2.0 version of this plugin is deprecated\" and points at GerritForge/events-kafka + GerritHub.io. The `github` plugin README carries the identical notice. Relicensing announced 2025-09-30.",
            "DOCUMENTED: GitHub interop via the bundled `replication` plugin is a first-class documented pattern. The config docs call out GitHub by name twice: a warning to omit the `+` force prefix \"when you are pushing to remote repositories that may have read/write access (e.g. GitHub) ... to prevent the risk of overwriting branches that have been modified on the remote\", and `remoteNameStyle` to cope with GitHub disallowing slashes in repo names. Default refspec is `refs/*:refs/*`; set `push = refs/heads/*:refs/heads/*` to mirror branches while explicitly NOT replicating `refs/changes/*` \u2014 https://gerrit.googlesource.com/plugins/replication/+/refs/heads/master/src/main/resources/Documentation/config.md",
            "DOCUMENTED: Gerrit 3.14 added \"Flows\" \u2014 server-side automation rules on a change, with REST endpoints `GET/POST /changes/{id}/flows/`, `GET/DELETE /changes/{id}/flows/{flow-id}`, `GET /changes/{id}/flows-actions/`. A flow is a list of stages, each a condition expression plus an action (e.g. condition `label:Verified+1` -> action `AddReviewer`), with stage state PENDING/DONE/FAILED/TERMINATED. Potentially useful for wiring 'agent verdict lands -> notify/route' without an external poller. New and unproven.",
            "INFERRED (strong evidence, no single doc sentence says \"immutable\"): patch sets are effectively immutable. Evidence: the refs are called \"static references\"; NoteDb meta is an append-only git notes history; clients push to the magic `refs/for/<branch>` and Gerrit \"maintains complete control over how the repository is updated\" and creates the refs/changes ref itself; and the REST API exposes Delete Change but no delete-or-modify for an individual patch set. Verify on your own install before relying on it as an invariant.",
            "INFERRED: the priority-1 requirement is fully met and the priority-2 requirement is met at reduced fidelity. A machine reviewer on Gerrit is an ordinary reviewer account with a `Service Users` membership and an `autogenerated:` tag. You lose robot_id/robot_run_id provenance and any bot-specific UI affordance; you keep threading, resolution state, suggested fixes, and votes."
          ]
        },
        {
          "name": "GerritHub.io (GerritForge hosted Gerrit-for-GitHub)",
          "license": "Closed / SaaS. The underlying Gerrit is Apache-2.0 but the hosted service and the GerritForge plugin stack backing it (github plugin, events-kafka) are now BSL / commercial.",
          "health": "Actively operated by GerritForge, who also hold 5 Gerrit maintainer seats. It is where GerritForge's post-BSL plugin development now happens (review.gerrithub.io).",
          "revision_noun": "YES \u2014 identical to Gerrit core, since it IS Gerrit. Same Change-Id / refs/changes/X/Y/Z / patch set model.",
          "machine_reviewer_api": "Same as Gerrit core (ordinary comments + labels + fix_suggestions; robot comments removed). Whatever Gerrit version GerritHub is running governs; you do not control the upgrade.",
          "push_events": "Available in principle (webhooks), but on a hosted multi-tenant instance you are subject to their configuration and network egress rules rather than your own gerrit.config.",
          "anchoring": "Same ported-comments behaviour as core.",
          "ops_cost": "Near-zero ops, but you give up control of version, plugin set, and data residency \u2014 and it is not self-hosted, which is an explicit requirement (#4).",
          "go_client": "Same REST surface, so andygrunwald/go-gerrit or a house client applies unchanged.",
          "verdict": "poor-fit",
          "key_facts": [
            "DOCUMENTED: \"Gerrit automatically replicates all the changes back and forth to GitHub. You can fetch your GitHub pull requests and upload them in Gerrit for review\" \u2014 https://review.gerrithub.io/static/intro.html",
            "DOCUMENTED: \"GerritHub pushes on your behalf your reviewed commits to your GitHub repositories. In order to get the replication work properly, you should push to Gerrit and use your GitHub repository for pull and pull-requests only.\" This is the canonical 'review in Gerrit, land in GitHub' pattern.",
            "DOCUMENTED: the self-hostable path to the same thing is the `github` plugin (GitHub OAuth + import pull requests as change sets), but its Apache-2.0 build is DEPRECATED \u2014 \"GerritForge has decided to change the license to BSL therefore the Apache 2.0 version of this plugin is deprecated\" \u2014 https://github.com/GerritCodeReview/plugins_github",
            "INFERRED: listed only for completeness. It fails requirement 4 (self-hostable) outright. If they want Gerrit's model on their own cluster, self-hosted Gerrit core + the bundled `replication` plugin pushing `refs/heads/*` to GitHub gives the same outcome with no BSL dependency."
          ]
        }
      ],
      "notes": "HOW THIS MAPS ONTO THE FOUR MEASURED GITHUB FAILURES\n\n1. \"The agent's reply is filed under a NEW review object, so the platform self-triggers.\" SOLVED STRUCTURALLY. Gerrit has no separate mutable \"review\" entity. A Set Review call produces a change message plus comments attached to a specific patch set; a reply is a comment with `in_reply_to` pointing at a comment UUID. Nothing new is minted that can look like a fresh human review. On top of that you get two independent guards: the `autogenerated:` tag prefix (rides along on emitted events, so your consumer can drop its own writes without a mirror table), and Service Users group membership (never enters the attention set; a reply only pulls the owner in if it carries a negative vote).\n\n2. \"Comment anchors go stale with no principled forwarding.\" SOLVED AS WELL AS ANY SYSTEM DOES IT, and honestly documented where it doesn't. `GET /changes/{id}/revisions/{rev}/ported_comments` forwards unresolved comments onto any newer patch set, and the ported comment IS the original comment (resolving the ported view resolves the original) \u2014 no forwarding bookkeeping on your side. The failure modes are written down rather than left to you: degrade to file comment, degrade to a different file, degrade to patch-set level, or refuse to port and leave it visible in the Comments Tab / Change Log. The docs also warn the endpoint is non-deterministic across calls, so treat any local copy as a projection.\n\n3. \"Two whole worktrees per observation, 789 MiB at 99.98% overlap, because a snapshot has a HEAD but a change has a BASE.\" SOLVED CLEANLY, and this is the single strongest argument for Gerrit. `refs/changes/X/Y/Z` is one addressable ref per patch set; the base is simply that commit's git parent, and `parents_data`/ParentInfo tells you whether the parent is a branch tip or patch set N of another change. One fetch, no duplicate worktree, and `RevisionInfo.kind` even classifies the delta (TRIVIAL_REBASE vs REWORK vs NO_CODE_CHANGE) \u2014 which is close to the semantic-rebase signal the current track is trying to synthesize.\n\n4. \"Server-side mirror state grew to a 45-column table.\" LARGELY DISSOLVED. Identity is (change number, patch set number). You can rebuild state on demand from `GET /changes/{id}/comments`, `/revisions/{n}/ported_comments`, and ChangeInfo with the PARENTS/CURRENT_REVISION options. You still need a small durable cursor, because stream-events is not replayable and webhooks retries are bounded and in-memory \u2014 but it can be a cursor, not a mirror.\n\nTHE ONE THING THAT ARGUES AGAINST GERRIT\n\nThe API purpose-built for exactly this use case was deleted eighteen months ago. Robot comments (robot_id, robot_run_id, robot-specific UI treatment, `/revisions/{id}/robot-comments`) were disabled by default in 3.12 and removed in 3.13. I verified this in master source, not just release notes: the string \"robot\" does not appear anywhere in Documentation/rest-api-changes.txt. The stated replacement, the Checks API, is a **frontend JavaScript plugin API** \u2014 check results live in your database and the browser fetches them per page load. There is no supported server-side REST endpoint to post a check result, and the server-side `checks` plugin that once provided one carries its own deprecation notice. Meanwhile the Jenkins-side `gerrit-code-review-plugin` also announced the checks plugin's deprecation, so this confusion is ecosystem-wide.\n\nThe practical consequence is modest but should be stated plainly: your agent becomes an ordinary reviewer account rather than a first-class robot. You keep threading (`in_reply_to`), per-comment resolution (`unresolved`), suggested fixes (`fix_suggestions` migrated onto ordinary comments and survived the purge), and verdicts (label votes). You lose machine-vs-human provenance in the data model \u2014 recoverable via the `autogenerated:` tag convention and the SERVICE_USER account tag, but by convention, not by type.\n\nOTHER THINGS WORTH KNOWING BEFORE COMMITTING\n\n- One change equals one commit. A multi-commit agent branch becomes N dependent Gerrit changes (a relation chain), not one reviewable unit. If the platform's mental model is \"a change is a branch\", that is a real impedance mismatch and probably the biggest modeling decision to make up front.\n- Every commit must carry a Change-Id trailer, and uploads go to the magic ref `refs/for/<branch>`, not a normal branch push. The agent's git plumbing changes \u2014 it needs the commit-msg hook or an equivalent trailer generator, plus push options (`%topic=`, `%notify=NONE`, `-o custom-keyed-value=k:v`) which are actually a nice channel for carrying ticket/run correlation IDs.\n- The `sslVerify` default in the webhooks plugin is `false`. Set it to true.\n- GerritForge relicensed its Apache-2.0 plugins to BSL on 2025-09-30 (events-kafka, github confirmed). Core and the bundled plugins are unaffected, but audit the license of any non-bundled plugin before depending on it.\n- Ops weight for a small team is real: Java 21, single-primary stateful JVM with a large PVC, full reindex required on the 3.12 upgrade, Jetty 9->12 in 3.14.1, and zero-downtime upgrades only under the high-availability/multi-site plugins. The k8s-gerrit operator exists and is active but is a 38-star project with one tag.\n- Reputation, honestly: reviews consistently recommend Gerrit for medium-to-large orgs with dedicated DevOps resources and explicitly advise against small teams prioritizing simplicity. That said, most of the UI complaints target the human experience, which matters far less when the primary reviewer is a program.\n\nSUGGESTED SHAPE IF THEY ADOPT IT\n\nSelf-hosted Gerrit 3.14 via the k8s-gerrit operator; agent runs as a Service Users member authenticating with an auth token over `/a/`; findings posted as ordinary threaded comments with `unresolved: true` and `fix_suggestions`; verdict as a vote on a dedicated label; every write tagged `autogenerated:jetbridge~<node>`; ingest via the bundled webhooks plugin filtered to patchset-created / comment-added / change-merged, with the events-log plugin installed purely as a gap-filler after a webhook outage; land in Gerrit and mirror `refs/heads/*` to GitHub via the bundled replication plugin (omit the `+` force prefix). Skip the Checks API entirely unless a Checks tab in the UI is worth shipping a TypeScript plugin for. Expect to fork or replace andygrunwald/go-gerrit \u2014 it lacks `ported_comments` and `fix_suggestions` on CommentInput, which are the two endpoints/fields this design depends on most.\n\nWORKING FILES: raw upstream docs downloaded for verification are in /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-tdmtrader-concourse-concourse--claude-worktrees-modest-noether-2975f7/1d03746f-d11d-456b-818a-0c15f2eced19/scratchpad/ (g-rest-api-changes.txt, g-access-control.txt, g-user-porting-comments.txt, g-user-attention-set.txt, webhooks-config.md, replication-config.md, events-kafka.md, github-plugin-README.md, and the go-gerrit sources gg-changes.go / gg-changes_revision.go). These are throwaway verification artifacts, not deliverables."

Robot comments really are removed (verified two ways). Health claims are accurate. Bundled-vs-third-party claims are accurate and verified against .gitmodules.\n\nWHAT DOES NOT HOLD \u2014 four material corrections, ranked by how much they should move the decision:\n\n1. FIX SUGGESTIONS ARE EXPERIMENT-GATED AND OFF BY DEFAULT. This is the most consequential error. The report states flatly that \"Fix suggestions SURVIVED the robot-comment removal and now live on ordinary comments,\" presenting this as the compensating capability for losing robot comments. The Gerrit 3.10 release notes say the field \"is only written if the experiment is enabled,\" requiring `[experiments] enabled = GerritBackendFeature__allow_fix_suggestions_in_comments` in gerrit.config, and the config REST API shows it defaulting to `\"enabled\": false`. The report never mentions the flag. Since the report's own PRACTICAL VERDICT recommends \"findings as ordinary threaded comments with fix_suggestions,\" this gate sits directly under the recommendation. It is likely fine to turn on \u2014 but it is an experiment flag on a data-writing feature (it stores to NoteDb), not a settled API, and it should be verified per-version before anything is designed against it.\n\n2. FLOWS IS MISATTRIBUTED AND IS AN EMPTY INTERFACE IN CORE. The report credits 3.14 with adding Flows. Flows was introduced in 3.13, experimental behind `UiFeature__show_flows_tab`; 3.14 redesigned the UI and added the REST API. More importantly, the 3.14 notes state Flows is \"disabled if no FlowService is registered\" \u2014 core Gerrit ships the pluggable service interface, not an implementation. The report's \"potentially useful for wiring agent verdict lands -> notify/route without an external poller\" is therefore not achievable out of the box; you would be writing the FlowService plugin. Downgrade from \"new and unproven\" to \"not present without a plugin you write.\"\n\n3. THE PORTED-COMMENTS CAVEATS ARE NOT AT THE CITED URL. The report attributes three specific caveats \u2014 callers \"shouldn't rely on the exact logic,\" the same UUID returned twice on copied files (\"This is not a bug but a feature\"), and \"Repeated calls of this endpoint might produce different results\" \u2014 to user-porting-comments.html, and builds its \"explicitly documented non-deterministic fallback ladder\" framing on them. I fetched that page as HTML and its master .txt source independently; neither contains any of the three statements, and an exact-phrase web search returned nothing. The doc's actual fallback is much simpler than the reported five-rung ladder: attempt to compute position, else \"Gerrit attaches these comments as File Level Comments,\" else (e.g. whole file reverted) the comment is not ported and stays at its original location. The favorable core semantics DO check out verbatim \u2014 \"Ported comments are not copies of the comment but the comment simply shown in another place,\" and the ported/not-ported sets (unresolved comments, unresolved drafts, resolved drafts port; resolved comments do not). So anchoring is still genuinely good; but treat the reported determinism warnings as unsourced. If those caveats exist they are elsewhere (plausibly the REST endpoint section), and the practical implication is the same either way: verify behavior on your own install rather than trusting either report.\n\n4. THE PARENTS/KIND DETAIL IS THINNER THAN REPORTED. `parents_data` under the `PARENTS` option and the field `is_merged_in_target_branch` are real. But the report's precise seven-field ParentInfo list, the six-value `kind` enum, and a per-revision `branch` field could not be confirmed from documentation across four fetch attempts (rest-api-changes is large enough that every extraction truncated before the entity definitions; the extractions that did resolve returned only `commit`/`subject`, which is CommitInfo's `parents[]`, not ParentInfo, and one explicitly said `branch` is a ChangeInfo attribute, not a RevisionInfo one). The \"each revision names its own base\" conclusion is still supported by `commit.parents[]` alone, which is unambiguous. The richer ParentInfo story is UNVERIFIABLE as written \u2014 do not design the base-tracking layer against those exact field names without reading the entity docs on a running instance.\n\nSMALLER PROBLEMS: go-gerrit is reported with 31 open issues; GitHub shows 15 (stars 106 and forks 53 are correct). The report states \"GerritCodeReview/gerrit last pushed 2026-08-07\" \u2014 a date in the future as of 2026-08-06; fabricated precision, though daily activity is not otherwise in doubt. The Jetty 9\u219212 move is in the 3.14 line generally per the 3.14 release notes, not specifically 3.14.1 as claimed. The claim that the `tag` \"rides along on the emitted events, so consumers can filter their own writes\" \u2014 the crucial mechanism for the team's self-trigger bug \u2014 is the one part of the tag story I could NOT verify; documentation confirms `autogenerated:` tags exist and are filterable in the web UI (PolyGerrit, since 2.16) and apply to change messages, votes and inline comments, but I found no doc confirming the tag appears in stream-events/webhook payloads. Verify this before relying on it, because it is precisely the GitHub failure mode they are trying to escape.\n\nON THE VERDICT: the report says \"strong-fit.\" I would say GOOD-FIT WITH A BUILD BUDGET. Priority 1 (revision noun) is genuinely, uniquely met \u2014 no correction survives that would undermine it. Priority 5 (anchoring) is met. Priorities 3 and 4 are met with caveats the report states honestly. But priority 2 (machine-reviewer API) is weaker than the report concludes: robot comments gone, fix suggestions behind an experiment flag, no server-side check storage at all, the checks plugin deprecated, Flows requiring a plugin you write, and the go-gerrit client missing every endpoint that matters here (ported_comments, fix_suggestions on CommentInput) while still carrying dead robot-comment types. The honest framing is that Gerrit gives them the data model they cannot get anywhere else, and they will hand-write the machine-reviewer layer on top of a clean REST surface. That is still likely the right trade \u2014 but it is a build, not an adoption.\n\nONE THING THE REPORT GOT RIGHT THAT DESERVES EMPHASIS: it labelled patch-set immutability as INFERRED rather than documented, and that restraint is correct. \"Static reference\" is a naming convention plus an access-control boundary, not a documented immutability invariant. Verify on your own install before treating it as one \u2014 it is the single assumption their whole sealing/observation design would rest on.",
