  {
    "survey": {
      "area": "Self-hostable GitHub-shaped forges: does GitLab CE or Forgejo/Gitea have a revision noun, and how good are their machine-reviewer APIs",
      "systems": [
        {
          "name": "GitLab CE / GitLab Free (self-managed)",
          "license": "MIT Expat for everything in gitlab-org/gitlab outside `ee/`, `jh/` and `doc/` (doc/ is CC BY-SA 4.0; ee/ and jh/ are proprietary \u2014 root LICENSE at https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/blob/master/LICENSE). The 100%-FOSS tree is gitlab-org/gitlab-foss, same MIT terms. Everything this evaluation depends on (diff versions, discussions, draft notes, approve/unapprove, webhooks) is documented `Tier: Free, Premium, Ultimate`, i.e. usable on an unlicensed self-managed install. INFERRED: most self-managed installs run the gitlab-ee package unlicensed rather than gitlab-foss; that ships proprietary ee/ code on disk even though it is gated off.",
          "health": "Current stable is 19.2 (released 2026-07-16); 19.3 not yet released as of 2026-08-06 (https://docs.gitlab.com/releases/19/gitlab-19-3-released/). Monthly minor releases plus patch releases, maintained by GitLab Inc. (public company). Healthiest of the three by a wide margin, at the cost of being a vendor-controlled open-core product with constant gravitational pull toward the paid tier.",
          "revision_noun": "YES \u2014 the closest thing to a real revision noun in any mainstream self-hostable forge. The object is `MergeRequestDiff`, exposed as a \"merge request diff version\": `GET /projects/:id/merge_requests/:iid/versions` and `GET .../versions/:version_id` (https://docs.gitlab.com/api/merge_requests/#retrieve-merge-request-diff-versions). Each version has its own integer `id`, `created_at`, `real_size`, `state`, `patch_id_sha` (git patch-id of the series) and, critically, all three SHAs: `base_commit_sha` = merge-base of source and target, `start_commit_sha` = HEAD of the TARGET branch when this version was created, `head_commit_sha` = HEAD of the source branch (https://docs.gitlab.com/api/merge_requests/#shas-in-the-api-response). A version names its own base \u2014 exactly the \"a snapshot has a HEAD but a change has a BASE\" gap. One version per PUSH, not per commit, never per API call (https://docs.gitlab.com/user/project/merge_requests/versions/). Versions are individually addressable and looked up server-side by the exact (base, start, head) triple: `MergeRequest#merge_request_diff_for` in app/models/merge_request.rb matches on all three columns. LIMITS, bluntly: (a) the CHANGE identity is still `merge_requests.iid` bound to a mutable source-branch name \u2014 no Gerrit-style Change-Id, so you get an immutable revision series hanging off a mutable change pointer; (b) you cannot create, name or seal a version through the API \u2014 a version only appears as a side effect of a git push; (c) immutability is by construction, not enforced: no API mutates the SHA triple, but the state machine has a `clean` event transitioning any state to `without_files`, so the system can drop a version's diff FILE rows while keeping the version row and its SHAs (app/models/merge_request_diff.rb); (d) INFERRED: a second hidden diff_type `merge_head` is recreated on mergeability checks and is not returned by the versions endpoint.",
          "machine_reviewer_api": "Strong, and all of it Free tier. Threads: `POST /projects/:id/merge_requests/:iid/discussions` with a full `position[]` hash \u2014 `position[base_sha]`, `position[head_sha]`, `position[start_sha]` (the doc explicitly says to copy them out of the versions response), `position[old_path]`/`[new_path]`, `position[old_line]`/`[new_line]`, `position[line_range]` for multi-line (https://docs.gitlab.com/api/discussions/#create-a-merge-request-thread). Reply in thread: `POST .../discussions/:discussion_id/notes`. Resolve/unresolve: `PUT .../discussions/:discussion_id` with `resolved=true|false` (https://docs.gitlab.com/api/discussions/#resolve-a-merge-request-thread). Batched review \u2014 the real machine-reviewer primitive \u2014 is the Draft Notes API: `POST .../draft_notes` accepts `position[]`, `in_reply_to_discussion_id` AND `resolve_discussion`, then `POST .../draft_notes/bulk_publish` with `note` (summary body) and `reviewer_state` = `requested_changes` | `reviewed` (https://docs.gitlab.com/api/draft_notes/). Stage N inline comments against a named version, publish them atomically with a verdict, one call, one review. Verdicts: `POST .../approve` accepts `sha` and returns 409 Conflict on head mismatch; `POST .../unapprove`; `PUT .../reset_approvals` is restricted to bot users with a project/group token \u2014 humans get 401 (https://docs.gitlab.com/api/merge_request_approvals/). Approval itself is Free (Developer+ may approve, advisory only); required approval RULES, code-owner rules and instance/group approval settings are Premium/Ultimate, and the webhook's `object_attributes.approval_rules` array is marked EE only. CI/check results: `POST /projects/:id/statuses/:sha` (https://docs.gitlab.com/api/commits/).",
          "push_events": "Real webhooks, no polling, no mirrored cursor. Merge Request Hook fires on open/close/reopen/update/merge plus `approval` (one user approved), `approved` (fully approved), `unapproval`, `unapproved`; `object_attributes.oldrev` is present on `update` when code was actually pushed \u2014 your \"a new version exists\" signal; `object_attributes.system: true` + `system_action` (`approvals_reset_on_push`, `code_owner_approvals_reset_on_push`) distinguishes machine-caused from human events (https://docs.gitlab.com/user/project/integrations/webhook_events/#merge-request-events). Note Hook fires on comment create/update with the full `position` block and `discussion_id`. Delivery headers give HMAC-SHA256 `webhook-signature` (GA in 19.1), `webhook-id`/`Idempotency-Key` stable across retries, and `X-Gitlab-Event-UUID` PROPAGATED through recursive chains (https://docs.gitlab.com/user/project/integrations/webhooks/#delivery-headers). GAPS: system notes never fire a Note Hook (`Notes::PostProcessService#execute` returns early `unless note.system?`), and INFERRED from source, a reviewer-state change to `requested_changes` with no published comment fires no webhook at all \u2014 `MergeRequests::UpdateReviewerStateService` only triggers GraphQL subscriptions and a system note, never `execute_hooks`. Drive the loop off Note Hook + approval actions, not reviewer state.",
          "anchoring": "Best-in-class among GitHub-shaped forges, with a documented fallback. A `DiffNote` carries THREE positions: `original_position` (validated `presence: true`, never rewritten), `position` (current, forwarded) and `change_position` (where it moved to when forwarding failed) \u2014 all three appear in API and webhook payloads. On every new version, `Discussions::UpdateDiffPositionService` runs `Gitlab::Diff::PositionTracer` over old_diff_refs \u2192 new_diff_refs; on success `note.position` is updated and `change_position` cleared; on failure `change_position` is set, a `diff_discussion_outdated` system note is posted, and if the project has `resolve_outdated_diff_discussions` enabled the thread auto-resolves with `resolved_by_push: true` (https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/blob/master/app/services/discussions/update_diff_position_service.rb; project flag in https://docs.gitlab.com/api/projects/). A `NoteDiffFile` snapshot of the original hunk is persisted at note creation so an outdated comment still renders its original context. Separately, GitLab pins SHAs so revisions stay resolvable after a force-push: `MergeRequests::KeepAroundRefsWorker` creates `refs/keep-around/<oid>` for each version's `start_commit_sha` and `head_commit_sha` (app/models/merge_request_diff.rb; ref namespace documented at https://docs.gitlab.com/development/gitaly/, which also warns keep-around refs \"have no lifecycle\" and says not to build NEW functionality on them). Self-documented bugs: unchanged-line anchoring (gitlab-org/gitlab#325161) and bad-SHA behaviour (#296829), both linked from the discussions API page.",
          "ops_cost": "Heaviest by an order of magnitude. Documented single-node baseline 8 vCPU / 16 GB RAM, 8 GB floor in a memory-constrained config (https://docs.gitlab.com/install/requirements/). Requires PostgreSQL plus Redis 7.2 or Valkey; runs Puma, Sidekiq, Gitaly, Workhorse, NGINX. Official Helm chart and a GitLab Operator exist for a single Kubernetes cluster. Storage risk for keeping every revision: diffs land in `merge_request_diff_files` in Postgres and that table is the documented growth problem; mitigation is `external_diffs_enabled` with `external_diffs_when: 'outdated'`, which offloads a version's diff to disk or object storage once a newer version exists or 7 days after merge/close (https://docs.gitlab.com/administration/merge_request_diffs/). Offloaded, not deleted \u2014 the version row and its SHA triple survive.",
          "go_client": "`gitlab.com/gitlab-org/api/client-go/v2` \u2014 v2.56.0 tagged 2026-08-05, i.e. active to the day, released every few days, in the gitlab-org namespace. Adopted successor to `github.com/xanzy/go-gitlab`, which is dead (last release v0.115.0, 2024-12-10). Coverage is already there: `MergeRequestsService.GetMergeRequestDiffVersions` / `GetSingleMergeRequestDiffVersion` (merge_requests.go), a full `DiscussionsService`, and a `DraftNotesService` with Create/Update/Publish/PublishAllDraftNotes. One gap: `PublishAllDraftNotes(pid, mergeRequest, ...RequestOptionFunc)` takes no options struct, so it cannot send `note` or `reviewer_state` \u2014 hand-roll that one POST or patch upstream (INFERRED from the signature in draft_notes.go).",
          "verdict": "partial-fit",
          "key_facts": [
            "DOCUMENTED: a merge request diff version records `base_commit_sha` (merge-base), `start_commit_sha` (target-branch HEAD at the time) and `head_commit_sha` (source HEAD), plus `patch_id_sha`. https://docs.gitlab.com/api/merge_requests/#shas-in-the-api-response",
            "DOCUMENTED: versions are individually addressable \u2014 `GET /projects/:id/merge_requests/:iid/versions/:version_id` returns that version's commits and diffs. https://docs.gitlab.com/api/merge_requests/#retrieve-a-merge-request-diff-version",
            "DOCUMENTED: \"Diff versions are updated on each push, not each commit.\" https://docs.gitlab.com/user/project/merge_requests/versions/",
            "DOCUMENTED: to anchor a comment you pass the version's three SHAs verbatim as position[base_sha]/[start_sha]/[head_sha]. https://docs.gitlab.com/api/discussions/#create-a-merge-request-thread",
            "DOCUMENTED: `POST .../draft_notes/bulk_publish` takes `note` and `reviewer_state` (`requested_changes`|`reviewed`) \u2014 batched inline comments plus a verdict in one call, Free tier. https://docs.gitlab.com/api/draft_notes/",
            "DOCUMENTED: approval is Free and advisory; required approval RULES are Premium/Ultimate; `object_attributes.approval_rules` in the webhook is EE only. https://docs.gitlab.com/user/project/merge_requests/approvals/ and https://docs.gitlab.com/api/merge_request_approvals/",
            "DOCUMENTED: `PUT .../merge_requests/:iid/reset_approvals` works only for bot users with a project/group token; humans get 401. https://docs.gitlab.com/api/merge_request_approvals/",
            "DOCUMENTED: `POST .../approve` with `sha` returns 409 Conflict on head mismatch \u2014 a built-in staleness guard for a bot verdict.",
            "SOURCE-DOCUMENTED: outdated diff comments set `change_position`, emit a `diff_discussion_outdated` system note, and auto-resolve with `resolved_by_push: true` when `resolve_outdated_diff_discussions` is set. https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/blob/master/app/services/discussions/update_diff_position_service.rb",
            "SOURCE-DOCUMENTED: `Gitlab::WebHooks::RecursionDetection` blocks a webhook re-triggered by an API call carrying the originating `X-Gitlab-Event-UUID`, COUNT_LIMIT 100 on chain length \u2014 a real platform answer to the GitHub self-trigger problem. https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/blob/master/lib/gitlab/web_hooks/recursion_detection.rb",
            "SOURCE-DOCUMENTED: system notes never fire a Note Hook \u2014 `Notes::PostProcessService#execute` short-circuits `unless note.system?`. https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/blob/master/app/services/notes/post_process_service.rb",
            "INFERRED (source, not docs): a reviewer setting state `requested_changes` with no published note fires NO webhook \u2014 `MergeRequests::UpdateReviewerStateService` calls GraphQL subscription triggers and SystemNoteService only, never `execute_hooks`.",
            "DOCUMENTED ABSENCE: no REST endpoint diffs version N against version N+1. `GET .../merge_requests/:iid/diffs` is latest-only with no version selector; per-version diffs come only from `/versions/:version_id`. \"Compare diff versions\" is a UI feature (INFERRED: the UI passes `diff_id` and `start_sha` query params). https://docs.gitlab.com/user/project/merge_requests/versions/#compare-diff-versions",
            "DOCUMENTED: old versions' commits are pinned against GC via `refs/keep-around/<object-id>`, created by `MergeRequests::KeepAroundRefsWorker` from each version's start and head SHAs \u2014 revision N's base and head stay resolvable after a force-push. https://docs.gitlab.com/development/gitaly/",
            "DOCUMENTED: service accounts are Free tier and GA since 18.11 (https://docs.gitlab.com/user/profile/service_accounts/); project access tokens, which mint a bot user, are Free (https://docs.gitlab.com/api/project_access_tokens/).",
            "DOCUMENTED: 8 vCPU / 16 GB single-node baseline, PostgreSQL + Redis/Valkey required. https://docs.gitlab.com/install/requirements/"
          ]

          "name": "Forgejo",
          "license": "GPL-3.0-or-later (verified against the LICENSE file at tag v16.0.2, https://codeberg.org/forgejo/forgejo/src/tag/v16.0.2/LICENSE). Copyleft \u2014 a genuine consideration if you ship modified server code as part of a product. No paid tier, no feature gating.",
          "health": "Genuinely healthy and independent. Latest stable v16.0.2 (2026-07-30); maintained LTS lines v15.0.6 (2026-07-30) and v11.0.16 (2026-07-09). 50 commits in the ten days to 2026-08-07. Governed by the Forgejo project itself with domains held by Codeberg e.V., a democratic non-profit for Free Software (https://forgejo.org/docs/latest/developer/governance/) \u2014 the deliberate contrast to Gitea's for-profit steward. Hard fork of Gitea since the v1.21 era; the codebases have now diverged enough that their APIs are not interchangeable.",
          "revision_noun": "NO. Forgejo inherits the GitHub shape wholesale. A pull request is `models/issues/pull.go` with a mutable `HeadCommitID` and a mutable `refs/pull/N/head`. No diff-version table, no patchset, no per-revision base. AGit-flow does not change this: `services/agit/agit.go` at v16.0.2, on a push to an existing topic, literally does `pr.HeadCommitID = opts.NewCommitIDs[i]` then `pull_service.UpdateRef(ctx, pr)` and creates a `CommentTypePullRequestPush` timeline comment recording old\u2192new OID. That comment is an audit line, not an addressable diff with its own base. The AGit doc confirms the model: association is by `topic` push option plus \"a linear commit history\", and if you rebase without `-o force-push` \"a new pull request will be opened, even if you use the same topic\" (https://forgejo.org/docs/latest/user/agit-support/). AGit is PR-creation-over-push, nothing more.",
          "machine_reviewer_api": "Decent GitHub-clone surface, one Forgejo-specific bonus, one serious hole. Available: `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/{index}/reviews` with `CreatePullReviewOptions{body, commit_id, event, comments[]}` where each comment is `{path, body, old_position, new_position, extra_lines_count}`; `POST .../reviews/{id}` submits a PENDING review (`SubmitPullReviewOptions{body, event}`); `POST .../reviews/{id}/comments` ADDS a comment to an existing review and `DELETE .../reviews/{id}/comments/{comment}` removes one \u2014 both Forgejo-only, Gitea lacks them; `POST .../reviews/{id}/dismissals` and `/undismissals`; `POST/DELETE .../requested_reviewers`. CI/check results: `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/statuses/{sha}`. THE HOLE: no resolve-conversation endpoint in v16.0.2 \u2014 the route tree in routers/api/v1/api.go has no `/pulls/comments/{id}/resolve`, and Codeberg's live swagger (Forgejo 16.0.0-dev) confirms it. `PullReviewComment` exposes a read-only `resolver` field so a bot can SEE who resolved a thread but cannot resolve one. Gitea 1.27 can. Verified by diffing https://codeberg.org/swagger.v1.json against https://gitea.com/swagger.v1.json.",
          "push_events": "Webhooks exist, typed, no polling. Event types at v16.0.2 (modules/webhook/type.go): create, delete, fork, push, issues, issue_assign, issue_label, issue_milestone, issue_comment, pull_request, pull_request_assign, pull_request_label, pull_request_milestone, pull_request_comment, pull_request_review_approved, pull_request_review_rejected, pull_request_review_comment, pull_request_sync, pull_request_review_request, wiki, repository, release, package, schedule, workflow_dispatch, action_run_failure, action_run_recover, action_run_success. `pull_request_sync` is the \"head moved\" signal. DIVERGENCE: Forgejo v16.0.2 has NO `status` hook event; Gitea 1.27 does. Loop suppression: none built in \u2014 no recursion-detection equivalent to GitLab's; you filter on sender user id yourself.",
          "anchoring": "Weakest of the three and effectively unusable for a multi-round agent loop. A code comment stores `TreePath`, a signed `Line` (negative = old side), and the `CommitSHA` the line blamed to at creation. On every push, `services/pull/review.go#InvalidateCodeComments` re-blames each line on the head branch; if the blame commit differs from the stored `CommitSHA`, or blame fails / has too few lines, it sets `Invalidated = true` and persists it. No re-anchoring, no forwarding, no new position, no reverse path \u2014 invalidation is terminal. The source carries `// FIXME differentiate between previous and proposed line` inside `checkInvalidation`. Worse for a machine consumer: `Invalidated` is NOT in the API representation \u2014 `PullReviewComment` has body, commit_id, created_at, diff_hunk, html_url, id, original_commit_id, original_position, path, position, pull_request_review_id, pull_request_url, resolver, updated_at, user. The API cannot tell your bot that a comment went stale. `original_commit_id`/`original_position` are preserved but `position` is never recomputed. No keep-around-ref equivalent, so after a force-push the previous head's objects become unreachable and prunable \u2014 exactly the failure that pushed you into sealing worktrees.",
          "ops_cost": "Excellent, and the reason people pick it. Single static Go binary plus SQLite, PostgreSQL or MySQL; official container images and a Helm chart; comfortably fits a few hundred MB of RAM. Neither Forgejo nor Gitea publishes numeric minimum hardware requirements \u2014 Gitea's own comparison table simply marks \"Low RAM/CPU usage\" \u2713 for Gitea and \u2718 for GitLab CE/EE (https://docs.gitea.com/installation/comparison). For a small team on one Kubernetes cluster this is a rounding error next to GitLab.",
          "go_client": "Awkward. `codeberg.org/mvdkleijn/forgejo-sdk/forgejo` is the community SDK: last TAGGED release v1.2.0 on 2024-10-20, though the repo is still being touched (commits through 2026-07-29, e.g. \"Add draft to PullRequest type\") \u2014 alive but nearly two years without a tag, and the Go module proxy will hand you the 2024 version. `code.gitea.io/sdk/gitea` v0.25.1 (2026-05-12) also works against Forgejo for the shared surface but by definition misses Forgejo-only routes (per-review comment endpoints) and exposes Gitea-only routes Forgejo lacks (resolve/unresolve). Both are thin REST wrappers; hand-rolling the ~8 calls you need is a reasonable alternative.",

            "SOURCE-DOCUMENTED: comment anchoring is a one-shot blame check that sets a terminal `Invalidated` flag; the source contains `// FIXME differentiate between previous and proposed line`. https://github.com/go-gitea/gitea/blob/v1.27.1/services/pull/review.go (same code in Forgejo)",

Gitea 1.27 LACKS the per-review comment mutation endpoints Forgejo has: `GET .../reviews/{id}/comments` exists but there is no POST to add a comment to an existing review and no DELETE.       "notes": "BLUNT ANSWER TO THE QUESTION ASKED\n\nForgejo and Gitea inherit the GitHub problem wholesale. Neither has a revision noun. A pull request is a row with a mutable `HeadCommitID` and a mutable `refs/pull/N/head`; the only per-push artefact is a timeline comment recording old\u2192new OID, which is not addressable and carries no base. AGit-flow \u2014 the thing people cite when claiming these forges are more than GitHub clones \u2014 is push-to-create-PR and nothing else, verified in source at codeberg.org/forgejo/forgejo/src/tag/v16.0.2/services/agit/agit.go where the update path is `pr.HeadCommitID = opts.NewCommitIDs[i]; UpdateRef(...)`. Adopting either would reproduce every one of your five measured consequences, and would make comment anchoring strictly WORSE than GitHub: their anchoring is a one-shot blame check that sets a terminal `Invalidated` flag which is not even exposed in the REST schema, so a machine reviewer cannot detect its own stale comments.\n\nGitLab CE is a different answer, and is the only mainstream self-hostable forge that has actually modelled this. It gives you an ordered series of immutable, individually addressable diff versions, each naming its own base \u2014 `base_commit_sha` (merge-base), `start_commit_sha` (target HEAD at the time), `head_commit_sha` (source HEAD) \u2014 created once per push, addressable at `/merge_requests/:iid/versions/:version_id`, resolvable server-side by the exact SHA triple, with the old commits pinned against GC by `refs/keep-around/<oid>`. That dissolves four of your five pains:\n  - base-vs-head: solved outright. A version IS a (base, start, head) triple. You do not need to seal two worktrees per observation; you need one snapshot plus the triple, and the forge guarantees the SHAs stay resolvable.\n  - comment anchoring: solved with a documented fallback. Three positions per diff note (`original_position` immutable, `position` forwarded by `Gitlab::Diff::PositionTracer`, `change_position` set when forwarding fails), plus a `diff_discussion_outdated` system note, plus optional project-level auto-resolve on outdated. When it cannot forward it says so in the payload \u2014 exactly the \"documented answer when it can't\" you asked for.\n  - self-trigger: solved at the platform level, not by convention. `Gitlab::WebHooks::RecursionDetection` blocks a webhook re-triggered by an API call carrying the originating `X-Gitlab-Event-UUID` and caps chains at 100. Additionally there is no \"review object\" whose creation your reply gets filed under \u2014 replies are notes in a discussion, and system notes never fire Note Hooks at all.\n  - 45-column mirror table: mostly solved. Webhooks carry `webhook-id`/`Idempotency-Key` stable across retries and HMAC-SHA256 signatures, and the version list is authoritative and immutable, so you key off (mr_iid, version_id) instead of mirroring state.\n\nWHAT GITLAB DOES NOT GIVE YOU \u2014 the honest residue:\n  - The CHANGE identity is still a mutable branch: `merge_requests.iid` plus a source-branch name. No Change-Id, no identity independent of the ref. You get an immutable revision series hanging off a mutable change pointer. If priority #1's first clause is non-negotiable, GitLab does not satisfy it and only Gerrit-lineage systems will.\n  - You cannot create or seal a revision. A version appears only as a side effect of a git push. There is no `POST /versions`, no way for the agent to declare \"this is revision 3\", no way to attach agent-side metadata to a version.\n  - There is no REST endpoint that diffs version N against version N+1. `/diffs` is latest-only; per-version diffs come from `/versions/:version_id` and are always against that version's own base. \"Compare versions\" is UI-only (INFERRED: the UI passes `diff_id` and `start_sha`). Computing inter-revision diffs yourself is trivial since you hold all six SHAs.\n  - Reviewer-state-only signals are invisible to webhooks (INFERRED from `MergeRequests::UpdateReviewerStateService`, which never calls `execute_hooks`). Drive the loop off Note Hook and the `approval`/`approved`/`unapproval`/`unapproved` MR actions.\n  - Operational cost is real: 8 vCPU / 16 GB single-node baseline plus Postgres and Redis/Valkey plus Gitaly/Sidekiq/Workhorse/Puma. For a small team on one cluster that is a serious tax next to a 200 MB Go binary. Budget for `external_diffs_enabled` with `when: 'outdated'` from day one or `merge_request_diff_files` becomes your largest table.\n\nTIER BOUNDARY, since you asked to be explicit about what is EE/paid:\n  FREE, usable on a self-hosted OSS deployment: diff versions API; discussions API with full position hashes; thread resolve/unresolve; the entire Draft Notes API including `bulk_publish` with `reviewer_state`; approve/unapprove/reset_approvals; commit status API; project and group webhooks with signing tokens; project access tokens; service accounts (GA Free since 18.11).\n  PREMIUM/ULTIMATE, off the table: required approval rules at project/MR/group/instance scope; CODEOWNERS-driven approvals; approval settings enforcement; the `object_attributes.approval_rules` webhook array. Practically: your bot can approve and nothing blocks it, so \"N approvals required before merge\" must be enforced by JetBridge, not by GitLab CE.\n\nFORK CHOICE IF YOU GO FORGEJO/GITEA ANYWAY (e.g. as a thin git host under a JetBridge-owned change model): the two have diverged precisely on the machine-reviewer surface. Gitea 1.27 has `POST /pulls/comments/{id}/resolve` and `/unresolve`; Forgejo 16.0.2 does not. Forgejo 16.0.2 has `POST`/`DELETE` on `/pulls/{index}/reviews/{id}/comments`; Gitea 1.27 does not. Gitea has a `status` webhook event; Forgejo does not. Verified by diffing the two live swagger documents. Neither has a resolve-thread-and-reply-within-one-review primitive comparable to GitLab's draft-notes flow.\n\nRECOMMENDATION IMPLIED BY THE EVIDENCE: if the team wants to adopt rather than invent, GitLab CE is the only candidate here worth a spike, and the spike should be narrow \u2014 can JetBridge treat `(project_id, mr_iid, version_id)` as the observation key, post a batched review against a named version's SHA triple via draft notes + `bulk_publish`, and survive a force-push without re-sealing worktrees? If yes, four of five pains go away and what remains is a mutable change identity you can paper over with a trailer or a JetBridge-side change record. If a truly commit-independent change identity is required, neither GitLab CE nor the Gitea family provides it and the search must move to Gerrit-lineage systems.\n\nWorking files from this research (raw upstream docs and source, downloaded for grepping) are in /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-tdmtrader-concourse-concourse--claude-worktrees-modest-noether-2975f7/1d03746f-d11d-456b-818a-0c15f2eced19/scratchpad/ \u2014 notably mr.md, disc.md, draft.md, appr.md, user_project_integrations_webhook_events.md, webhooks.md, updpos.rb, rc.go, agit.go, fj_api.go, forgejo_swagger.json and gitea_swagger.json."

I found **six** defects, one of which is a real correctness error against the code the reporter themselves cited. The verdict \"partial-fit\" stands and I would not soften it.\n\nMethod note: my first three attempts to read https://docs.gitlab.com/api/merge_requests/ returned \"section not found\" for the versions endpoint. That was **fetch truncation, not absence** \u2014 the page is 4,455 lines. I re-verified by downloading doc/api/merge_requests.md from master and grepping. Anyone re-checking this via a summarizing fetcher will get a false negative on the report's central claim. The anchor is real (line 4080).\n\n=== WHAT SURVIVED (the important part) ===\n\n(d) THE REVISION-NOUN CLAIM SURVIVES SCRUTINY. This was the claim I most expected to break, and it held at the schema level, not just the UI level:\n- doc/api/merge_requests.md line 4080 documents `GET /projects/:id/merge_requests/:merge_request_iid/versions`, and line 4146 the per-version `GET .../versions/:version_id`. Both are real REST endpoints, page tier `Free, Premium, Ultimate`.\n- The \"SHAs in the API response\" table is verbatim as reported: `base_commit_sha` = \"The merge-base commit SHA between the source branch and the target branches\"; `start_commit_sha` = \"The HEAD commit SHA of the target branch when this version of the diff was created\"; `head_commit_sha` = \"The HEAD commit of the source branch.\"\n- Not a UI convenience: `MergeRequest#merge_request_diff_for` (app/models/merge_request.rb:1503-1518) builds a matcher on all three columns `start_commit_sha`/`head_commit_sha`/`base_commit_sha` and resolves a version server-side by that triple. The base is a persisted column, not derived at render time.\n- The reporter's own stated limits are all correct, including the ones that hurt their case: change identity is `merge_requests.iid` on a mutable branch (no Change-Id); you cannot mint a version via API; and the `clean` event (`transition any => :without_files`, app/models/merge_request_diff.rb:66-81, comment \"Diff files have been deleted by the system\") means immutability is by construction, not enforced.\n- Their INFERRED merge_head claim is CONFIRMED and I can upgrade it: `has_many :merge_request_diffs, -> { regular }` (merge_request.rb:57-58) is what the versions endpoint iterates (lib/api/merge_request_diffs.rb), so the second `merge_head` diff type is structurally invisible to the API. Note `MergeRequest#patch_id_sha` reads from `merge_head_diff` (merge_request.rb:2915) \u2014 the MR-level patch id comes from the diff the API will not show you.\n\n(e) THE MACHINE-REVIEWER API DOES WHAT IS CLAIMED. I found the endpoint docs. Draft Notes is `Tier: Free, Premium, Ultimate`; `POST .../draft_notes` accepts `position[base_sha|head_sha|start_sha|old_path|new_path|old_line|new_line|line_range|position_type]` plus `in_reply_to_discussion_id` and `resolve_discussion`; `POST .../draft_notes/bulk_publish` accepts `note`, `internal`, and `reviewer_state` \u2208 {`requested_changes`, `reviewed`}. Batched-review-with-verdict in one call is real and free.\nApprovals doc (doc/api/merge_request_approvals.md) states explicitly: \"The following endpoints are available on all tiers, including Free: Approve / Unapprove / Reset approvals / Retrieve approval state... All other endpoints require Premium or Ultimate.\" The `sha` 409 guard and the bot-only `reset_approvals` 401-for-humans rule are quoted verbatim in the docs.\n\n(a) TIER GATING IS HONESTLY REPORTED. I specifically hunted for paid-only features smuggled in as free and found none. The report correctly flags approval *rules*, code-owner rules, and instance/group settings as Premium/Ultimate, and `object_attributes.approval_rules` as EE only (webhook_events.md:982). Free approval being advisory is confirmed: \"GitLab Free allows all users with at least the Developer role to approve merge requests. These approvals are optional and don't prevent merging without approval.\"\n\n(b) NO THIRD-PARTY PLUGINS PRESENTED AS CORE. Everything cited is in gitlab-org/gitlab master. The Go client is first-party (gitlab-org namespace).\n\n(c) HEALTH CLAIMS ARE EXACT. doc/releases/19/gitlab-19-2-released/index.md front matter `date: 2026-07-16`, body \"On July 16, 2026, GitLab 19.2 was released\". doc/releases/19/gitlab-19-3-released/index.md is titled \"GitLab 19.3 (not yet released)\". 19.1 was 2026-06-18 \u2014 monthly cadence confirmed. Go client v2.56.0 tagged 2026-08-05T17:53:41Z (proxy.golang.org); xanzy/go-gitlab last release v0.115.0 at 2024-12-10T15:29:35Z. Both exactly as reported.\n\nLicense verified verbatim against root LICENSE: doc/ is CC BY-SA 4.0, ee/ and jh/ under their own licenses, \"Content outside of the above mentioned directories or restrictions above is available under the MIT Expat license.\"\n\n=== THE SIX DEFECTS ===\n\n1. **WRONG \u2014 keep-around does not pin the base.** The report's key_facts says keep-around refs mean \"revision N's base and head stay resolvable after a force-push\". The code it cites says otherwise: `enqueue_keep_around_commits` (merge_request_diff.rb:1066-1078) passes `[start_commit_sha, head_commit_sha]` \u2014 **start and head, not base**. The report's prose section gets this right (\"each version's `start_commit_sha` and `head_commit_sha`\") and then the summary line contradicts it. For JetBridge this matters directly: `base_commit_sha` is the merge-base, and it is the field that closes their \"a snapshot has a HEAD but a change has a BASE\" gap. That specific SHA has no keep-around ref. It is normally reachable from the target branch, but nothing pins it. If you build a durable revision record keyed on base, pin it yourself.\n   Also note the same doc page the report cites warns keep-around refs \"have no lifecycle, don't use them for any new functionality\" \u2014 the report quotes this but files it as reassurance; it reads more like a warning against depending on it.\n\n2. **OVERSTATED \u2014 \"never per API call\" is false.** Versions are per-push, yes (docs confirm twice). But `PUT /projects/:id/merge_requests/:merge_request_iid/rebase` (doc/api/merge_requests.md:3309-3316) rewrites `source_branch` from a pure API call, which produces a new diff version. A machine reviewer that rebases will self-trigger a new revision. This is exactly the failure mode JetBridge is fleeing from GitHub, so the parenthetical is the wrong reassurance.\n\n3. **OVERSTATED \u2014 the requested_changes \"fires no webhook\" inference is only half true.** `MergeRequests::UpdateReviewerStateService` indeed never calls `execute_hooks`. But when the reviewer had already approved, that service calls `remove_approval`, and `MergeRequests::RemoveApprovalService#trigger_approval_hooks` ends with `execute_hooks(merge_request, 'unapproved')`. So: requested_changes from a fresh reviewer = silent; requested_changes from a previously-approving reviewer = an `unapproved` MR hook fires. A loop driven off approval actions will see one and not the other. The report's operational advice (\"drive off Note Hook + approval actions\") happens to be right, but for a reason it didn't state.\n\n4. **INCOMPLETE \u2014 `webhook-signature` is opt-in, not automatic.** Reported as though every delivery carries it. webhooks.md:644 says \"Included only when a [signing token] is configured\", and :205 repeats it. GA-in-19.1 is correct (flag `webhook_signing_token` removed, issue 596374; introduced 19.0). You must configure a signing token per hook or you get no signature at all.\n\n5. **INCOMPLETE \u2014 `system_action` is narrower than presented.** Sold as the general \"machine-caused vs human\" discriminator. webhook_events.md:1100-1116 scopes it: \"These system-initiated webhook events are only triggered by push events\", the two values are both `*_reset_on_push`, and \u2014 the sentence that matters \u2014 \"**Other approval reset scenarios do not trigger webhooks.**\" It distinguishes push-triggered approval resets, not machine actions in general.\n\n6. **INCOMPLETE \u2014 the Go client has two gaps, not one.** The `PublishAllDraftNotes(pid, mergeRequest, ...RequestOptionFunc)` gap is real and confirmed (draft_notes.go:180-187 posts to `bulk_publish` with no body at all, so neither `note` nor `reviewer_state` can be sent). But the report then says version coverage \"is already there\" \u2014 the `MergeRequestDiffVersion` struct (merge_requests.go:248-259) has ID, Head/Base/StartCommitSHA, CreatedAt, MergeRequestID, State, RealSize, Commits, Diffs and **no `PatchIDSHA` field**. `patch_id_sha` is the one field that gives you content-identity across rebases, and the Go client silently drops it. Two hand-rolled calls, not one.\n\n=== MINOR NUANCES (not defects) ===\n- `Discussions::UpdateDiffPositionService` is verbatim as described, with one unmentioned condition: the `diff_discussion_outdated` system note is posted only `if outdated && current_user`. A background/system-initiated reposition posts no note.\n- `RecursionDetection` COUNT_LIMIT = 100 confirmed. Unmentioned: `block?` returns true if the hook id is *already in the chain cache at all*, not just on count overflow \u2014 so the second legitimate firing of the same hook within one chain is suppressed. It also no-ops entirely unless a request UUID was set. Useful, but it is a circuit breaker, not a semantic dedupe.\n- `Notes::PostProcessService#execute` confirmed: `unless note.system?` wraps `execute_note_hooks`. System notes are webhook-invisible. Given #3 and #5 above, a surprising amount of MR state change is only observable via polling or GraphQL subscriptions.\n\n=== ONE THING I COULD NOT VERIFY, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS MOST TO JETBRIDGE ===\nWhether you can create a discussion anchored to a **non-latest** version's SHA triple. The whole value proposition here is \"review revision 3 as an addressable object\". Every doc example (discussions.md:1119) says \"Get the *latest* merge request version\", and the known-issue note at :1114 warns that wrong base/head/start SHAs hit bug #296829. Nothing documents old-version anchoring as supported or unsupported. Test this against a real instance before committing \u2014 if it only works against latest, the revision noun is readable but not reviewable, and most of the adoption case collapses.\n\nSecond untested assumption: whether `reviewer_state` on `bulk_publish` requires the bot to already be a registered reviewer on the MR. Undocumented.",

  {
    "survey": {
      "area": "Dedicated code-review systems that are NOT forges (point-at-an-existing-git-host): Phorge/Differential, Review Board, and the lighter Gerrit-adjacent field",
      "systems": [
        {
          "name": "Phorge \u2014 Differential (+ Conduit, Herald, Harbormaster)",
          "license": "Apache-2.0. Fully open source, single edition, no paid tier, no CLA-gated features. Canonical repo is we.phorge.it; github.com/phorgeit/phorge is a read-only mirror (its own description says \"Read only mirror!\").",
          "health": "Alive, but thin and in maintenance mode. Stable tags use a YYYY.WW scheme; measured tag dates from a clone of the mirror: 2026.27 = 2026-06-30, 2025.51 = 2025-12-18, 2025.18 = 2025-05-02, 2024.35 = 2024-08-24, 2024.19 = 2024-05-10, 2023.49 = 2024-01-02. So roughly 2 stable releases per year, latest ~5 weeks old. master commit counts per calendar year: 345 (2023), 281 (2024), 600 (2025), 407 (2026 YTD; newest commit 2026-08-05). BUS FACTOR ~1: of 100 commits sampled from the last 12 months, 80 are Andre Klapper, 13 Aviv Eyal, 4 Valerio Bozzolan, 2 mainframe98, 1 other. Reading the last two years of src/applications/differential/conduit/ history, the work is PHP 8.x compatibility, nullability fixes, deprecation bookkeeping and bug fixes \u2014 there is no new feature work on the Differential or Conduit surface. Upstream Phabricator was discontinued by Phacility on 2021-06-01; secure.phabricator.com is frozen but still readable, and remains the only place several design decisions are documented. New installs are defensible if you accept that Differential's API will not grow, and that you are betting on one maintainer.",
          "revision_noun": "YES \u2014 one of the two best revision models outside Gerrit. A Differential Revision (monogram D123, plus a stable PHID) is created before any commit exists and never changes identity; it is not a ref and not a SHA. Hanging off it is an ordered series of Diffs. Each Diff is a first-class immutable object with its own integer ID, its own PHID, and its own URL /differential/diff/<id>/ (DifferentialDiff::getURI, DifferentialDiffPHIDType). `arc diff` creates a NEW Diff and attaches it as an update; existing Diffs are never rewritten. Each Diff records its own base: the storage class has a sourceControlBaseRevision column, and differential.diff.search returns it structurally as refs: [{\"type\":\"base\",\"identifier\":\"<sha>\"}, {\"type\":\"onto\",\"name\":\"<branch>\"}, {\"type\":\"branch\",...}, {\"type\":\"bookmark\",...}] (DifferentialDiff::getFieldValuesForConduit). Diff content is stored server-side as changesets + hunks, so a diff is SELF-CONTAINED \u2014 Phorge never needs a worktree, let alone two, to render or comment on a revision. Interdiff between any two revisions is addressable as /D123?vs=<oldDiffID>&id=<newDiffID>. Additionally, with a staging area configured, `arc diff` pushes each diff as a real immutable git tag refs/tags/phabricator/diff/<diffID> (DifferentialDiff::getStagingRef), so every revision is ALSO an addressable git ref your CI can just fetch. Commit\u2194revision binding is by the \"Differential Revision:\" commit-message trailer (DifferentialRevisionIDCommitMessageField) \u2014 the commit points at the change, never the reverse.",
          "machine_reviewer_api": "MIXED \u2014 verdicts and top-level comments yes; inline review comments effectively NO. Conduit is a roughly-JSON-RPC HTTP API with per-user API tokens (Settings > Conduit API Tokens) and a self-documenting web console. WHAT A BOT CAN DO: cast verdicts and comment via differential.revision.edit using modular transactions \u2014 DifferentialRevisionAcceptTransaction::TRANSACTIONTYPE = 'differential.revision.accept' (ACTIONKEY 'accept'), 'differential.revision.reject' (ACTIONKEY 'reject'), plus resign / request_review / plan-changes; and PhabricatorTransactions::TYPE_COMMENT for prose. Read everything via differential.revision.search, differential.diff.search, differential.changeset.search and transaction.search \u2014 the latter surfaces existing inline comments as transactions of type 'inline' with {diff:{id,phid}, path, line, length, replyToCommentPHID, isDone} (DifferentialRevisionInlineTransaction::getFieldValuesForConduit). WHAT A BOT CANNOT DO: publish inline comments. The only write endpoint is differential.createinline (params: revisionID, diffID, filePath, isNewFile, lineNumber, lineLength, content) and it constructs a DifferentialInlineComment and ->save()s it with no transaction \u2014 i.e. an UNSUBMITTED DRAFT visible only to the bot. It has no replyTo parameter (so no threaded replies) and no parameter for the done/resolved state. Draft inlines are converted into publishable TYPE_INLINE transactions only by PhabricatorApplicationTransactionEditor::newAutomaticInlineTransactions(), whose only callers are DifferentialRevisionEditEngine::newAutomaticCommentTransactions() and DiffusionCommitEditEngine, which in turn is called only from PhabricatorEditEngine::buildCommentResponse() \u2014 the WEB comment-form path. PhabricatorEditEngine::buildConduitResponse(), which is what differential.revision.edit executes, never calls it. The legacy escape hatch is gone: differential.createcomment's attach_inlines parameter is now declared 'unsupported' with the source comment \"The legacy attach_inlines flag is now ignored and has no effect. See T13513\", and the whole method was deprecated 04/2026 in favour of differential.revision.edit. THE DOCUMENTED SUBSTITUTE IS HARBORMASTER, and this is an explicit upstream design decision: epriestley closed T1394 (\"Support creating audits with inline comments through the API\") as Wontfix on 2015-03-27 with \"This use case is obsoleted by Harbormaster (T1049). When implemented, Harbormaster will let you report unit and lint results for commits and revisions.\" Harbormaster precisely: a Revision is a 'Buildable'; a Herald rule with the 'Run build plans' action starts a Build against it; the build plan's usual shape is 'Make HTTP Request' to your system then 'Wait for Message'; your system reports back with harbormaster.sendmessage(buildTargetPHID, type=pass|fail|work, unit=[...], lint=[...]). Lint messages carry {name, code, severity, path, line, char, description}; unit results are analogous. These render as per-line annotations on the changeset of that specific diff and gate the revision. That is a real, per-revision, line-anchored machine-reviewer channel \u2014 it just is not a comment thread and cannot be replied to or resolved.",
          "push_events": "YES \u2014 first-class, well documented, and structurally the right shape. Herald > Webhooks. Two modes: Firehose (every event) or Enabled + a Herald rule using the 'Call webhooks' action to narrow scope. Delivery is POST with a JSON body: {\"object\":{\"type\",\"phid\"}, \"triggers\":[{\"phid\"}], \"action\":{\"test\",\"silent\",\"secure\",\"epoch\"}, \"transactions\":[{\"phid\"},...]}. Authenticated by HMAC-SHA256 of the raw request body under a per-hook key, delivered in X-Phabricator-Webhook-Signature. Documented delivery semantics: retried until 2xx or garbage-collected (7 days default), 10-second timeout, single-threaded per hook, auto-paused on error bursts, EXPLICITLY may arrive out of order and may be arbitrarily delayed (compare action.epoch to now). The payload is deliberately only pointers \u2014 you resolve them with differential.revision.search (by object PHID) and transaction.search (by transaction PHIDs). This kills the mirrored-cursor problem: transaction PHIDs are stable global identities, and every transaction carries an authorPHID, so a bot filters its own writes in one predicate. There is no GitHub-style pathology where a reply becomes a new review object \u2014 in Differential everything, including a reply, is just another transaction on D123.",
          "anchoring": "Documented, aggressive forward-porting, with an equally documented admission that it is sometimes wrong. Storage anchor is exact: an inline is stored against a changesetID, i.e. a specific file inside a specific immutable Diff \u2014 so nothing ever actually goes stale at rest. Display is the aggressive part. From the Inline Comments user guide: \"When a revision is updated, we attempt to port inline comments forward and display them on the new diff. Ported comments have a pale, ghostly appearance and include a button which allows you to jump back to the original context where the comment appeared.\" The same document states the failure mode outright \u2014 \"In the general case, it is not possible to always port comments to the same lines humans would automatically\" and \"We are very aggressive about porting comments forward, even in the presence of heavy changes\" \u2014 and gives the governing rule: \"When a line of code with an inline comment on it changes, we can not know if the change addressed the inline or not. We take the cautious route and always assume it did not, and that humans need to verify problems have really been addressed.\" Users can opt out (Settings > Diff Preferences > Show Older Inlines) to keep comments pinned to their original diff. Separately, each inline has a fixedState / isDone flag that survives revisions and is exposed on read via transaction.search \u2014 but there is no Conduit method to SET it.",
          "ops_cost": "LAMP, and a second full stack for a Go shop. Documented requirements: MySQL 8.0+ or MariaDB 10.5.1+ \u2014 and note \"you will need a server with multiple databases\" (Phorge shards ~40 schemas); PHP 7.2.25+ or 8.0+ with required extensions mbstring, iconv, mysqli, curl, pcntl, posix (optional gd, zip, opcache, apcu); a web server that can run PHP (Apache + mod_php or nginx + php-fpm \u2014 the PHP builtin server is explicitly unsupported). Plus the phd daemon fleet for the task queue, webhook delivery, repository import and Harbormaster. NO official Docker image and no Helm chart \u2014 only community images (cooperspencer/phorge, Zeigren/phorge_docker, HackerspaceKRK/phorge-docker). Kubernetes support in Almanac/Drydock/Harbormaster is an open ticket (T15057), not a shipped feature. For a small team on one cluster this is PHP + MySQL + supervisord-shaped daemons with no upstream k8s story.",
          "go_client": "Third-party only, and thin. github.com/uber/gonduit (MIT) is the well-known one but was last pushed 2021-06-11 \u2014 effectively abandoned. github.com/thought-machine/gonduit (MIT) is a live fork, last pushed 2026-06-09, but has 2 stars and is single-company maintained. Its coverage happens to match this use case: requests/applicationeditor.go supplies the generic {objectIdentifier, transactions} EditRequest that drives differential.revision.edit; requests/harbormaster.go supplies HarbormasterSendMessageRequest with typed Unit/Lint result slices; plus transactionsearch.go, search.go, differential_query.go, differential_getrawdiff.go, phid_lookup.go. Conduit itself is plain HTTP POST with a JSON blob, so hand-rolling is a day's work. No gRPC, no OpenAPI spec.",
          "verdict": "partial-fit",
          "key_facts": [
            "Differential Revision D123 has a PHID and exists independent of any commit; each attached Diff is separately addressable at /differential/diff/<id>/ \u2014 https://github.com/phorgeit/phorge/blob/master/src/applications/differential/storage/DifferentialDiff.php (see getURI() and DifferentialDiffPHIDType)",
            "Each Diff names its own base. differential.diff.search returns refs including {\"type\":\"base\",\"identifier\":\"<sha>\"} and {\"type\":\"onto\",\"name\":\"<branch>\"}, sourced from the sourceControlBaseRevision column \u2014 DifferentialDiff::getFieldValuesForConduit, https://github.com/phorgeit/phorge/blob/master/src/applications/differential/storage/DifferentialDiff.php",
            "Every diff is also a real immutable git tag when a staging area is configured: getStagingRef() returns 'refs/tags/phabricator/diff/'.$this->getID() \u2014 same file; documented in the Harbormaster guide, https://github.com/phorgeit/phorge/blob/master/src/docs/user/userguide/harbormaster.diviner",
            "Inline comments created via the API are DRAFTS. differential.createinline saves a DifferentialInlineComment with no transaction and has no replyTo or done parameter \u2014 https://github.com/phorgeit/phorge/blob/master/src/applications/differential/conduit/DifferentialCreateInlineConduitAPIMethod.php",
            "INFERRED (from source reading, not from docs; corroborated by community reports): there is NO Conduit path to publish those drafts. newAutomaticInlineTransactions() is called only from EditEngine's web comment path (PhabricatorEditEngine::buildCommentResponse -> newAutomaticCommentTransactions), while differential.revision.edit runs PhabricatorEditEngine::buildConduitResponse(), which does not call it \u2014 https://github.com/phorgeit/phorge/blob/master/src/applications/transactions/editengine/PhabricatorEditEngine.php and .../editor/PhabricatorApplicationTransactionEditor.php",
            "differential.createcomment declares attach_inlines and silent as 'unsupported' (\"now ignored and has no effect. See T13513\") and the whole method was deprecated 04/2026 in favour of differential.revision.edit \u2014 https://github.com/phorgeit/phorge/blob/master/src/applications/differential/conduit/DifferentialCreateCommentConduitAPIMethod.php",
            "DOCUMENTED upstream decision that machine reviewers use Harbormaster, not inline comments: T1394 closed Wontfix 2015-03-27, \"This use case is obsoleted by Harbormaster (T1049). When implemented, Harbormaster will let you report unit and lint results for commits and revisions.\" \u2014 https://secure.phabricator.com/T1394 (T6461 was merged into it, https://secure.phabricator.com/T6461)",
            "harbormaster.sendmessage(buildTargetPHID, type=pass|fail|work, unit=[...], lint=[...]) is the machine-reviewer write surface; lint entries are {name, code, severity, path, line, char, description} \u2014 https://github.com/phorgeit/phorge/blob/master/src/applications/harbormaster/conduit/HarbormasterSendMessageConduitAPIMethod.php and .../storage/build/HarbormasterBuildLintMessage.php",
            "Webhooks: Firehose or Herald-triggered, HMAC-SHA256 in X-Phabricator-Webhook-Signature, 7-day retry, 10s timeout, explicitly out-of-order and possibly delayed; payload carries transaction PHIDs which you resolve with transaction.search \u2014 https://github.com/phorgeit/phorge/blob/master/src/docs/user/userguide/webhooks.diviner (rendered at https://we.phorge.it/book/phorge/article/webhooks/, which is behind an Anubis proof-of-work gate for bots)",
            "Comment porting is documented as intentionally aggressive and sometimes wrong; disableable per-user via Settings > Diff Preferences > Show Older Inlines \u2014 https://github.com/phorgeit/phorge/blob/master/src/docs/user/userguide/differential_inlines.diviner",
            "Harbormaster's own guide opens with \"WARNING: Harbormaster is still very rough\" and lists 'Automatic Staging Areas' \u2014 the recommended change-handoff mechanism \u2014 as \"This mechanism has not been built yet, so you can not use it.\" \u2014 https://github.com/phorgeit/phorge/blob/master/src/docs/user/userguide/harbormaster.diviner",
            "Install requirements (MySQL 8.0+/MariaDB 10.5.1+ with multiple databases, PHP 7.2.25+/8.0+, listed extensions) \u2014 https://github.com/phorgeit/phorge/blob/master/src/docs/user/installation_guide.diviner",
            "Go clients: https://github.com/uber/gonduit (MIT, last push 2021-06-11) and the live fork https://github.com/thought-machine/gonduit (MIT, last push 2026-06-09)"

        },
        {
          "name": "Review Board (Community Edition), plus Review Bot and RB Gateway",
          "license": "MIT for the server (PyPI ReviewBoard declares license: MIT; github.com/reviewboard/reviewboard is MIT). Review Bot is MIT (github.com/reviewboard/ReviewBot). RB Gateway is MIT and written in Go. Since RB 8 the product is split into three plans activated from the Admin UI: Community Edition \u2014 \"The open source Review Board... remains fully free for any size of deployment\" \u2014 plus paid Review Board Plus and Enterprise. Paid-only features are Office/PDF/Google-Docs document review, code review reports, \"user role management and automation rules\", Azure DevOps / Bitbucket Data Center / ClearCase / GitHub Enterprise / Keysight SOS integrations, database import/export, \"improved server scalability\", and support SLAs. Everything relevant to a machine-reviewer backend \u2014 the full REST API, webhooks, status updates, issue tracking, the Jenkins/CircleCI/Travis integrations, and Review Bot \u2014 is in the free Community Edition.",
          "health": "Healthy, and the only candidate here with a real commercial maintainer. Vendor: Beanbag, Inc. (Christian Hammond, David Trowbridge). PyPI release history: 8.0 on 2026-05-28, 7.0.6 on 2026-03-31, 7.0.5 on 2026-03-19, 7.0.4 on 2025-08-06, 7.0.3 on 2024-12-18, 7.0 on 2024-06-06, 6.0 on 2023-10-17 \u2014 roughly one major per year plus point releases. github.com/reviewboard/reviewboard last pushed 2026-08-05 with active release-8.x and release-9.x branches (a commit on 2026-07-31 adds \"documentation for service accounts\", i.e. bot identities are getting explicit support in 9.x \u2014 INFERRED from the commit subject, 9.x is unreleased). 1.7k stars. HONEST CAVEAT: Review Bot 4.1 (released 2026-04-07, last repo push 2026-05-07) documents support only for Review Board 5.0\u20137.0 and Python 3.8\u20133.12 \u2014 it has not caught up to RB 8.0. That matters little here because your agent replaces Review Bot's role, but it is a real lag signal for the extension ecosystem.",
          "revision_noun": "YES \u2014 the cleanest first-class model of the two, and the one that most directly dissolves your three measured problems. A Review Request has a stable numeric id at /api/review-requests/{id}/ and is created independently of any commit. Under it, /api/review-requests/{id}/diffs/{diff_revision}/ is an ordered series of Diffs: the docs say \"A diff is revisioned, and more than one can be associated with any particular review request\", and the revision field \"Starts at 1 for public diffs. Draft diffs may be at 0.\" Published diffs are immutable \u2014 the only way to change the code is to POST a new diff, which mints a new revision; the diff-list POST doc states \"The new diff will have a revision of 0\" until published. Each revision names its own base explicitly: base_commit_id = \"The ID/revision this change is built upon. If using a parent diff, then this is the base for that diff\" (added in 1.7.13), with an optional parent_diff_path for a base branch that is not yet pushed. Interdiffs between arbitrary revisions are first-class (filter comments with ?interdiff-revision=; comments carry an interfilediff pointer). Since 4.0 the request can also preserve commit structure: created_with_history, commit_count, a Diff Commit List under each diff revision, and base_filediff_id on comments for cumulative diffs. Critically for your 789 MiB problem: Review Board stores the unified diff plus base_commit_id and fetches original file content from the repository on demand \u2014 it never snapshots a worktree, let alone two.",
          "machine_reviewer_api": "STRONG, and explicitly designed for bots \u2014 this is the reason to look at Review Board. Plain REST/JSON, HATEOAS link maps, discoverable from the Root List Resource. Auth: `Authorization: token rbp_...` API tokens with per-resource/per-method API Token Policies (built-in Full access / Read-only, or a custom policy), or HTTP Basic, or OAuth2. THE BOT REVIEW FLOW: (1) POST /api/review-requests/{id}/reviews/ with body_top, body_bottom, ship_it \u2014 \"The new review will start off as private. Only the author of the review (the user who is logged in and issuing this API call) will be able to see and interact with the review\"; if a draft already exists it returns 303 and updates it. (2) POST .../reviews/{rid}/diff-comments/ with REQUIRED filediff_id, first_line, num_lines, text, plus optional interfilediff_id, base_filediff_id, issue_opened. (3) PUT .../reviews/{rid}/ with public=1 to publish the whole thing atomically \u2014 one review, one event, all comments included. VERDICT: ship_it is a boolean on the review; the review request itself exposes computed approved and approval_failure, documented as \"On a default install, a review request is approved if it has at least one Ship It! and no open issues.\" THREADED REPLIES: first-class and separate from reviews \u2014 POST .../reviews/{rid}/replies/, then POST .../replies/{reply_id}/diff-comments/ with REQUIRED reply_to_id (\"The ID of the comment being replied to\"), then publish the reply. THREAD RESOLUTION: first-class \u2014 every diff comment has issue_opened (bool) and issue_status \u2208 {open, resolved, dropped, verifying-resolved, verifying-dropped}, settable by PUT; Issue Verification lets the commenter require they sign off before an issue closes. CI/CHECK RESULTS: a separate object type, Status Updates (added 3.0; the reviews.status_updates feature is FeatureLevel.STABLE, i.e. on by default). POST /api/review-requests/{id}/status-updates/ with service_id, summary, description, url, url_text, timeout, state \u2208 {pending, done-success, done-failure, error, timed-out, not-yet-run, cancelled} \u2014 and the two fields that matter most: change (a Change Description id, i.e. the exact revision event the check ran against, \"for example, the change adding a diff that was built by CI\") and review (a Review id, so the check's badge and its inline findings are one linked object). GET supports ?change=, ?service-id=, ?state= for reconciliation.",
          "push_events": "YES \u2014 core WebHooks, and their shape directly fixes your self-trigger bug. Configured in the Admin UI with Enabled, URL, Events, and an \"Apply to\" scope limiting the hook to one repository. Payload encoding JSON / XML / Form-Data, or a custom Django-template payload; optionally HMAC-signed with a per-hook secret. Documented events and their context objects: review_request_published (event, is_new, review_request), review_request_closed (event, closed_by, close_type \u2208 submitted|discarded, review_request), review_request_reopened (event, reopened_by, review_request), review_published (event, review, diff_comments, file_attachment_comments, screenshot_comments, review_request), reply_published (event, reply, diff_comments as comment REPLIES, file_attachment_comments, screenshot_comments, review_request). Two structural properties matter for you: (a) reply_published is a DISTINCT event from review_published \u2014 an agent replying inside a thread produces a reply object, never a new review object, so the GitHub \"my own reply is filed as a new review with non-null submitted_at\" pathology does not exist here; (b) the payload carries the review/reply AND its comments inline, so there is no mirrored cursor to maintain. Note there is no webhook event for status-update creation, so a bot posting a check result does not wake itself at all. INFERRED: you must still filter review_published on review.user to ignore reviews your own bot publishes \u2014 this is a one-predicate filter, not a state machine.",
          "anchoring": "Deliberately does NOT forward-port, and documents what it does instead \u2014 which for a machine reviewer is the better contract. A diff comment is anchored to filediff_id (a specific file inside a specific immutable diff revision) + first_line + num_lines, optionally plus interfilediff_id for a comment made while viewing an interdiff, and base_filediff_id for cumulative-diff comments on history-enabled requests. That anchor can never go stale, because the object it points at is immutable and never rewritten. The documented behaviour when a new revision lands is navigational, not heuristic: \"if you... come back to discover that a new diff revision has been uploaded... a warning box will appear telling you that you have additional comments on an older revision, with a helpful link to jump back to that revision.\" Continuity across revisions is carried by ISSUES rather than by re-anchoring text: issue_status lives on the comment, survives new diff revisions, is aggregated in a per-review-request Issue Summary Table filterable by status and reviewer, can be closed or reopened by either the request owner or the filing reviewer, and feeds review_request.approved. The docs also warn about the one real diff-viewer caveat: \"if a newer diff is based on a newer revision of a file, you may see other changes made to that file between those revisions that has nothing to do with the review itself\" \u2014 i.e. keep successive revisions on the same base where you can.",
          "ops_cost": "Modest and k8s-friendly. Python/Django. Needs a database (PostgreSQL, MySQL or MariaDB), a memcached server, a local filesystem path for the site directory, and a front web server (Nginx recommended) because the container's Gunicorn deliberately does not serve static media. Official Docker images since 3.0.21: beanbag/reviewboard:latest, :X.Y, :X.Y.Z, with published docker-compose samples. Hard rule: \"If you're using multiple Review Board Docker containers, they must all use the exact same version.\" GIT-SPECIFIC CAVEAT \u2014 read this before committing: \"Git has a very limited remote protocol, which isn't capable of some of the requests Review Board and other similar tools require. Because of this, if Review Board does not have local file-based access to your main Git repository, you will need to set up a wrapping service, such as GitWeb or cgit.\" Options are therefore: mount a local clone, run GitWeb/cgit, or run their own RB Gateway (MIT, written in Go, last pushed 2026-05-26) as a small sidecar in front of your git host. Forgejo, GitLab, GitHub and Bitbucket are also supported repository types. Review Bot, if you wanted it, adds a Celery broker (RabbitMQ) plus worker pods \u2014 you would almost certainly skip it, since your agent IS the analysis tool. Realistic footprint: ~3\u20134 pods (web, db, memcached, optional rb-gateway) on one cluster. No PHP anywhere.",
          "go_client": "NONE official and none third-party worth naming \u2014 this is the one gap. The official clients are Python (RBTools ships rbtools.api). The API is, however, unusually easy to write a Go client against: stable URI templates discoverable from the Root List Resource, a single `Authorization: token <value>` header, ordinary JSON bodies, and only about six endpoints needed for a full agent loop (reviews, diff-comments, replies + reply diff-comments, status-updates, diffs, review-requests) plus HMAC verification on the webhook receiver. Note reviewboard/rb-gateway is itself Go and MIT, so Go is already in the ecosystem \u2014 just not as a client library.",
          "verdict": "strong-fit",
          "key_facts": [
            "Diff revisions are immutable, ordered and individually addressable at /api/review-requests/{id}/diffs/{diff_revision}/, and each records base_commit_id, \"The ID/revision this change is built upon\" \u2014 https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/diff/ and https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/diff-list/",
            "Status Updates are the purpose-built machine-reviewer object: state \u2208 {pending, done-success, done-failure, error, timed-out, not-yet-run, cancelled}, plus change (the specific Change Description / revision event) and review (a linked Review) \u2014 https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/status-update/ and https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/status-update-list/",
            "Status updates are on by default: StatusUpdatesFeature has feature_id 'reviews.status_updates' and level = FeatureLevel.STABLE \u2014 https://github.com/reviewboard/reviewboard/blob/master/reviewboard/reviews/features.py",
            "A bot posts a review as: POST reviews/ (private draft, ship_it optional) -> POST reviews/{id}/diff-comments/ with required filediff_id, first_line, num_lines, text (optional interfilediff_id, base_filediff_id, issue_opened) -> PUT reviews/{id}/ public=1 \u2014 https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/review-list/ and https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/review-diff-comment-list/",
            "Threaded replies are a distinct resource with a required reply_to_id pointing at the parent comment \u2014 https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/review-reply/ and https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/review-reply-diff-comment-list/",
            "Thread resolution is machine-settable: issue_opened plus issue_status \u2208 {open, resolved, dropped, verifying-resolved, verifying-dropped} on every diff comment \u2014 https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/review-diff-comment/ ; user-facing semantics incl. Issue Verification at https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/users/reviews/issue-tracking/",
            "review_request.approved / approval_failure give a single machine-readable gate: \"approved if it has at least one Ship It! and no open issues\" \u2014 https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/review-request/",
            "Webhooks: five events including review_published and the SEPARATE reply_published, per-repository scoping, JSON/XML/Form-Data or custom Django-template payload, optional HMAC secret \u2014 https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/admin/configuration/webhooks/ and the resource at https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/web-hook/",
            "Comments stay anchored to the revision they were made on; the documented UX for a new revision is a \"warning box... with a helpful link to jump back to that revision\" \u2014 https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/users/reviews/reviewing-diffs/",
            "Automated Code Review is a documented product concept since 3.0, presented as \"Checks run (also known as status updates)\", with bundled Jenkins/CircleCI/Travis integrations and the explicit note that \"Custom automated review tools can also be developed entirely in-house\" \u2014 https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/users/reviews/automated-review/",
            "Auth is token-based with per-resource policies: `Authorization: token rbp_...` \u2014 https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/authenticating/",
            "Official Docker images (beanbag/reviewboard) since 3.0.21, requiring a DB, memcached and a front web server \u2014 https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/admin/installation/docker/",
            "Git repositories need local file access or a wrapping service (GitWeb, cgit, or RB Gateway) \u2014 https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/admin/configuration/repositories/git/ ; RB Gateway is Go/MIT at https://github.com/reviewboard/rb-gateway",
            "Licensing and plan split: Community Edition \"remains fully free for any size of deployment\"; Plus/Enterprise add document review, reports, role management/automation rules, some integrations and scalability \u2014 https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/admin/installation/choosing-a-plan/",
            "Release cadence from PyPI: 8.0 (2026-05-28), 7.0.6 (2026-03-31), 7.0 (2024-06-06), 6.0 (2023-10-17) \u2014 https://pypi.org/project/ReviewBoard/#history ; Review Bot 4.1 supports only RB 5.0\u20137.0 \u2014 https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/reviewbot/latest/"
          ]

          "name": "Patchwork (getpatchwork/patchwork)",
          "license": "GPL-2.0",
          "health": "Maintained and real: latest tag v3.2.1, repo last pushed 2026-08-01, ~315 stars. Community-maintained (the ozlabs / Linux-kernel-adjacent world; it is what patchwork.kernel.org and patchwork.ozlabs.org run).",
          "revision_noun": "PARTIAL, and not in the way you need. It models Series (a patch set, with an optional cover letter) and Patch, both with stable integer ids and REST URLs. But a \"v2\" posting arrives as a NEW mail thread and becomes a NEW series with a new id; Patchwork does not model \"revision 2 of change X\" as a first-class ordered relation \u2014 linkage between versions is by convention and heuristics on subject/submitter. No base-commit field on a series in the general case. The immutability is real (a patch is a frozen mail), but the change identity is not.",
          "machine_reviewer_api": "WEAK. REST API v1.4 at /api/1.4/ exposing bundles, covers, events, patches, people, projects, series, users. A bot CAN create checks: POST /api/patches/{patch_id}/checks/ with state/context/target_url/description \u2014 that is a genuine CI-result surface. A bot CANNOT post review comments: /api/patches/{id}/comments and /api/covers/{id}/comments are GET-only in the OpenAPI schema (comments_list / comment read; no create operation). Review discussion happens by replying to the mailing list, so a machine reviewer would have to send email. No inline line anchors, no thread resolution beyond a per-comment 'addressed' flag and per-patch state/delegate fields. The API can be disabled entirely by the administrator.",
          "push_events": "NO. There is an /api/{v}/events/ collection you POLL \u2014 precisely the mirrored-cursor pattern you are trying to escape. INFERRED (from the published OpenAPI schema having no webhook paths): no webhook support in 3.2.x.",
          "anchoring": "N/A \u2014 there are no line-anchored comments to anchor.",
          "ops_cost": "Python/Django + PostgreSQL or MySQL, plus a mail ingestion path (parsemail/procmail hooked to a list). Cheap to run; the mail plumbing is the real operational cost and the real cultural cost.",
          "go_client": "NONE. Plain REST with an OpenAPI 3 schema published in-repo (docs/api/schemas/latest/patchwork.yaml), so codegen is trivial \u2014 but there is little worth generating for this use case.",

          "license": "Apache-2.0",
          "health": "Dormant. github.com/google/git-appraise has 5.3k stars and is NOT archived, but was last pushed 2023-08-12 and has no releases. There is no server, no hosted component and no maintainer activity to speak of. Treat it as prior art, not as a dependency.",
          "revision_noun": "PARTIAL and interesting as PRIOR ART. Reviews are stored distributedly in git refs (refs/notes/devtools/reviews, /discuss, /ci, /analyses) rather than in a database. So the anchor is exact and the history is append-only \u2014 the same property you want. But there is no stable change identity separate from the commit chain, no numbered revision series, and no server to talk to.",
