      "notes": "HEADLINE: in the non-forge review-tool category there is exactly one system that already models everything you listed \u2014 Review Board Community Edition (MIT, 8.0, released 2026-05-28). Phorge has an arguably nicer revision model but a documented dead end on the specific capability you need most.\n\nTHE ONE-PARAGRAPH COMPARISON. Both Phorge and Review Board give you a real change object with a stable id, an ordered series of immutable individually-addressable revisions, and each revision naming its own base \u2014 so both dissolve your \"a snapshot has a HEAD but a change has a BASE\" problem, and both store diffs rather than trees so neither needs your 789 MiB double-worktree seal. They diverge on the machine-reviewer write path. Review Board's is complete and purpose-built: draft review -> line-anchored diff comments (filediff_id + first_line + num_lines) -> atomic publish, ship_it as the verdict, first-class threaded replies with a required reply_to_id, issue_status as an explicit resolution state machine, and Status Updates as a separate CI/check object that links BOTH to a specific change description AND to a review. Phorge's is a dead end: differential.createinline writes a draft that no Conduit method can publish, has no replyTo parameter and no way to set the done state \u2014 and upstream closed the request for this as Wontfix in 2015 (T1394) on the grounds that machine reviewers should use Harbormaster lint/unit results instead.\n\nWHAT DOES NOT EXIST, stated plainly:\n- There is NO way for a bot to publish an inline comment on a Phorge Differential revision via the API. Not deprecated, not awkward \u2014 absent. The only sanctioned machine annotation channel is harbormaster.sendmessage lint/unit results, which do anchor to path+line on a specific diff and do gate the revision, but are not comments: they cannot be replied to, cannot be resolved, and do not participate in the review conversation. My claim that no Conduit publish path exists is INFERRED from reading PhabricatorEditEngine::buildConduitResponse() versus buildCommentResponse() (only the latter reaches newAutomaticInlineTransactions), not from documentation; it is corroborated by community reports and by the fact that the legacy attach_inlines escape hatch was explicitly neutered per T13513. If Phorge is otherwise attractive, VERIFY THIS EMPIRICALLY against a scratch install before designing around it \u2014 and note the fix, if you wanted to carry a patch, is small and local: teach the Conduit edit path to call newAutomaticCommentTransactions().\n- There is NO Go client for Review Board, official or third-party. The API is trivial to wrap (six endpoints plus HMAC webhook verification); their own RB Gateway is Go/MIT, so Go is already in the ecosystem.\n- There is NO maintained, well-adopted Go Conduit client either. uber/gonduit is MIT but last pushed 2021-06-11; thought-machine/gonduit is alive (2026-06-09) but has 2 stars and one corporate maintainer. Its coverage does happen to include the two calls you would need (generic transaction edit, harbormaster.sendmessage with typed lint/unit).\n- Phorge has NO official Docker image and NO Helm chart. Kubernetes support inside Almanac/Drydock/Harbormaster is an open ticket (T15057), not a feature.\n- Neither system has an event for \"a bot posted a check result\", which is good: posting a status update (RB) or a Harbormaster message (Phorge) does not wake your own listener.\n\nON YOUR SPECIFIC GITHUB FAILURES, mapped:\n1. \"The agent's reply is filed under a NEW review object, so we self-trigger.\" Review Board makes reply_published a DISTINCT webhook event from review_published, and a reply is a distinct resource tied to exactly one parent review with each reply-comment tied to a parent comment \u2014 the pathology cannot occur. Phorge has no review object at all; everything is a transaction on D123 carrying an authorPHID, so filtering your own writes is one predicate. Both are strictly better than GitHub here. INFERRED but obvious: you still filter review_published (RB) or transaction author (Phorge) on your bot's user id.\n2. \"Comment anchors go stale with no principled forwarding.\" The two systems take opposite, both-documented positions. Phorge forwards aggressively and says in its own user guide that this is sometimes wrong and that the rule is \"always assume the comment was not addressed\"; the durable storage anchor is nevertheless exact (changesetID = file within an immutable diff), and porting can be disabled per-user. Review Board never forwards: the anchor is filediff_id + line inside an immutable diff revision, and the documented behaviour on a new revision is a banner linking back to the older revision. Review Board then carries continuity through ISSUES (issue_status open/resolved/dropped/verifying-*, aggregated in an Issue Summary Table, feeding review_request.approved) rather than through re-anchoring text. For an agent, Review Board's answer is the better contract \u2014 the anchor is verifiable and \"is this still a problem?\" is explicit state rather than a heuristic.\n3. \"We seal two worktrees per observation.\" Neither system needs a worktree from you. Review Board takes a unified diff plus base_commit_id and fetches original file content from the repository itself (which does mean it needs local clone access, GitWeb/cgit, or RB Gateway in front of your git host \u2014 plan for that pod). Phorge stores changesets and hunks server-side and additionally, with a staging area configured, pushes each diff as an immutable git tag refs/tags/phabricator/diff/<diffID> that your CI can just fetch \u2014 that tag is arguably the single most useful primitive in either system for a CI platform, since it turns \"revision 3 of this change\" into a real fetchable ref.\n4. \"Mirror state grew to a 45-column table.\" Both webhook payloads carry stable identities you can dereference on demand instead of mirroring: Phorge sends transaction PHIDs you resolve with transaction.search; Review Board sends the review/reply plus its comments inline. Phorge's webhook doc is unusually honest about delivery semantics you must design for \u2014 retries for 7 days, 10s timeout, single-threaded per hook, and explicitly possibly out-of-order and possibly arbitrarily delayed (compare action.epoch to now). Review Board's webhook doc does not make equivalent ordering guarantees either way; INFERRED \u2014 assume at-least-once and unordered there too and reconcile against the API.\n\nPROJECT HEALTH, honestly:\n- Review Board: commercially backed by Beanbag, Inc. (Christian Hammond, David Trowbridge), ~1 major per year, 8.0 on 2026-05-28, repo pushed 2026-08-05 with live 8.x and 9.x branches. The MIT core is genuinely free for any deployment size; the paid Plus/Enterprise tiers do not gate anything you need. Two caveats: Review Bot 4.1 still documents support only for RB 5.0\u20137.0, and \"user role management and automation rules\" is a paid feature (you would implement your policy in your own platform anyway).\n- Phorge: alive but effectively one maintainer. 407 master commits in 2026 YTD and 600 in 2025 sounds healthy until you see that 80 of 100 sampled commits in the last year are by a single person, and that essentially all of it is PHP 8.x compatibility and bug fixing. Two stable tags a year (2026.27 on 2026-06-30). New installs are advisable only if you accept a frozen API surface and a LAMP stack with no upstream container story. Its own Harbormaster guide still says \"Harbormaster is still very rough\" and lists the recommended change-handoff mechanism, automatic staging areas, as \"This mechanism has not been built yet, so you can not use it.\"\n\nALSO CONSIDERED, briefly, and why they lose: Patchwork (GPL-2.0, v3.2.1, active) has real series/patch objects and a checks API a bot can write to, but comments are GET-only over the API and the review medium is email \u2014 plus its event stream is a polled /events/ collection, i.e. exactly the mirrored cursor you are escaping. git-appraise (Apache-2.0, 5.3k stars, Go) is the most intellectually relevant prior art \u2014 immutable content-addressed comments in refs/notes/devtools anchored to (commit, file, line), plus separate /ci and /analyses namespaces for machine output \u2014 but it has no server, no API, no push, and was last touched 2023-08-12; read it, do not adopt it. Gitiles is a read-only repository browser and not a review system at all, despite living next to Gerrit. Atlassian Crucible is closed source, new sales ended 2025-05-13, and support ends 2028-05-15. Radicle (heartwood, actively developed, mirror pushed 2026-08-06) does model patches as collaborative objects with revisions, but it is a peer-to-peer forge rather than something you point at an existing git host, and I did not verify its revision/base semantics this session \u2014 out of scope for this question and better assessed alongside the forges.\n\nRECOMMENDATION IF YOU WANT ONE: an external agent platform can absolutely drive Review Board as its review backend today, over documented public REST, with no forked code and no mirror table \u2014 and the pieces it needs (immutable addressable revision with a named base, line-anchored bot comments, threaded replies, machine-settable resolution state, a check object linked to a specific revision event, and a push event that distinguishes a reply from a review) all exist as first-class documented resources. Phorge can be driven for verdicts, prose comments, revision/diff reads and Harbormaster lint/unit annotations \u2014 that is a coherent product, and the staging-tag-per-diff primitive is excellent \u2014 but not for inline review conversation, and closing that gap means carrying a patch against a single-maintainer PHP codebase."
    },
    "checks": [
      {
        "system": "Phorge \u2014 Differential (+ Conduit, Herald, Harbormaster)",
        "claims_checked": [
          {
            "claim": "License is Apache-2.0, single edition, no paid tier, no CLA-gated features",
            "status": "CONFIRMED",
            "evidence": "Clone of github.com/phorgeit/phorge: LICENSE is verbatim 'Apache License Version 2.0, January 2004'. No edition/tier gating exists anywhere in the tree. README: 'Phorge is a community-maintained fork of Phabricator', 'developed and maintained by The Phorge Team (https://phorge.it)'. No enterprise/premium build path found."
          },
          {
            "claim": "Conduit, Herald and Harbormaster are core, not bundled plugins or third-party",

        "system": "Review Board (Community Edition) 8.0 + Review Bot 4.1 + RB Gateway 2.0 \u2014 ADVERSARIAL VERIFICATION RESULT\n\nBOTTOM LINE: the report is unusually accurate. I could not refute its core structural claims, and on two points the documentation is *stronger* than what was reported. But four claims are materially overstated, one is imprecise, and the report omits two gaps that bear directly on JetBridge's loop.\n\nWHAT SURVIVED SCRUTINY (verified against primary docs and source, not inferred)\n\n- Revision noun is real, not UI sugar. I found stronger evidence than the report cited: the Diff Resource exposes only GET and PUT, and the PUT doc says \"This is used solely for updating extra data on a diff. The contents of a diff cannot be modified.\" There is no DELETE method. Combined with \"Every public revision of a diff that was posted is available for review\" (reviewing-diffs), published revisions are immutable, undeletable and individually addressable at /api/review-requests/{id}/diffs/{n}/. This is a storage-level fact, not a UI convenience.\n- The full machine-reviewer flow is documented endpoint-by-endpoint, verbatim as reported: POST reviews/ (private draft, 201 or 303-if-draft-exists), POST reviews/{id}/diff-comments/ (filediff_id/first_line/num_lines/text required; interfilediff_id, base_filediff_id, issue_opened optional), PUT reviews/{id}/ public=1. Replies are a genuinely separate resource with a REQUIRED reply_to_id. issue_status \u2208 {open, resolved, dropped, verifying-resolved, verifying-dropped}. approved/approval_failure quote is verbatim. Auth header format including the rbp_ prefix example is verbatim.\n- Status Updates are real and on by default: reviewboard/reviews/features.py StatusUpdatesFeature feature_id='reviews.status_updates', level=FeatureLevel.STABLE. change_id and review_id are documented POST params.\n- Webhooks: exactly five events, per-repository \"Apply to\" scoping, JSON/XML/Form-Data or Django-template payload, optional HMAC. I verified in source (reviewboard/notifications/webhooks.py) that the default payload really does inline the comments (_serialize_review includes diff_comments, file_attachment_comments, screenshot_comments, and \u2014 undocumented in the events table \u2014 general_comments). No mirrored cursor needed.\n- Plan split is exactly as reported. RB 8.0 release notes and choosing-a-plan list the paid features verbatim; CE \"remains fully free for any size of deployment.\" I also checked the OSS repo's new reviewboard/licensing/ module (added 8.0): it is license-*management* plumbing (LicenseStatus, providers, admin UI), not a gate on core review features. Paid features live in separate proprietary packages.\n- Health: master last pushed 2026-08-05, active release-8.x and release-9.x branches, 1.7k stars, MIT (pyproject `license = { text = 'MIT' }`; the license file is COPYING, not LICENSE). Docker tag beanbag/reviewboard:8.0 published 2026-05-28. Review Bot 4.1 genuinely does say \"Review Bot 4.1 supports Review Board 5.0-7.0, and Python 3.8-3.12\" \u2014 the lag caveat is honest. rb-gateway last commit 2026-05-26, Go, MIT.\n- The \"service accounts\" inference the reporter flagged was CORRECT and I confirmed it from the commit body: service accounts are \"special managed users used for automations and integrations,\" registered via ServiceAccountHook, and notably they do NOT consume a license seat. But this is unreleased 9.x, requires writing a Python extension, and is not on the published docs site.\n\nWHERE THE REPORT IS WRONG OR OVERSTATED (four items)\n\n1. \"Each revision names its own base explicitly\" \u2014 OVERSTATED, and this is the one that matters most to JetBridge. base_commit_id is OPTIONAL, client-supplied and unvalidated: \"This may not be provided for all diffs or repository types, depending on how the diff was uploaded.\" The canonical Diff Resource example payload literally shows \"base_commit_id\": null. Worse, the review request's `branch` field is documented as \"a free-form field that can store any text\" \u2014 Review Board has no validated target-branch or merge-base concept. So the revision *object* is immutable and addressable (true), but its recorded base is a string the client promises to fill in correctly. That is better than GitHub's nothing, but it is not a system-enforced base.\n\n2. \"It never snapshots a worktree\" \u2014 TRUE but conditional in a way that undercuts the 789 MiB fix. Review Board fetches unpatched file content from the repository on demand (Original File Resource, /diffs/{rev}/files/{id}/original-file/). That means the base commit must be reachable by the Review Board server. An agent's unpushed worktree base does not work; you must either push the base or ship a parent diff. The report mentions parent_diff_path but presents \"no snapshot\" as unconditional.\n\n3. \"A bot posting a check result does not wake itself at all\" \u2014 OVERSTATED. Correct that there is no status-update webhook event (I confirmed only five signals are connected in webhooks.py). But the documented and intended pattern links a Review to the status update via review_id, and publishing that Review fires review_published. Only a *bare* status update with no linked review is silent. So the self-trigger filter is still needed on the exact path that carries the bot's findings.\n\n4. \"An agent replying inside a thread produces a reply object, never a new review object\" \u2014 imprecise. At the API and webhook layer the claim holds, and I confirmed the mechanism: Review.publish() sends reply_published if is_reply() else review_published, mutually exclusive. But a reply IS a Review row in the database (base_reply_to FK; the signal's sender is Review). The GitHub pathology is genuinely absent, but not for the reason stated.\n\nMinor: PUT on a status update accepts one extra state the report omits, `request-run`. PyPI 7.0.5 (2026-03-19) is YANKED \u2014 the report lists it as a normal release. RB 8.0's own release notes date the release May 27, 2026 (2026-05-28 is the PyPI upload).\n\nOPERATIONAL COST IS UNDERSTATED\n\n\"Mount a local clone\" is not free: the docs require the clone contain \"the very latest changes,\" suggesting \"a cron job pull the latest changes at least once a minute.\" And the Docker doc requires a DB, memcached, a front web server (Gunicorn deliberately serves no static media), AND \"a local file system path to store the Review Board site directory (which will contain the static media files to serve)\" \u2014 in Kubernetes that is a ReadWriteMany volume shared between the app pod and the nginx pod. I found no official Helm chart. \"~3-4 pods\" is directionally right but the shared-PVC requirement is the real friction. On the plus side, RB 8.0 added CIDR blocks for /health/ specifically citing Kubernetes.\n\nTWO OMISSIONS THE REPORT SHOULD HAVE FLAGGED\n\n- Review Board does not merge or land anything. It has no merge API, no branch protection, no \"required checks block merge.\" approved/approval_failure is an advisory boolean you must enforce yourself; RB Gateway 2.0 can \"auto-close review requests on push,\" which is closure-after-the-fact, not gating. JetBridge's loop ends in a merge; that half stays theirs to build.\n- \"User role management and automation rules\" is Plus/Enterprise ($12/user/month annual). If they want role-scoped bot policy or declarative approval automation rather than hand-rolled logic, that is behind the paywall. The CE substitutes are Django permission groups and API token policies.\n\nREVISED VERDICT: still strong-fit, for the reason given \u2014 but adopt it knowing (a) the base is a client-supplied string, not an enforced pointer, (b) bases must be pushed somewhere RB can read, (c) you still need the self-review filter, and (d) merge remains yours to build.",

Additionally the Review Request `branch` field is documented as \"a free-form field that can store any text\" \u2014 there is no validated target-branch/merge-base. https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/diff/ , https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/review-request/"

Diff-comment POST lists filediff_id, first_line, num_lines, text as Required; base_filediff_id, interfilediff_id, issue_opened optional; \"The review must be a draft review.\" https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/review-list/ , https://www.reviewboard.org/docs/manual/latest/webapi/2.0/resources/review-diff-comment-list/"

    "survey": {
      "area": "Git-native and email-native review models (Radicle, Tangled, SourceHut, kernel/b4/public-inbox, Jujutsu, Sapling/ReviewStack, git-appraise)",
      "systems": [
        {
          "name": "Radicle (Heartwood) \u2014 radicle-node + rad CLI + Collaborative Objects",
          "license": "MIT OR Apache-2.0 (dual). heartwood ships LICENSE-APACHE and LICENSE-MIT; crates.io metadata is \"MIT OR Apache-2.0\". GitHub mirror radicle-dev/heartwood reports Apache-2.0.",
          "health": "HEALTHY, fastest-moving of everything surveyed. Latest release 1.10.0 \"Sunflower\" on 2026-08-05 (one day before this research) containing 143 commits by 16 contributors. 2026 cadence: 1.6.0 Jan 14, 1.7.0 Mar 18, 1.7.1 Mar 20, 1.8.0 Mar 30, 1.9.0 May 19, 1.9.1 May 22, 1.10.0 Aug 5 \u2014 roughly every 4-8 weeks. GitHub mirror radicle-dev/heartwood pushed_at 2026-08-06T08:41Z, 255 stars, not archived, open_issues 0 (issues live in Radicle itself, not GitHub). 17 distinct committer emails in the last 200 commits; dominant maintainers lorenz.leutgeb@radicle.dev, fintan.halpenny@gmail.com, adrian.duke@gmail.com \u2014 i.e. a paid core team (Radworks) plus contributors. RISK: 1.10.0 announces that Radicle 2.0 development has started; refs/heads/master is now Radicle 2 and refs/heads/releases/1 is maintenance-only. A breaking major is in flight. RISK: project moved radicle.xyz -> radicle.dev on 2026-04-23; radicle.dev returns HTTP 403 to naive fetchers (needs a browser User-Agent), which breaks link-checking tooling. Adoption is real but small: the release notes say the team \"scanned the network\" and found \"several thousand repositories\" \u2014 that is the whole network, not per-project.",
          "revision_noun": "YES \u2014 this is the only system surveyed that models exactly what you described, and it models it better than Gerrit-style patchsets in one respect (each revision names its own base). DOCUMENTED in crates/radicle/src/cob/patch.rs: `pub struct Revision { id: RevisionId, author: Author, description: NonEmpty<Edit>, base: git::Oid /* \"Base branch commit, used as a merge base\" */, oid: git::Oid /* \"Reference to the Git object containing the code (revision head)\" */, discussion: Thread<Comment<CodeLocation>>, reviews: BTreeMap<ActorId, Review>, timestamp: Timestamp, resolves: BTreeSet<(EntryId, CommentId)> /* \"Review comments resolved by this revision\" */ }`. A RevisionId is an EntryId \u2014 the git OID of the COB operation commit that created it, so it is content-addressed and immutable by construction. The user guide states: \"Patch versions, called revisions in Radicle, are immutable. Therefore, each update to a patch creates a new revision, and patch updates are non-destructive, even when you force-push.\" and \"All patches start with an initial revision. This revision has the same identifier as the patch itself.\" The patch object id is stable and independent of any commit; the revision id is separate from the code head (\"you see two identifiers for the revision, the first one is the hash of the revision, and the second is the hash of the code itself\"). `base` is computed by the remote helper as `stored.merge_base(&target, head)` (crates/radicle-remote-helper/src/push.rs:508) \u2014 so BASE is first-class, which is precisely the thing you had to seal two worktrees to reconstruct. Anyone (not just the author) can push a new revision: \"Although Calyx isn't the author of the patch he can propose a new revision. In fact, anyone can do that.\"",
          "machine_reviewer_api": "MIXED \u2014 the data model is ideal, the transport is awkward. THE CRITICAL FACT: the official HTTP API is READ-ONLY. Radicle's own seeder guide states the HTTP Daemon \"is configured to have direct read-only access to the node's storage and database\" and \"For seed nodes, the HTTP Daemon is always configured as a read-only service over the node's state.\" Write endpoints were deliberately removed upstream: the community fork cytechmobile/radicle-http-api README says verbatim \"This is a temporary fork of the original `radicle-httpd` API that allows us to keep `Create/Update/Delete` that was removed from the original API. We still need these to support: machine-to-machine communication, such as integration with other 3rd party systems (e.g. CI engines that would typically want to _push_ updates to Radicle, once a job has completed)\" \u2014 and that fork is stale (last push 2025-03-11), zero stars, and self-describes as \"**not** part of the Radicle core stack. Please treat as a community project.\" radicle-httpd on crates.io is stuck at 0.10.0 published 2024-06-05. SO: the real machine write path is local, via the `rad` binary against node storage. It is genuinely complete. (a) Typed CLI: `rad patch review <patch-id> --revision <rev-id> --accept|--reject -m <msg>`; `rad patch comment <REVISION_ID> -m <msg> [--reply-to <COMMENT_ID>]`; `rad patch resolve <patch-id> --review <REVIEW_ID> --comment <COMMENT_ID> [--unresolve]`; `rad patch update`, `label`, `assign`, `edit`, `redact`, `react`, `archive`, `ready`. (b) THE ESCAPE HATCH THAT MATTERS: `rad cob update --repo <RID> --type xyz.radicle.patch --object <patch-id> -m <msg> actions.jsonl` deserializes arbitrary `cob::patch::Action` values from a JSONL file (crates/radicle-cli/src/commands/cob.rs:161-184, read_jsonl_actions at :347). The full serde-tagged action vocabulary is: `edit`, `label`, `lifecycle`, `assign`, `merge`, `review` {revision, summary, verdict, labels}, `review.redact`, `review.comment` {review, body, location, reply_to, embeds}, `review.comment.edit`, `review.comment.redact`, `review.comment.react`, `review.comment.resolve`, `review.comment.unresolve`, `review.react`, `review.edit` / `review.edit.v2`, `revision` {description, base, oid, resolves}, `revision.edit`, `revision.react`, `revision.redact`, `revision.comment` {revision, location, body, reply_to, embeds}, `revision.comment.edit/redact/react`. Verdict is `{\"Accept\"|\"Reject\"}`. This is a bot-complete API: post a review with a verdict on a NAMED revision, post inline code comments, thread replies, resolve threads. (c) Read side: `rad cob show --format json` (JSON is the DEFAULT format) and `rad cob log --format json --from <oid> --until <oid>` give the raw operation stream, cursored by git OIDs. GAP WORTH KNOWING: the typed `rad patch comment` CLI does NOT expose `--location`, so inline code comments via the friendly CLI are only reachable through the interactive hunk-by-hunk TUI, which the argument parser itself marks \"This operation is obsolete\". Inline comments from a bot must go through `rad cob update` JSONL. GAP: no native CI/check COB in core yet. radicle-ci-broker (v0.30.0, published 2026-07-09, MIT OR Apache-2.0, maintained by Lars Wirzenius) currently writes patch COMMENTS; a first-class \"Checks\" COB is designed but not shipped in 1.10.0 \u2014 you would define your own COB type. Radicle explicitly supports this: \"if a user or organization were to define a new COB type under their domain, it might look something like com.acme.task\" and `rad cob create/update --type <TYPENAME>` handles arbitrary external types. STABILITY WARNING, documented: heartwood VERSIONING.md says \"Unless clearly stated in the documentation, none of the user-facing commands should be considered stable APIs, and may therefore break in <minor> or <major> version updates.\"",
          "push_events": "YES, and it is a genuinely good fit for your \"no mirrored cursor\" requirement. `rad node events` connects to the node's Unix control socket and emits newline-delimited JSON indefinitely (crates/radicle-cli/src/commands/node/events.rs: `node.subscribe(timeout)` then `serde_json::to_string(&event)` per event; args say \"This command will connect to the node and print events to standard output as they occur. If no timeout or count is specified, it will run indefinitely.\"). Event enum (crates/radicle/src/node/events.rs): RefsFetched { remote: NodeId, rid: RepoId, updated: Vec<RefUpdate> }, RefsSynced, SeedDiscovered, SeedDropped, PeerConnected, ... RefUpdate is Updated{name, old, new} | Created{name, oid} | Deleted{name, oid} | Skipped{name, oid} \u2014 so every event carries the OLD and NEW git OID of each changed ref. Because COBs live at refs/cobs/<typename>/<object-id>, a patch COB change arrives as a ref update whose old/new OIDs bound exactly the operations you have not seen. You do not maintain a cursor \u2014 the previous ref OID IS the cursor, and you replay with `rad cob log --from <old> --until <new> --format json`. That is a structurally better answer than GitHub webhooks. INFERRED (not directly confirmed in source): the ref names in RefsFetched are namespaced as refs/namespaces/<nid>/refs/cobs/... rather than bare refs/cobs/...; verify empirically before writing a parser. Also note events fire on FETCH from a remote peer \u2014 for a single-node deployment where the agent and reviewer share one node, local writes may not generate RefsFetched; there is also a local `rad inbox` notifications database. Verify which one your topology needs.",
          "anchoring": "BEST ANSWER OF ANY SYSTEM SURVEYED, and it is principled rather than heuristic. Comments carry `CodeLocation { commit: Oid, path: PathBuf, old: Option<CodeRange>, new: Option<CodeRange> }` where CodeRange is `Lines{range}` or `Chars{line, range}` (crates/radicle/src/cob/common.rs:470-489). The anchor is pinned to a SPECIFIC COMMIT OID, so it never goes stale \u2014 it permanently means what it meant. Radicle then solves forwarding declaratively instead of by re-diffing: a new revision explicitly carries `resolves: BTreeSet<(EntryId, CommentId)>` \u2014 \"Review comments resolved by this revision.\" The author (or your agent) states which prior comments this revision addresses, rather than the server guessing. Combined with per-revision immutability, the documented workflow is: don't forward anchors, compare revisions. User guide: \"The immutability of patch revisions makes code review a breeze. It's easy to know what you've reviewed and you can quickly tell what has changed since your last review by comparing patch revisions.\" `rad patch diff <patch-id> --revision <rev-id>` diffs any named revision. Threads also have explicit `reply_to: Option<CommentId>` and separate `review.comment.resolve` / `review.comment.unresolve` actions, so \"resolved\" is an explicit, addressable, reversible state \u2014 not an inference.",
          "ops_cost": "LOWEST of everything surveyed, by a wide margin, and a good fit for one small k8s cluster. Two static Rust binaries: `radicle-node` (p2p, TCP 8776) and optionally `radicle-httpd` (read-only JSON gateway). NO external database \u2014 sqlite is a vendored/bundled dependency (Cargo.toml: `sqlite = \"0.37.0\"`, crates/radicle/Cargo.toml: `sqlite = { workspace = true, features = [\"bundled\"] }`); storage is git plus embedded SQLite indexes under the Radicle home dir. Optional web UI is radicle-explorer, a static SPA served by nginx that talks to the read-only httpd; a docker-compose.yaml is provided upstream and there is a one-click Railway seed template. NixOS packaging exists. Private repositories are a documented protocol feature. Cost of ownership is therefore roughly: one StatefulSet, one PVC, one nginx sidecar. The real cost is not ops \u2014 it is that all writes are LOCAL to a node, so your reviewer bot must run co-located with (or exec into) a node that holds the repo and a signing key, rather than calling an HTTP endpoint from anywhere.",
          "go_client": "NONE. The entire stack is Rust (heartwood workspace: radicle, radicle-cli, radicle-cob, radicle-core, radicle-node, radicle-protocol, radicle-fetch, radicle-surf, ...). radicle-httpd is Rust. There is no Go SDK, no Go COB library, and no gRPC. Your three realistic Go options, in increasing order of coupling: (1) shell out to `rad` \u2014 reads via `rad cob show/log --format json` (JSON is the default), writes via `rad cob update ... actions.jsonl` with a Go-side struct set mirroring the Action enum. This is the pragmatic choice and is well-typed on both ends because everything is serde-tagged JSON. (2) Speak the node control socket directly \u2014 the protocol is JSON over a Unix socket and heartwood ships a `radicle-schemars` binary that emits JSON Schema for `radicle::node::Command`, `radicle::node::CommandResult`, and `radicle::profile::Config` (crates/radicle-schemars/src/main.rs), so you could codegen Go types. Note this covers node control, NOT COB actions \u2014 no schema is generated for cob::patch::Action. (3) Reimplement COB reduction in Go over refs/cobs/** with go-git. Do not do (3): the CRDT reduction (topological order over the Entry DAG, with a merge function for concurrent branches) is exactly the part that is subtle, and Radicle 1.10.0's own release notes describe finding and fixing convergence bugs in that logic.",
          "verdict": "strong-fit",
          "key_facts": [
            "DOCUMENTED: revision = immutable, individually addressable, names its own base. `Revision { id: RevisionId /* = git OID of the COB operation */, base: git::Oid, oid: git::Oid, discussion, reviews, resolves }` \u2014 https://github.com/radicle-dev/heartwood/blob/master/crates/radicle/src/cob/patch.rs (struct Revision ~line 1577)",
            "DOCUMENTED: \"Patch versions, called revisions in Radicle, are immutable. Therefore, each update to a patch creates a new revision, and patch updates are non-destructive, even when you force-push.\" \u2014 https://radicle.dev/guides/user (fetch with a browser User-Agent; radicle.dev 403s bare fetchers)",
            "DOCUMENTED: `base` is the real merge-base, computed server-side by the remote helper: `stored.merge_base(&target, head)?` \u2014 crates/radicle-remote-helper/src/push.rs:508. This is the exact fact that removes your need to seal two worktrees per observation.",
            "DOCUMENTED: the answer to comment-anchoring across revisions is declarative, not heuristic. Comments anchor to `CodeLocation { commit, path, old, new }` (immutable, pinned to a commit OID) and a new revision carries `resolves: BTreeSet<(EntryId, CommentId)>` \u2014 \"Review comments resolved by this revision.\" \u2014 crates/radicle/src/cob/patch.rs and crates/radicle/src/cob/common.rs:470",
            "DOCUMENTED: the HTTP API is read-only. \"For seed nodes, the HTTP Daemon is always configured as a read-only service over the node's state.\" \u2014 https://radicle.dev/guides/seeder",
            "DOCUMENTED: write endpoints were removed from upstream radicle-httpd, and the only machine-to-machine fork is unmaintained. \"This is a temporary fork of the original `radicle-httpd` API that allows us to keep `Create/Update/Delete` that was removed from the original API. We still need these to support: machine-to-machine communication, such as integration with other 3rd party systems (e.g. CI engines...)\" and \"This is **not** part of the Radicle core stack.\" \u2014 https://github.com/cytechmobile/radicle-http-api (last push 2025-03-11)",
            "DOCUMENTED: `rad cob update --type xyz.radicle.patch --object <id> -m <msg> actions.jsonl` applies arbitrary typed COB actions from JSONL \u2014 this is the complete bot write API, including inline `review.comment` with a `location`. `rad cob show --format json` defaults to JSON. \u2014 crates/radicle-cli/src/commands/cob.rs and cob/args.rs",
            "DOCUMENTED: full patch action vocabulary includes `review` {revision, summary, verdict: Accept|Reject, labels}, `review.comment` {review, body, location, reply_to}, `review.comment.resolve`/`unresolve`, `revision` {description, base, oid, resolves} \u2014 crates/radicle/src/cob/patch.rs enum Action (~line 225-378)",
            "DOCUMENTED: `rad node events` streams newline-delimited JSON from the node control socket indefinitely; every RefUpdate carries old+new OIDs, so the previous ref OID is your cursor \u2014 no mirrored state. \u2014 crates/radicle-cli/src/commands/node/events.rs, crates/radicle/src/node/events.rs, crates/radicle/src/storage.rs:239",
            "DOCUMENTED: COBs live at refs/cobs/<reverse-dns-typename>/<object-id> and user-defined types are a supported extension point (\"com.acme.task\"). You can define your own check/verdict COB type rather than waiting for Radicle's \u2014 https://radicle.dev/guides/protocol (Extending COBs)",
            "DOCUMENTED stability caveat: \"Unless clearly stated in the documentation, none of the user-facing commands should be considered stable APIs, and may therefore break in <minor> or <major> version updates.\" \u2014 heartwood VERSIONING.md",
            "DOCUMENTED: 1.10.0 released 2026-08-05 with 143 commits by 16 contributors; Radicle 2.0 development has begun on master with releases/1 as the maintenance branch \u2014 https://radicle.dev/2026/08/05/radicle-1.10.0",
            "DOCUMENTED: no external DB. sqlite is a bundled Rust dependency; storage is git + embedded SQLite. Two static binaries, optional static SPA. \u2014 heartwood Cargo.toml, https://radicle.dev/guides/seeder",
            "DOCUMENTED: CI is not yet a first-class COB. radicle-ci-broker v0.30.0 (2026-07-09, MIT OR Apache-2.0, maintainer Lars Wirzenius) subscribes to the same node event socket you would and writes patch comments; a Checks COB is designed but not shipped \u2014 https://crates.io/crates/radicle-ci-broker, https://blog.liw.fi/posts/2026/radicle-status-quo-01/",
            "INFERRED: exact ref-name shape inside RefsFetched (namespaced vs bare) \u2014 verify empirically before parsing.",
            "INFERRED: whether purely-local COB writes (agent and human on the same node) generate node events at all, or only cross-peer fetches do. RefsFetched is explicitly \"received changes ... from another node\". This matters a lot for a single-node k8s deployment \u2014 test it first."
          ]
        },
        {
          "name": "Tangled (tangled.org) \u2014 ATProto forge with round-based pull requests",
          "license": "MIT (./license in tangled.org/core: \"MIT License, Copyright (c) 2025 Anirudh Oppiliappan, Akshay Oppiliappan and ...\"). Commercial entity behind it: Tangled Labs Oy (Finland).",
          "health": "ACTIVE but self-declared ALPHA everywhere. Every release tag is suffixed `-alpha`: latest is v1.16.1-alpha; v1.16.1-alpha tagged 2026-07-16; 31 tags total. HEAD commit 2026-08-05. Go monorepo (go 1.25.9). The website header literally renders the word \"alpha\" next to the logo. The docs contain a chain of breaking-upgrade notes (\"Upgrading to v1.16.0-alpha\", v1.15, v1.14, v1.13, \"Upgrading from v1.8.x\", \"from v1.7.x\") \u2014 i.e. self-hosters have had to do migration work roughly every release. Maintained primarily by two people (Anirudh Oppiliappan / anirudh.fi, Akshay Oppiliappan / oppi.li) plus community. Real but small adoption; the homepage activity feed is mostly personal dotfiles repos.",
          "revision_noun": "PARTIAL \u2014 the concept is exactly right, the storage is weaker than it sounds. CONCEPT (documented, and it is the closest thing to your requirement outside Radicle): \"Tangled uses a 'round-based' review format. Your initial submission starts 'round 0'... This resubmission starts 'round 1'.\" And the stated rationale is verbatim your problem statement: \"Submissions are immutable: how many times have your reviews gone out-of-date because the author pushed commits during your review? / Reviews are attached to submissions: at a glance, it is easy to tell which comment applies to which 'version' of the pull-request / The author can choose when to resubmit! ... a new round begins when they choose to hit 'resubmit' / It is possible to 'interdiff' and observe changes made across submissions.\" STORAGE REALITY (from lexicons/pulls/pull.json in tangled.org/core): a pull is ONE mutable ATProto record `sh.tangled.repo.pull` containing `rounds: [#round]`, where `round` is only `{ patchBlob: blob(application/gzip), createdAt: datetime }` with the description \"revisions of this pull request, newer rounds are appended to this array. appviews may reject records [that] do not treat this field as append-only. the blob format is gzipped text-based git-format-patches.\" So: (a) a round is NOT its own immutable record \u2014 it is an array element in a record that is rewritten on every resubmit, and append-only-ness is enforced by appview convention, not by the data model; (b) a round does NOT declare its own base commit \u2014 the base is only implicit inside the format-patch bytes (git format-patch can carry a `base-commit:` trailer, but the lexicon neither requires nor records it); (c) what IS immutable and content-addressed is the patchBlob CID. Each round IS individually addressable over HTTP at `/pulls/<n>/round/<r>.patch`. Server-side, the appview keeps `pull_submissions(pull_at, round_number, patch, ...)` with `unique(pull_at, round_number)` and the comment \"content, these are immutable, and require a resubmission to update\" (appview/db/db.go:158-176).",
          "machine_reviewer_api": "WEAKEST PART, and it is a hard blocker for your use case as it stands. There is NO inline code comment and NO verdict object in the protocol. `sh.tangled.repo.pull.comment` (lexicons/pulls/comment.json) has exactly: `pull: at-uri`, `body: string`, `createdAt`, `mentions[]`, `references[]`. That is it \u2014 no round/submission reference, no file, no line, no hunk, no reply-to, no verdict, no resolved flag. I grepped the whole repo: there is no line/hunk anchoring anywhere outside the code-search lexicon. `sh.tangled.repo.pull.status` is pull-level only, with knownValues open/closed/merged \u2014 there is no accept/reject/request-changes. CRUCIALLY, the round association for a comment is NOT declared by the writer: the appview's SQLite has `pull_comments.submission_id` with a FK to pull_submissions (appview/db/db.go:177-198, and the later migration at :1806), and `appview/models/comment.go:91-98` notes \"if it's for PR, PullSubmissionId should not be nil\" \u2014 meaning the appview assigns the submission at ingest time, i.e. it is inferred from whatever round is current when the comment lands. That is the same class of bug you already hit on GitHub, just relocated. READ API: \"Bobbin is an API appview for Tangled records. It serves XRPC endpoints for sh.tangled.* ... It is read-only, there is no auth, since that should all be handled direct-to-PDS and knot respectively.\" and \"Bobbin has no permanent storage.\" WRITE PATH: standard ATProto \u2014 `com.atproto.repo.createRecord` against the actor's PDS with the sh.tangled.* lexicons, plus knot-side XRPC for git ops. That is a clean, well-specified, machine-usable surface with a mature Go SDK ecosystem (bluesky-social/indigo). CI: there is a first-party CI (\"spindles\", lexicons/ci/* and lexicons/pipeline/*) with pipeline/status records, so posting check results is modeled.",
          "push_events": "YES \u2014 and the event vocabulary is the single best-designed thing in this whole survey for your specific self-trigger bug. Documented events: `pull_request:created`, `pull_request:resubmitted` (\"a new round (revision) was pushed to a pull request\"), `pull_request:merged`, `pull_request:closed`, `pull_request:reopened`, plus `push` and repo rename. Note what is NOT there: comments and reviews do not emit webhooks at all, so a machine reviewer physically cannot self-trigger by replying \u2014 the thing that broke you on GitHub is structurally impossible here. Payload carries `round_number` (\"identifies the latest round\") and `patch_url` (\"always points at that round's patch\"), with the patch deliberately not inlined \"(patches can be large); fetch it from patch_url instead.\" HMAC secret for authenticity, documented Go verification example in the docs, automatic retries, 30s delivery timeout. Also `sh.tangled.knot.subscribeRepos` exists as an XRPC subscription (firehose-style), plus the ATProto jetstream. Configured per-repo in Settings -> Hooks.",
          "anchoring": "NOT APPLICABLE \u2014 there is nothing to anchor. Comments are pull-level prose only; the protocol has no file/line/hunk location field at all. The system's documented answer to \"what changed since I reviewed\" is not comment forwarding but INTERDIFF: `patchutil/interdiff.go` and `cmd/interdiff/main.go` implement it, and the stacked-PR feature layers Jujutsu change-ids on top so individual commits can be tracked across rounds (\"Jujutsu ... works around this by assigning stable 'change id's to each change ... If you edit a commit, its SHA changes, but its change-id remains the same. And this is the essence of our new stacked PRs feature!\"). Architecturally that is the right answer \u2014 compare revisions, don't migrate anchors \u2014 but with no inline comments there is no anchor problem to solve yet, which also means no anchor capability for your agent to use.",
          "ops_cost": "MODERATE, and higher than it looks because of ATProto. You self-host a \"knot\" (git server; NixOS, Docker, and manual setup guides exist, plus a \"Secure Mode\") and optionally a \"spindle\" (CI). But writes are ATProto records, so identity and record storage live on a PDS \u2014 you would also want to self-host a PDS to avoid depending on Bluesky infrastructure for your review data, and the appview (the thing that computes rounds and renders PRs) is a separate service on SQLite. Docs cover \"Running the appview\" and \"Running knots and spindles\", plus openbao for secrets. So realistically 3-4 services (PDS, appview, knot, spindle) versus Radicle's one-plus-optional-nginx. Docker compose exists in-tree. Go, single binaries, small footprint each.",
          "go_client": "BEST OF ANY SYSTEM HERE. The entire stack is Go (module tangled.org/core, go 1.25.9), lexicon-generated Go types are checked in at api/tangled/*.go, and ATProto has a mature Go SDK (bluesky-social/indigo). The webhook verification example in the docs is written in Go. If your priority order put Go first this would win outright.",
          "verdict": "partial-fit",
          "key_facts": [
            "DOCUMENTED: the round model is a direct statement of your problem. \"Submissions are immutable... Reviews are attached to submissions... a new round begins when they choose to hit 'resubmit'\" \u2014 https://blog.tangled.org/pulls",
            "DOCUMENTED: webhook events distinguish `pull_request:resubmitted` (\"a new round (revision) was pushed to a pull request\") from created/merged/closed/reopened, and comments/reviews emit NO webhook at all \u2014 so bot self-triggering is structurally impossible. Payload carries `round_number` and a per-round `patch_url`. HMAC secret, retries, Go verification sample. \u2014 https://docs.tangled.org/ (Webhooks section)",
            "DOCUMENTED BLOCKER: `sh.tangled.repo.pull.comment` has only {pull: at-uri, body, createdAt, mentions[], references[]}. No round ref, no file, no line, no reply-to, no verdict, no resolved. \u2014 tangled.org/core lexicons/pulls/comment.json",
            "DOCUMENTED BLOCKER: `sh.tangled.repo.pull.status` knownValues are only open/closed/merged. There is no accept/reject/request-changes verdict object. \u2014 lexicons/pulls/state.json",
            "DOCUMENTED CAVEAT: a round is an array element in a mutable record, not its own immutable record, and append-only-ness is enforced by convention: \"newer rounds are appended to this array. appviews may reject records [that] do not treat this field as append-only.\" A round records only {patchBlob, createdAt} \u2014 NO base commit. \u2014 lexicons/pulls/pull.json",
            "DOCUMENTED CAVEAT: comment-to-round binding is inferred server-side at ingest (appview SQLite `pull_comments.submission_id` FK, models/comment.go:91-98), not declared by the writer \u2014 the same failure mode you already hit.",
            "DOCUMENTED: read API Bobbin is read-only and stateless (\"It is read-only, there is no auth, since that should all be handled direct-to-PDS and knot respectively\", \"Bobbin has no permanent storage\"). Writes go direct to a PDS via com.atproto.repo.createRecord. \u2014 https://docs.tangled.org/ (Bobbin section)",
            "DOCUMENTED: interdiff is implemented (patchutil/interdiff.go, cmd/interdiff/main.go) and stacked PRs use Jujutsu change-ids to track individual commits across rounds \u2014 https://blog.tangled.org/stacking",
            "DOCUMENTED: alpha. Latest tag v1.16.1-alpha (2026-07-16), HEAD 2026-08-05, 31 tags, every one -alpha, with breaking upgrade notes for v1.13/1.14/1.15/1.16 and from v1.7.x/v1.8.x. MIT. Two primary maintainers.",
            "INFERRED: adding inline comments + a verdict object would mean designing new lexicons and patching the Go appview. That is real upstream work, but it is Go, MIT, and the round substrate you'd be building on already exists \u2014 this is the most plausible \"fork/extend\" candidate if you decide not to adopt Radicle."
          ]
        },
        {
          "name": "SourceHut \u2014 lists.sr.ht patchsets + builds.sr.ht",
          "license": "AGPL (services) and BSD for some components; sourcehut.org footer: \"Source Code (AGPL, BSD et al)\". The GraphQL schema files themselves are CC-0/public domain.",
          "health": "HEALTHY-ISH but thinly staffed and distracted. Two paid people (Drew DeVault, Conrad Hoffmann) plus volunteers. Q2 2026 update (2026-05-28) is candid: Drew's quarter was \"mostly 'invisible' labor\" \u2014 an EU grant proposal, finances, \"mitigating rolling DDoS attacks\", and a wave of 300+ spam signups in a month. Actual API work in 2026 is led by an unpaid volunteer (Simon Martin), who \"added some improvements for lists.sr.ht's patch review view, associating new patchset revisions with their previous versions and adding a UI for navigating between different versions of a patch.\" The docs wiki was last committed 2026-08-01, so it is live. sr.ht has been in \"public alpha\" for its entire existence. Cultural note, relevant to you but NOT a technical blocker for a self-hosted instance: sourcehut.org advertises \"No AI features whatsoever\" as a headline principle, and the project deployed Anubis against LLM crawlers. I found no evidence of a ban on AI-generated contributions (that is Codeberg, not sr.ht), so do not repeat that claim \u2014 but upstream is not going to build features for you.",
          "revision_noun": "YES for versions, NO for immutability-with-base. `type Patchset { id: Int!, created, updated, subject: String!, version: Int!, prefix, status: PatchsetStatus!, submitter, coverLetter, thread, supersededBy: Patchset, supersedes: Patchset, list, patches(cursor): EmailCursor!, tools: [PatchsetTool!]!, mbox: URL! }`. So a patchset IS a first-class server object with a stable integer id, an explicit `version: Int!`, and an explicit v1<->v2<->v3 chain via `supersedes`/`supersededBy`. `type Patch { index, count, version, prefix, subject, trailers: [Trailer!]! }` \u2014 these are parsed out of the `[PATCH v2 3/4]` subject line, i.e. version identity ultimately derives from a human-typed subject prefix, not from a content-addressed identifier. THE FATAL GAP FOR YOU: a Patchset records NO base commit. There is no `base`, no merge-base, no target ref on the object at all \u2014 the patch bytes are just an mbox. Reconstructing \"what was this applied to\" means parsing `base-commit:` trailers out of the emails, if the sender bothered to emit them. This is the exact deficiency you are trying to escape.",
          "machine_reviewer_api": "NOT VIABLE for a machine reviewer. I enumerated every mutation in the lists.sr.ht GraphQL schema. The complete list is: createMailingList, updateMailingList, deleteMailingList, updateUserACL, updateSenderACL, updateMailingListACL, deleteACL, updatePatchset(id, status), createTool(patchsetID, details, icon), updateTool(id, details, icon), mailingListSubscribe/Unsubscribe, importMailingListSpool, createUserWebhook, deleteUserWebhook, createMailingListWebhook, deleteMailingListWebhook, updatePreferences \u2014 plus several @internal ones (triggerUserEmailWebhooks, triggerListEmailWebhooks, archiveMessage, requestSubscription/confirmSubscription/...). THERE IS NO MUTATION TO POST A COMMENT, A REPLY, OR A REVIEW. The only non-@internal way for a bot to say anything is to send SMTP to ~user/list@lists.sr.ht and hope the threading headers land right. `archiveMessage(listID, message: Upload!)` exists but is marked @internal. So: verdicts YES but only as a 7-state enum on the whole patchset (updatePatchset -> PROPOSED | NEEDS_REVISION | SUPERSEDED | APPROVED | REJECTED | APPLIED, also settable by an `X-Sourcehut-Patchset-Update` email header); CI checks YES and cleanly (`createTool`/`updateTool` with ToolIcon PENDING|WAITING|SUCCESS|FAILED|CANCELLED and a `details` string \u2014 the type's own doc says \"Used to add some kind of indicator for a third-party process associated with a patchset, such as a CI service validating the change\"); inline comments NO; thread replies via API NO; thread resolution NO (the concept does not exist). Auth is OAuth 2.0 / personal access tokens with per-service scopes (PATCHES RO/RW etc.), documented complexity limits (200 default), 3s query timeout, opaque cursors, semver `version` resolver with a `deprecationDate` field.",
          "push_events": "YES, and the design is elegant. GraphQL-native webhooks: `enum WebhookEvent { LIST_CREATED, LIST_UPDATED, LIST_DELETED, EMAIL_RECEIVED, PATCHSET_RECEIVED }` with `type PatchsetEvent implements WebhookPayload { uuid, event, date, patchset: Patchset! }`. You register a GraphQL QUERY along with the webhook; the server executes your query in a webhook context and POSTs the resulting JSON \u2014 so you shape the payload yourself and can pull in related data without a second round trip. Authenticity via `X-Payload-Signature` (base64 ed25519 over body||nonce) and `X-Payload-Nonce`, with the instance public key at `/query/api-meta.json`. Webhooks run read-only (\"Webhook queries cannot execute mutations\"), inherit the registering credential, and are auto-disabled when it expires. Delivery records with response status/body/headers are queryable and kept \"at least 90 days\". PATCHSET_RECEIVED is genuinely the right granularity for you. Docs warn: \"Webhook support is fairly new and is still under development for many of our APIs.\"",
          "anchoring": "NONE. There is no inline comment primitive at all, therefore no anchor and no documented forwarding answer. Review is prose email replies quoting diff context, correlated by human eyeballs. `supersedes`/`supersededBy` gives you \"v2 replaced v1\" but nothing at comment granularity. `mbox: URL!` per patchset means you can fetch the raw bytes and do your own range-diff externally, which is what you would have to do.",
          "ops_cost": "HIGHEST of everything surveyed, and disproportionate for a small team. Self-hosting sr.ht means standing up meta.sr.ht (identity/OAuth) plus lists.sr.ht plus builds.sr.ht plus git.sr.ht if you want hosting \u2014 each is a separate Python/Go service with its own PostgreSQL database, Redis, and Celery workers, and lists.sr.ht additionally requires a working inbound and outbound MTA with correct DKIM/SPF/DMARC and a list-processing pipeline. You are taking on operating email as infrastructure. For one k8s cluster and a small team this is a poor trade for what you get back. Note also that Conrad is mid-migration replacing the builds.sr.ht Python shell with Go, so the stack is in flux.",
          "go_client": "PARTIAL, and better than most. `hut` (git.sr.ht/~emersion/hut) is the official CLI, written in Go, and it drives lists.sr.ht patchsets over GraphQL \u2014 including `hut lists patchset apply`. It is a usable reference implementation and a source of Go types. But there is no generated Go SDK; you would run gqlgen/genqlient against the published schema. The schemas themselves are public domain and fetchable raw (e.g. https://git.sr.ht/~sircmpwn/lists.sr.ht/blob/master/api/graph/schema.graphqls?raw=1), and each service exposes a GraphQL playground, so codegen is straightforward.",
          "verdict": "poor-fit",
          "key_facts": [
            "DOCUMENTED: a patchset IS a first-class versioned object. `type Patchset { id: Int!, version: Int!, status: PatchsetStatus!, supersededBy: Patchset, supersedes: Patchset, patches, tools, mbox: URL! }` \u2014 https://git.sr.ht/~sircmpwn/lists.sr.ht/blob/master/api/graph/schema.graphqls (line ~499)",
            "DOCUMENTED BLOCKER: I enumerated every mutation in the lists.sr.ht schema. There is no comment, reply, or review mutation. The only bot-writable things are updatePatchset(status), createTool, updateTool, and webhook/ACL/list management. Posting review text requires SMTP. `archiveMessage` is @internal.",
            "DOCUMENTED: CI check attachment is clean and purpose-built. `createTool(patchsetID: Int!, details: String!, icon: ToolIcon!)` / `updateTool(id, details, icon)` with ToolIcon PENDING|WAITING|SUCCESS|FAILED|CANCELLED; the type doc says \"Used to add some kind of indicator for a third-party process associated with a patchset, such as a CI service validating the change.\"",
            "DOCUMENTED: verdicts exist but only patchset-wide. `enum PatchsetStatus { UNKNOWN, PROPOSED, NEEDS_REVISION, SUPERSEDED, APPROVED, REJECTED, APPLIED }`, settable via `updatePatchset` or the `X-Sourcehut-Patchset-Update` email header \u2014 https://man.sr.ht/lists.sr.ht/",
            "DOCUMENTED: GraphQL-native webhooks with PATCHSET_RECEIVED and a `PatchsetEvent { patchset: Patchset! }` payload; you supply the GraphQL query that shapes the payload; ed25519 signature via X-Payload-Signature over body||nonce, instance key at /query/api-meta.json; deliveries retained >=90 days \u2014 https://man.sr.ht/graphql.md",
            "DOCUMENTED: version identity comes from parsing the subject line. `type Patch { index, count, version, prefix, subject, trailers }` are described as parsed from `[PATCH myproject v2 3/4] Add foo to bar`.",
            "DOCUMENTED GAP: a Patchset carries no base commit and no target ref. Base recovery means parsing base-commit trailers out of the mbox, if present.",
            "DOCUMENTED: `hut` is the official Go CLI and does drive patchsets over GraphQL \u2014 https://git.sr.ht/~emersion/hut",

            "INFERRED: how `supersedes`/`supersededBy` is actually computed (subject-line matching vs In-Reply-To vs manual) is not documented; the Q2 2026 note says Simon Martin added \"associating new patchset revisions with their previous versions\", implying it is derived heuristically.           "name": "Linux kernel email workflow \u2014 b4 + Change-Id trailers + public-inbox",
          "license": "b4: GPL-2.0-or-later (PyPI metadata carries GPLv2 text). public-inbox: AGPL-3.0. git range-diff: GPL-2.0.",
          "health": "b4 is HEALTHY and very actively maintained: v0.16.0 released 2026-08-05 (one day before this research), after v0.15.2 (2026-04-09), v0.15.1 (2026-03-24), v0.15.0 (2026-03-20), v0.14.3 (2025-10-01). Maintained by Konstantin Ryabitsev at the Linux Foundation; canonical repo https://git.kernel.org/pub/scm/utils/b4/b4.git. Note the gap: v0.14.2 Sep 2024 -> v0.14.3 Oct 2025 was a 12-month quiet period, then a burst in 2026. public-inbox is maintained by Eric Wong, stable and boring, but its own TODO still lists \"HTTP(S) search API\" as unimplemented (\"possibilities include JMAP or GraphQL\").",
          "revision_noun": "PARTIAL \u2014 stable change identity YES, addressable immutable revision object NO. The identity mechanism is genuinely good and is the intellectual ancestor of Gerrit's Change-Id and jj's change ID: `b4 prep` generates and tracks a `change-id` trailer (format like `20230103-fstests-setgid-v6-2-4ce5852d11e2`) that \"will follow through all versions of the patch\", and b4 \"automatically discovers older and newer revisions of a tracked series using change-id and in-reply-to chains\". Series versioning is real and automatic: b4 \"rerolls\" to the next version after a successful `b4 send`, with `--force-revision N` to override, and tracking data is stored (default `commit` strategy) in an empty commit at the start of the series \u2014 alternatives are the branch description in .git/config or the series tip. Base is explicit: `base-commit:` trailers and `git format-patch --base`; b4 requires you to \"specify the `base-commit` to use for your combined series\" when there are dependencies, and you can reference a dependency by its change-id plus version. BUT: there is no revision OBJECT anywhere. A \"revision\" is a set of RFC822 messages sharing a subject prefix and a change-id trailer, scattered across an archive keyed by Message-Id. Nothing enforces immutability, nothing enforces that v2 declares its relationship to v1, and identity degrades to string matching the moment a sender omits the trailer.",
          "machine_reviewer_api": "NOT VIABLE as a review backend \u2014 it is fundamentally human-email-shaped, and that is by design. There is no server to POST to. A machine reviewer would have to (a) hold an SMTP identity and send correctly-threaded MIME with In-Reply-To/References, (b) express verdicts as trailers (Reviewed-by:, Acked-by:, Tested-by:, Nacked-by:) which are conventions parsed by tooling, not a typed verdict field, (c) express inline comments as interleaved unquoted lines inside a quoted diff \u2014 a format with no schema. There is no thread-resolution concept at all. There is no check/CI object; kernel CI (e.g. patchwork) is a separate mirror system with its own database \u2014 exactly the 45-column mirror you are trying to delete. The closest thing to a machine surface is b4's own local state: `b4 review` (introduced v0.15, enhanced v0.16) keeps \"a lightweight SQLite tracking database\" plus \"a per-series review branch in git\" storing \"comments, trailers, and review state between sessions\", with `b4 review show-info` emitting plain-text or JSON for scripting and `B4_TRACKING_FILE` for CI, and `b4 review cron` running headless. But the docs are explicit that this is \"a technology preview (alpha)\" and that \"Commands, keybindings, configuration options, and on-disk formats may change in incompatible ways between releases.\" It is one maintainer's local workbench, not a shared server.",
          "push_events": "NONE in any useful sense. public-inbox exposes NNTP, IMAP, POP3, Atom feeds, and HTML \u2014 plus the archive itself as a clonable git repository, which is the one genuinely nice property (you can `git fetch` the archive and diff refs to find new messages, so the git ref IS a cursor). But there is no webhook, no event stream, and no JSON API; public-inbox's own TODO still lists an HTTP search API as unimplemented. Practically, you would poll IMAP IDLE or a git remote. That fails your requirement 3.",
          "anchoring": "NONE, and the documented answer is honest about its own limits. There is no anchor object. What exists is interdiff: `b4 diff` shells out to `git range-diff` to compare two versions of a series, and `b4 shazam` applies them (v0.16 overhauled its conflict resolution with inline resolution). The documented limitation is severe: `b4 diff -v` \"requires that both versions being compared are part of the same thread, which is rarely the case\", and \"both revisions need to cleanly apply to the current tree, which may not always be easy to achieve.\" The docs' own stated hope is that \"this may work better as more series use the `change-id` trailer to keep track of revisions across discussion threads\" \u2014 i.e. the change-id mechanism exists precisely because thread-based correlation does not work.",
          "ops_cost": "public-inbox itself is cheap: Perl, git-backed, Xapian for search, no SQL server, well-packaged in Debian.           "go_client": "NONE. b4 is Python; public-inbox is Perl. There is no Go library for either. The only Go-shaped pieces are generic: an IMAP/SMTP client (emersion/go-imap, go-message) and `git range-diff` via exec. You would be writing an email client, not integrating with an API.",

            "DOCUMENTED: series versioning is automatic (auto-reroll after `b4 send`, `--force-revision N`), and tracking data is stored in an empty commit at the start of the series (default `commit` strategy) or in the branch description \u2014 same URL",
            "DOCUMENTED: base is explicit via `base-commit:` trailers / `git format-patch --base`, and dependencies are referenced by change-id + version \u2014 same URL",
            "DOCUMENTED LIMITATION on interdiff: `b4 diff -v` \"requires that both versions being compared are part of the same thread, which is rarely the case\" and both revisions \"need to cleanly apply to the current tree, which may not always be easy to achieve\" \u2014 https://b4.docs.kernel.org/en/latest/maintainer/diff.html",
            "DOCUMENTED: `b4 review` is alpha. \"technology preview (alpha)\"; \"Commands, keybindings, configuration options, and on-disk formats may change in incompatible ways between releases.\" State is a local SQLite tracking DB plus a per-series review branch in git; `b4 review show-info` emits JSON; `b4 review cron` runs headless \u2014 https://b4.docs.kernel.org/en/latest/maintainer/review.html",
            "DOCUMENTED: b4 v0.16.0 released 2026-08-05, v0.15.0 2026-03-20, prior release v0.14.3 2025-10-01 after a 12-month gap. GPLv2. Maintainer Konstantin Ryabitsev / Linux Foundation \u2014 https://pypi.org/pypi/b4/json, https://git.kernel.org/pub/scm/utils/b4/b4.git/",
            "DOCUMENTED: public-inbox has no JSON/HTTP API.             "INFERRED: the one idea worth stealing is the change-id trailer as a commit-message-level stable identity that survives rebase and force-push, readable with plain `git interpret-trailers`. That is ~20 lines of Go and needs none of the rest of this stack.",
            "INFERRED: the clonable-git-archive property (fetch the archive repo, diff refs to find new messages) is the same cursor-free pattern Radicle gives you for COBs \u2014 but without a typed review object it buys you very little."

          "health": "VERY HEALTHY. v0.44.0 released 2026-08-06 (the day of this research); repo jj-vcs/jj pushed 2026-08-07, 30,854 stars, not archived. jj-lib on crates.io at 0.44.0, updated 2026-08-06, 254k downloads.           "revision_noun": "PARTIAL, and importantly NOT what you asked for. jj gives you stable CHANGE identity, not an ordered series of addressable revisions of a change. Glossary, quoted: \"A change ID is a unique identifier for a change. They are typically 16 bytes long and are often randomly generated.\" \"A change is a commit as it evolves over time. Changes themselves don't exist as an object in the data model; only the change ID does.\" So a change ID identifies the LATEST version of a change; the history of prior versions is the evolog (predecessors), which is jj-local metadata, not a published series. There is no base-naming, no review attachment point, no per-revision object. jj is a VCS, not a review system \u2014 there is no review object of any kind. THE PART THAT IS DIRECTLY USEFUL TO YOU: since v0.30.0 jj writes the change ID into the git commit object itself as a non-standard `change-id` header (32-character reverse-hex), a format standardized jointly with GitButler. That means stable change identity is expressible in plain git, readable by any tool, with no jj dependency at all. Documented caveats: \"This is a non-standard header and is not preserved by all `git` tooling\" \u2014 `git commit --amend` preserves it, rebase strips it, GitHub and other forges generally preserve it; `git show`/`git log` do not display it, so you must use `git cat-file -p <ref>`. Configurable via `git.write-change-id-header`. Historically (and still, for commits jj did not create) change IDs live in a StackedTable at .jj/repo/store/extra/ along with predecessors, and for commits created by plain git jj derives the change ID as the bit-reversed commit ID.",
          "machine_reviewer_api": "NONE \u2014 jj has no review concept, no comments, no verdicts, no threads, no checks. Not applicable. (Tangled and Gerrit both consume jj change IDs from the outside; jj itself is agnostic.)",
          "push_events": "NONE. There is no server. jj has an operation log (\"the DAG formed by operation objects\") which is a local undo/audit structure, not a publish/subscribe mechanism.",
          "anchoring": "NOT APPLICABLE \u2014 no comments exist.           "ops_cost": "ZERO as a server (there isn't one). As a mandated developer tool it is nonzero: jj is pre-1.0 with a distinct mental model, and forcing it on humans is a real adoption cost.           "go_client": "NONE for jj-lib \u2014 it is a Rust crate (jj-lib 0.44.0) and there is no Go binding, no FFI, and no stable C ABI.             "DOCUMENTED and this is the single most reusable finding in this whole report: since v0.30.0 jj writes the change ID as a `change-id` git commit header (32-char reverse-hex), a format standardized with GitButler. \"The header is written by default in commits created by `jj` (since version `0.30.0`) and this functionality can be turned off using the `git.write-change-id-header` setting.\" \u2014 https://docs.jj-vcs.dev/latest/git-compatibility/",

          "revision_noun": "PARTIAL. So base IS declared and post-rebase identity IS handled \u2014 but via a mutable `reviewRef` plus an `alias` fixup, and \"if there are multiple requests for a single commit, then they are sorted by timestamp and the final request is treated as the current one.\" Last-write-wins by timestamp is not an immutable ordered series.           "machine_reviewer_api": "NO SERVER \u2014 the API is git itself, which is arguably the point. CI results (refs/notes/devtools/ci) have `{ agent, status: success|failure, url, timestamp }` attached to \"the revision that was built and tested\". So: threading YES, resolution YES, inline anchoring YES with column precision, CI checks YES \u2014 all as a plain JSON-in-git-notes format with published JSON Schemas, all version-tagged by a `v` field.       ],
      "notes": "HEADLINE: exactly one system in this space actually models what you described, and it is Radicle. Its Revision struct literally is your requirement \u2014 `Revision { id: <content-addressed COB entry OID>, base: git::Oid, oid: git::Oid, discussion: Thread<Comment<CodeLocation>>, reviews, resolves: BTreeSet<(EntryId, CommentId)> }` \u2014 immutable, individually addressable, naming its own merge-base, with comments pinned to a commit OID and a declarative statement of which comments a new revision resolves. Nothing else here has all four properties. Tangled has the right IDEA (rounds) with a thin implementation. SourceHut has versions but no way for a bot to speak. b4 has identity but no server. jj has identity but no review. ReviewStack has neither.\n\nTHE THREE THINGS THAT WILL DECIDE THIS, all of which cut against Radicle:\n1. Radicle's HTTP API is read-only, by policy, and the community fork that restored Create/Update/Delete \"to support machine-to-machine communication\" is unmaintained and disclaims being part of the core stack. Your bot must therefore run co-located with a radicle-node and a signing key and shell out to `rad`. Whether that is acceptable in your k8s topology is the actual decision, not the data model.\n2. There is no Go client for Radicle and there will not be one. Your integration is `rad cob show/log --format json` for reads and `rad cob update ... actions.jsonl` for writes, with hand-written Go structs mirroring the serde-tagged Action enum. That is honest, workable, and about a day of typing \u2014 but it is subprocess-shaped, and heartwood's own VERSIONING.md warns \"none of the user-facing commands should be considered stable APIs, and may therefore break in <minor> or <major> version updates.\"\n3. Radicle 2.0 development started with 1.10.0 (2026-08-05): master is now Radicle 2, releases/1 is maintenance. Pin to the 1.x line and budget for a migration.\n\nWHAT DOES NOT EXIST ANYWHERE, so stop looking for it: a self-hostable open-source review system with (a) immutable base-naming revisions, (b) a typed REST/gRPC bot API for verdicts and inline threads, (c) push events, AND (d) a Go client. Radicle has a,c and half of b. Tangled has b,c,d and half of a. Nothing has all four. If you want all four you are either wrapping Radicle behind a Go service you own, or extending Tangled (MIT, Go, two lexicons short of what you need).\n\nTWO PRIMITIVES WORTH STEALING EVEN IF YOU ADOPT NOTHING:\n- The `change-id` git commit header (jj v0.30.0+, 32-char reverse-hex, standardized with GitButler, config `git.write-change-id-header`). Stable change identity in plain git, readable with `git cat-file -p`, survives `--amend`, stripped by rebase, generally preserved by forges. ~30 lines of Go, zero dependencies, no VCS migration. This directly attacks the \"no stable identity across force-push\" half of your problem without adopting anything.\n- Radicle's `resolves: BTreeSet<(EntryId, CommentId)>` on a revision. The principled answer to comment anchoring across revisions is not to forward anchors \u2014 it is to pin each comment to an immutable commit OID and have the NEXT revision declare which comments it resolves. That converts an unsolvable heuristic into a data field. You can adopt this design in your own schema today regardless of which backend you pick.\n\nCORRECTIONS TO LIKELY PRIORS:\n- ReviewStack is NOT dead-dead (site up, docs up, MIT license files added April 2026) but it is NOT maintained either \u2014 since Feb 2026 it has received only dependency bumps and two cosmetic fixes, while its sibling addons/isl commits daily. More importantly it adds NO revision noun: versions are reconstructed in the browser from GitHub's timeline, with an explicit rebase-correctness TODO in diffVersions.ts. Adopting it would not change your machine-reviewer situation at all.\n- Radicle Heartwood is Rust, as expected \u2014 but the surprise is that the CLI exposes a fully general typed-JSON write path (`rad cob create/update` with JSONL actions) that is strictly more capable than the friendly `rad patch` subcommands. In particular, `rad patch comment` has no `--location` flag and the only interactive path to inline comments is a review builder the argument parser itself labels \"This operation is obsolete\" \u2014 so a bot posting inline comments MUST use the JSONL path. This is easy to miss and would have looked like a missing capability.\n- SourceHut's lists.sr.ht is often described as having a patchset API. It has a patchset READ api and a status/CI-tool WRITE api, but I enumerated every mutation in the schema and there is no comment, reply, or review mutation at all. Review text can only be posted by SMTP. Treat any claim that a bot can \"review on sr.ht via GraphQL\" as false.\n- Do not repeat a claim that SourceHut bans AI-generated contributions \u2014 that is Codeberg. What is documented is a marketing stance (\"No AI features whatsoever\") and anti-crawler measures. Irrelevant to a self-hosted AGPL instance, but relevant to whether upstream would accept features you need.\n\nRELATED, NOT WORTH A FULL ENTRY: git-bug (github.com/git-bug/git-bug) \u2014 Go, GPL-3.0, 9,967 stars, pushed 2026-07-06, stores issues as distributed git objects with a CRDT-ish model very similar to Radicle COBs, and b4 v0.16 added a `b4 bugs` tech preview integrating it. It is issues, not code review, so it does not solve your problem \u2014 but if you end up designing your own git-native object model in Go, its entity/operation packages are the best Go reference implementation available and are actively maintained, unlike git-appraise.\n\nLOCAL ARTIFACTS from this research, if you want to inspect the sources directly (absolute paths, scratch only, safe to delete):\n- /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-tdmtrader-concourse-concourse--claude-worktrees-modest-noether-2975f7/1d03746f-d11d-456b-818a-0c15f2eced19/scratchpad/heartwood \u2014 shallow clone of radicle-dev/heartwood at 1.10.0. Key files: crates/radicle/src/cob/patch.rs (Revision struct ~1577, enum Action ~225), crates/radicle/src/cob/common.rs:470 (CodeLocation/CodeRange), crates/radicle-cli/src/commands/cob.rs (JSONL action path), crates/radicle-cli/src/commands/cob/args.rs (create/update/log/show CLI), crates/radicle/src/node/events.rs (event enum), crates/radicle/src/storage.rs:239 (RefUpdate), rad-patch.1.adoc, VERSIONING.md.\n- /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-tdmtrader-concourse-concourse--claude-worktrees-modest-noether-2975f7/1d03746f-d11d-456b-818a-0c15f2eced19/scratchpad/tangled-core \u2014 shallow clone of tangled.org/core. Key files: lexicons/pulls/pull.json (round model), lexicons/pulls/comment.json (the gap), lexicons/pulls/state.json, appview/db/db.go:158-198 (pull_submissions / pull_comments), patchutil/interdiff.go.\n- /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-tdmtrader-concourse-concourse--claude-worktrees-modest-noether-2975f7/1d03746f-d11d-456b-818a-0c15f2eced19/scratchpad/lists_schema.graphqls \u2014 the full lists.sr.ht GraphQL schema (public domain / CC-0), useful for gqlgen codegen if SourceHut is revisited.\n\nOPEN QUESTIONS I COULD NOT SETTLE AND WOULD VERIFY EMPIRICALLY BEFORE COMMITTING TO RADICLE:\n1. Do purely LOCAL COB writes generate node events? The RefsFetched doc comment says \"received changes to Git references ... from another node\" \u2014 in a single-node k8s deployment where your agent and your human both operate the same node, you may get no events at all and have to watch refs directly or use `rad inbox`. This is the single highest-risk unknown for requirement 3.\n2. The exact ref-name shape inside RefUpdate (namespaced refs/namespaces/<nid>/refs/cobs/... vs bare refs/cobs/...). Action::Revision inserts under a fresh key with debug_assert!(!self.revisions.contains_key(&entry)) (patch.rs:1043-1060). Action::RevisionEdit only pushes onto description: NonEmpty<Edit> (patch.rs:1024-1042) \u2014 base and oid are never writable after creation. This is the real answer to the caller's problem and it is genuinely better than a GitHub head SHA.\n- CodeLocation { commit: Oid, path, old: Option<CodeRange>, new: Option<CodeRange> } at crates/radicle/src/cob/common.rs:470; CodeRange is Lines{range} | Chars{line,range}. Three separate problems: (a) rad cob has NO announce path at all \u2014 grep for \"announce\" across crates/radicle-cli/src/commands/cob* returns nothing, while rad patch announces by default via --announce/--no-announce (patch/args.rs:32-37, patch.rs:317-319). So `rad cob update` writes locally and the node emits nothing; you must chase it with `rad sync --announce`. (b) The local-write event that does exist, Event::LocalRefsAnnounced (crates/radicle/src/node/events.rs:79; emitted only from announce_own_refs in crates/radicle-protocol/src/service.rs:2132), carries refs: RefsAt { remote, at } \u2014 the sigrefs commit OID, NOT a Vec<RefUpdate>. Otherwise, we just ignore this operation\" (patch.rs:1100-1122). reviews is BTreeMap<ActorId, Review>. A CI bot that posts a second verdict on the same revision gets a silent no-op \u2014 it must use review.edit.v2 instead. The crate was deleted from heartwood on 2024-06-06 (commit a4989b7c, message: \"The HTTP daemon is moved to the `radicle-explorer` repository\"). crates.io froze at 0.10.0 because publishing moved, not because development stopped \u2014 it is at 0.27.0 in radicle-explorer as of 2026-07-29. This changes the health read (the daemon is actively maintained) without changing the functional read (still read-only).\n\n7. LICENSE IS SPLIT AND THE REPORT ONLY COVERS HALF. heartwood is dual MIT/Apache-2.0 (both LICENSE files present) \u2014 confirmed.
